- **A dropped image file** arrives as a path; the shell reads it only if it came from that very drop, is png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp and is at most 10 MB. Anything else gets the same short note as a paste ("Only images can be attached.").

The diagnostics are what a maintainer would otherwise have to ask for: version, platform, which screen, how many events, whether hooks and orchestration are on. Deliberately absent are project names, file paths and cost — those are the user's repositories and the user's money, and neither helps fix a crooked button. The data directory is left out for the same reason: it contains their username.

There is no rewriting of what the user typed. Turning "button is crooked" into prose needs a model, and Caprock makes no API calls; adding one would mean asking for a key or posting their words to a server. Structure does the same job — a kind, their title and words verbatim, diagnostics underneath.

Long descriptions are clipped at 6,000 characters with a visible marker, because GitHub silently truncates a prefilled issue past roughly 8k and losing the end of someone's report without telling them is worse than asking them to trim it.


## Report a bug

`components/BugReportSheet.tsx`, `lib/support.ts` ([ADR-073](08-decisions.md)). The owner, 2026-10-17 (translated): a bug report the user can type or dictate with the phone keyboard, attach a few screenshots, and send to Caprock support — not a GitHub issue, because it may contain personal data. It goes to Caprock support at caprock.dev through `POST /v1/support` ([03-contracts.md § Report a bug](03-contracts.md)), only on the button.

**Where.** On a computer, ⌘K *Report a bug…* opens it as a centred dialog, 480px wide. On a narrow screen it is a sheet that rises from the bottom (a grab handle, rounded top, the safe area kept). The phone's entry, the chat bar's **+** menu, went with that bar (owner, 2026-10-21: removed, unused and in the way). No shortcut of its own (rule 12). *Send feedback* (above) is unchanged.

**Top to bottom**, the copy exact:

- **Report a bug** — the title.
- **What happened?** — a textarea, focused on open so the keyboard and its mic are up, 16px so iOS does not zoom; under it *Type, or tap the mic on your keyboard to dictate*. At most 8,000 characters.
- **+ Add screenshot** — a file picker (`accept="image/*"`, several at once), up to 3; each becomes a 56×72 thumbnail with a × to remove it, and the button goes once there are three. Each is drawn at most 1600 px on the long side and re-encoded as JPEG at 0.85 before it is kept (`prepareShot`); a picture the browser cannot decode goes as it is when it is a PNG, JPEG or WebP of at most 4 MB, and anything else is refused in a line. More than three picked: the first that fit are taken and the line says how many were left out.
- **Email for a reply (optional)** — `type="email"`, the phone's email keyboard, *you@example.com* as its placeholder.
- **The privacy line, before the button**, word for word: **Sent to Caprock support (caprock.dev), not GitHub:** your text, the screenshots you attached, your email if given, Caprock version and OS. Nothing else — no transcripts, no logs.
- **Cancel** and **Send to support**, side by side, Send the wider one.

**Sending.** Empty text sends nothing and says *Say what happened first.* in a line above the buttons, the caret back in the box; a text over the limit, a screenshot over 4 MB, more than 8 MB together or an email that is not an address say so the same way. While a report is on its way both buttons are disabled and a backdrop click or Escape does not close the sheet. Sent: the form is cleared, the sheet closes, and a toast says *Sent. Thank you — we reply by email if you left one.* for six seconds. Not sent: the form stays as it was, and the line says *Not sent: <the daemon's error>. Your report is kept — try again.* Nothing is retried on its own. Cancel, Escape and the backdrop close the sheet and keep what was typed until it is sent or the page reloads.

## Session Detail

- Live terminal (xterm.js) + input bar to type back (the live terminal shipped in v0.2.0 for sessions Caprock spawns; the timeline + diff tabs shipped in v0.1.0).
- **Links in every terminal** (this one and the app's tabs, `ui/src/lib/termlinks.ts`):
  plain `http(s)` URLs (`@xterm/addon-web-links`) and OSC 8 hyperlinks, which
  Claude Code prints (xterm's `linkHandler`), open on **Cmd+click** on macOS
  and **Ctrl+click** elsewhere. A plain click does nothing, so a drag still
  selects. Hover underlines the link and shows the hint below (with the
  address when an OSC 8 label hides it). Only `http`, `https` and `mailto`
  open; `file:`, `javascript:` and custom schemes are ignored, because the
  text came from whatever ran in the terminal. The app opens the link in the
  default browser through its `open_external` command, a browser in a new tab
  (`noopener`). **⇧⌘-click** (Ctrl+Shift+click elsewhere; owner, 2026-10-17,
  translated: "Cmd+click is the default … I want another chord, and there I
  see and choose which browser to open it in") on an `http(s)` link opens a
  small chooser at the pointer (`components/LinkChooser.tsx`, loaded on first
  use) with two choices (owner, 2026-10-17, translated: "I just want to
  choose either the default browser or the built-in one"): the link faint at
  its top, then **Default browser**, the default's name as its hint
  (*Google Chrome*), focused, which opens as ⌘-click does; **Caprock
  browser**, a browser tab (ADR-075), shown wherever one can exist — the
  desktop app, never a phone or the plain web — whether the in-app browser
  is on or off: picked while off it turns it on, the pick being the consent,
  and the toast says *In-app browser turned on · Settings → In-app browser
  to turn it off* (`pickCaprock`, `lib/browsertab.ts`); then a rule and
  **Copy link**. Last, quiet, **Other browser…** expands in place into the
  other web browsers installed here (`GET /v1/browsers`, which lists real
  browsers only: no chat app, terminal, editor or bank's "secure browser"
  wrapper that merely handles https), focusing the first; one picked goes
  to `POST /v1/browsers/open`, and a failure says *Could not open it in X:
  …* inside the chooser, which stays open. With no other browser (a paired
  phone, an older daemon, only the default) the row is not there. ↑ ↓ Home
  End move, Enter picks, Esc, Tab, a click outside or a resize close it. A
  `mailto` link has no chooser: the chord opens it as ⌘-click does. The
  rows are `ChooserEntry`s (`lib/linkchooser.ts`). The hover hint names both
  clicks through `keys()`: *⌘-click to open · ⇧⌘-click to choose where it
  opens* (*Ctrl+click to open · Ctrl+Shift+click to choose where it opens*
  off macOS); a `mailto` link's names only the first.
  Both clicks are rows of the shortcuts list (`LINK_OPEN_CLICK`,
  `LINK_CHOOSE_CLICK`, `lib/appkeys.ts`), and the *every listed shortcut, on
  every OS* tests press each as macOS, Windows and Linux spell it.
- **Live diff tab** — `git diff` of the agent's worktree, auto-refreshed on Edit/Write events.
  Every file expands on its own (`components/DiffFiles.tsx`). Built for a
  phone first: 44px rows below `sm`, the status as one letter, the directory
  giving way before the file name; an open file's header sticks under the
  page header (measured from `[data-shell-header]`, which wraps on a phone);
  *next* closes the file and opens the one after it, and closing a file read
  halfway scrolls its row back into view. Lines **wrap** on a phone and scroll
  sideways inside the diff on a desktop (the *wrap* button switches it); the
  page itself never scrolls sideways. One line-number column on a phone (the
  old number on a removed line, tinted), two from `sm` up; on a desktop the
  patch keeps its 50vh box. A patch can be 200 KB, so it is drawn 400 rows at
  a time (*Show 400 more lines*, *Show all*), and a line past 2,000
  characters is cut until asked — a minified JSON line wrapped is thousands of
  rows.
- Timeline of tool events; token/cost sparkline for this session.
- **Chat** (WP-14, `components/ChatView.tsx`, `lib/chat.ts`) — the session
  as a conversation built from its stored events: your prompts as bubbles,
  what the agent wrote as text (`Prose`), each tool call as one line that
  opens to its input and result; subagent steps are left out. Order is the
  daemon's `(ts, id)`, and events are merged by id, so a late or replayed
  event lands in place and never twice. Opens on the newest 300 events with
  the newest 120 messages in the DOM; scrolling to the top reveals 120 more
  from memory, then pages back from the daemon (`?before=`). The scrolling
  rule holds (`useStickToBottom`, "↓ N new"). The keys bar (`TerminalKeys`)
  is the chat field, for an owned live session on a device that may control
  it: its six keys type through `POST /v1/agents/{id}/input`, one request at
  a time, and a message goes through the daemon's queue ([ADR-053](08-decisions.md)).
  A phone-width window opens a session on Chat, with the figures panel hidden
  there (and on the Terminal tab); a desktop still opens on Timeline. On a
  phone the chat is the screen — see § From a phone, *Chat first*. In the app
  workspace an agent tab is shown as its chat or as its terminal — see
  *Chat or Terminal* below.
  - *Chat or Terminal* (`lib/sessionView.ts`, owner, 2026-10-20, translated:
    "a chat should look like a chat, not like an ugly terminal"). The strip
    over an agent tab carries one quiet icon on its right, beside the
    inspector's (owner, 2026-10-20, variant A; until then two worded
    bordered pills, *Chat | Terminal*): no words and no border, the size
    and hover of the strip's other icon buttons, showing the *other* view —
    a terminal glyph over the chat, a chat bubble over the terminal — named
    *Show as terminal* / *Show as chat* in its tooltip and label; one click
    flips (`[data-view-switch]`, `data-view` the view it opens; a shell, a
    file or a page tab has none).
    A chat (ADR-071) — a quick chat, or any session whose folder is under
    the data directory's `chats/` — opens on Chat; a project session opens
    on Terminal. A switch is remembered for that session in this window's
    storage (`caprock.app.session-view`, the newest 200), so the tab opens
    the same way after a reload; there is no global default. Both views
    read the same session: the terminal stays mounted under the chat, its
    panel hidden as on a tab switch, so it paints nothing (no WebGL frame)
    while the chat is shown, and switching starts, signals and types
    nothing. The Chat view is this same `ChatView` with `desk`: a reading
    column no wider than 780px on a wide window (padding, so the scrollbar
    stays at the edge), each tool call as one quiet line in plain words
    (`donePhrase`: *Searched the web: …*, *Read main.go*, *Ran npm test*)
    that opens to the call as it was, *Message…* in the field and the raw
    keys behind **Keys**. The reply chips (*Reply to the agent*), the
    *Working* line and the permission card are the chat's own. What only
    the terminal answers says so in one line with **Open in Terminal**,
    which flips the switch: a question with options (AskUserQuestion —
    the daemon draws no card for it, its answers are not Yes and No) shows
    what it asks and its options, read-only, until its result lands; and
    several prompts at once keep the card's *answer there* with the same
    link.
  - *Working* (`lib/chatActivity.ts`). From the main thread's prompt until
    its Stop (or the session's end), one line under the conversation says the
    agent is at work: a spinner, *Claude is thinking…* (the session's agent
    by name), or the call it runs — the newest `tool.pre` with no result,
    as a phrase (*Running npm test*, *Reading main.go*, *Searching TODO*) —
    and a m:ss clock from the prompt. While a permission prompt waits it
    reads *Waiting for your permission*, in the warning colour, and where the
    page does not already draw the card (the Chats screen, the app's chat
    tab) the Yes / No card (`PermissionPrompt`, no keys) sits under it; the
    app's inspector and the bottom card stand down while the chat is shown,
    so one card answers it. A quick chat starts in the default permission
    mode, so a web fetch or a command asks; before 2026-10-19 the chat showed
    nothing while it did, and nothing while a long turn ran (a user's report:
    "no sign the agent was thinking about anything").
  - *What you send* (`OutgoingBubble`). Send draws your bubble at once
    with *Sending…* under it, then *Sent ✓* once the daemon took it; it
    gives way to the session's own record of the prompt (the first main-thread
    `turn.user` after it whose text holds the words), or goes after two
    minutes (a slash command records none). A message the daemon held is the
    queue's bubble instead (*Queued messages*, below). One the daemon refused
    reads *Not sent* with **Retry** and **Dismiss**. Before 2026-10-19 nothing
    showed until the transcript caught up, and the owner could not tell
    whether a message had gone.
  - *A reply's words arrive late.* Claude Code writes a reply as several
    transcript lines with one message id — thinking first, then the text —
    and the daemon stores the first and merges the text into it
    (store.MergeAssistantText). The merge is published as an `event_update`
    frame (03-contracts.md § Live socket), and the chat replaces the held
    event by id, as a refetch does (`mergeEvents`). Before, the words reached
    no open chat until it was opened again, while the turn's cost already
    showed (measured on a preview daemon: never within 30 s, 108 ms after).
  - *Pasted text.* A prompt's `<pasted_content …>…</pasted_content …>`
    blocks are drawn as a quiet *Pasted · N lines* block inside the bubble,
    the first six lines and *Show N more lines* past that; the typed words
    around them read as typed. A `<system-reminder>` inside a prompt is left
    out, and a `[Pasted text #1 +12 lines]` placeholder is a block with no
    text. The timeline's one-line `you:` row reads a paste as
    `[pasted · N lines]`.
  - *Queued messages* (`lib/queue.ts`, `components/QueuedList.tsx`). Send
    posts the text to `POST /v1/agents/{id}/queue`: typed in at once when
    the session sits at its prompt, otherwise held by the daemon and typed
    in when the turn ends, one message per turn, never into a permission
    prompt or a question. Held messages sit under the conversation as
    ordinary outgoing bubbles, not a choice: Send is the whole decision and
    the daemon types the message in by itself. Under each bubble one muted
    line says what it waits for (*Sends when Claude finishes*, with
    *· 1 of 2* when several; *Waiting: a permission prompt is on the screen;
    answer it first*; *Waiting: the agent asked a question; answer it in the
    terminal*), with small text links **Edit** (in place, Save or Discard
    changes; ⌘/Ctrl+Enter saves, Esc discards), **Cancel** and **Send now**
    (types it in now; secondary; not offered while a prompt or a question
    waits). *2026-10-19, owner (translated): "I dictated, sent, it didn't
    send, I still have to press a button — totally not obvious. It should
    just send by default."* The composer's status line reads *Message will
    send when the agent is free*. Edit's buttons stay 44px below
    `sm`. The queue is the daemon's, so the phone and the Mac show the same
    one: every change is a `queue` frame, and the list is read again after
    a reconnect. Sending jumps the view to the bottom, as a messenger does.
    A message forwarded from the Telegram bot (ADR-081) reads *from
    Telegram* after its *Queued* pill while held, and in faint 11px under
    its bubble once it is in the conversation (`via` on the row, and on the
    prompt's event payload).
    A message held while offline (*Will send when connected*) goes to the
    queue on reconnect rather than straight into the terminal.
  - *`/` and `@` pickers* (`components/ChatPicker.tsx`, `lib/pickers.ts`).
    `/` at the very start of the field lists the session's slash commands
    (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/commands`: the agent's built-ins, then the
    folder's, then the user's, a *project*/*user*/*skill* tag on the
    custom ones, the argument hint in mono); `@` at the start of a word
    lists the files of the session's folder (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/files`),
    fuzzy-filtered as typed, 50 at most. The list floats above the field
    (it never resizes the conversation under the reader): ↑ ↓ move, Enter
    or Tab picks, Esc closes and keeps the text; rows are 44px below `sm`
    and a tap keeps the field's focus, so a phone's keyboard stays up. A
    pick replaces the trigger and the word under the caret with
    `/name ` or `@path `; nothing is sent until Send. Both lists are read
    once per session per minute. The placeholder says it: *Type · /
    commands · @ files*. The terminal's keys bar has the pickers too, but
    not the queue: what is typed there answers what is on the screen.

### Continue, and why not

The session header offers **continue here** for an ended session and **branch
here** for one running somewhere else, or — when neither can work — says why in
place of the button: the transcript is gone (Claude Code deletes them after 30
days by default), the folder it ran in no longer exists, Caprock cannot find
`claude` to start it, or the agent is not one Caprock can resume. Codex and
OpenCode sessions get **continue here** once ended (by their own id, see
[ADR-031](08-decisions.md)) and their own **copy command** either way; a Codex
or OpenCode session Caprock started that was never typed into says there is
nothing to continue. The server decides, from what is on disk
([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)); the header used to decide from who started
the session and which agent it was. Measured on a copy of the owner's
database on 2026-09-29, over its 116 ended Claude Code sessions: the 96 started
elsewhere all offered the button and 65 of those failed — 44 transcripts
deleted, 21 folders gone, each a terminal that opened and died — while the 20
Caprock had started offered nothing, though 12 of them could be resumed
(FB-036). The Terminal tab of an ended session
says it has ended and offers the same continue, rather than the copy written
for a live session somebody else started.

**A continue keeps the session's permission mode.** The owner runs Claude Code
with permissions skipped; a session he continued from Caprock came back in the
default mode and asked before every command (2026-10-06), because the resume
named no mode. The daemon now fills one in when the request has none
(`resume.permission_mode`, [03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)): the mode the
session's own hooks last reported, else the Settings preference below. Beside
**continue here**, **branch here** and **Continue it here** a small select
shows that mode in the dialog's words (*Bypass · never asks*) and can change it
before the click; *default mode* is offered only when nothing was recorded. A
card on Now, with no room for a select, names it after the button (*bypass
permissions*), and the project terminal's **⋯** menu names it beside each
continue or branch. The trust prompt is unaffected: Caprock pre-accepts the
folder for every Claude Code launch, and `--permission-mode
bypassPermissions` (like `--dangerously-skip-permissions`) never suppressed it.

**The mode changes while the session runs** (ADR-054). A chip
(`PermissionModeChip`) in the session header and in the cockpit's first block
says the mode: the session's footer read now, else its newest hook, else its
command line (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/permission-mode`). For a Claude Code
session Caprock started and holds, the chip opens a menu in two parts. *In
this session* lists the modes the process can reach, under *Switch now
(Shift+Tab)*: *Manual*,
*Accept edits*, *Plan*, *Bypass* when it was started able to reach it,
*Auto* — and picking one makes the daemon
press Shift+Tab until the footer shows it; the chip then shows what the
footer says, and a switch that did not get there says so beside the chip in
the daemon's words, with the mode it was left in. *Needs a restart* lists
the rest (*Restart in Bypass* in a process without it, *Restart in Don't
ask*); picking
one asks first, in the menu, what a restart does, and only then stops the
process and resumes the conversation under its own id in the same tab. The
first bypass restart on a machine shows the bypass consent note above
*Accept and restart*. **Each mode is a two-line row** (owner, 2026-10-12:
"great, but hard to read"): a small mark (a raised hand, a pencil with a
check, a list, a bolt, a sparkle, a struck shield; the two that take the
guard off in the warning colour), the name at 15px semibold in the
foreground colour, and what it does on its own line at 13px muted
(*Reads and plans; changes nothing*). The current mode has an accent bar on
its left, a tint and *now*. A row that cannot be picked says why on a line
of its own in the muted colour (*The agent is working: restart when its
turn ends.*), never as faint grey on grey. The menu is 340px wide (less on
a narrow window), scrolls when the window is short, and every line in it
measured at least 4.5:1 against its background in all seven themes. The menu waits: nothing while a prompt or a question
is on the screen, no restart while the agent works. It opens fixed under the
chip, so a phone's header row, which scrolls sideways, does not clip it. A
session Caprock did not start shows the chip without a menu; its tooltip
says the mode changes in that terminal with Shift+Tab. Codex, OpenCode and
the others show no chip: their mode is not read.

***Bypass · never asks* now never asks.** It is started as
`--dangerously-skip-permissions`, the flag the orchestrator's own unattended
workers use; `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` leaves the session
interactive, and the owner picked bypass for an autonomous run and was asked on
nearly every call (2026-10-07). Sessions are also started with `--add-dir` for
the directories Caprock writes to itself, so a pasted screenshot is not a
question. What neither can lift is Claude Code's own
`permissions.blockReadsOutsideWorkingDirectories`, a perimeter that asks in
every mode and on every command whose paths are computed at run time; a user
who wants an unattended run turns that setting off. See
[03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md).

**A session that exits leaves a shell, not a dead tab.** `/exit` — or `exit`,
or a crash — used to leave the tab showing a terminal nothing was behind: the
pty's exit frame reached the terminal client and no component listened. The
tab now becomes a shell in the session's own folder, keeping its place in the
strip and in a split (`replace-session`, `ui/src/lib/tabs.ts`); a shell that
exits is closed, since there is nothing to put in its place. A quick chat is
the exception: its folder is Caprock's own, so its tab says *The chat ended*
with *Close tab* and *Start again* instead ([21-app.md](21-app.md) § A quick
chat's tab). Each window
showing the tab asks for that shell with `replaces: <session>`, so the app, a
browser tab and a phone get one shell between them, not one each.

**A restart of Caprock does not end a session it started** — the terminal lives
in a pty-host and the next daemon reattaches it, so the Terminal tab simply
shows it running ([ADR-033](08-decisions.md#adr-033--an-owned-session-outlives-the-daemon-its-terminal-lives-in-a-pty-host)).
What is left is a session started by a release from before that, or one whose
holder died (`detached`). Its Terminal tab says, in these words: *"This
session's terminal was closed when Caprock restarted. The conversation is saved
— Continue it here resumes it in a new terminal."* **Continue it here** is the
primary button: the same conversation under the same id, after the daemon stops
the terminal-less process (it is Caprock's own). Below it, **open a copy
instead** starts a fork and says that it leaves the old process running; **copy
command** stays. The header offers **continue here** for it too, not **branch
here**. The panel used to say *"Caprock restarted since this session began, and
its terminal went with that run"* over **branch here**, and the owner could not
tell what had happened or what the button would do (FB-040 before that: it
opened an empty terminal).

### Continue in another agent (relay)

**Continue in… ▾** sits beside continue/branch in the session header and lists
the agents this machine can start (`ui/src/components/RelayDialog.tsx`). It
answers the same wish as continue when continue cannot work — the transcript
gone, an agent Caprock does not resume — or when the work should move to
another agent. Choosing one opens a dialog whose first sentence says this
starts a **new** session with a summary as its first message, not the same
conversation, and that the agent sees only what is in the box. Below: the
folder, the passage's age, the changed-file and PR counts, and the brief
itself in an editable box (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/relay`). Nothing is sent
until **Start <agent> with this message**; the new session opens on its
terminal. A folder that no longer exists disables the button and says why.
Both pages name the other end: "Carries on from a <agent> session: <title> —
started with a summary, not its conversation" and "Carried on in <agent>
<id>" ([ADR-032](08-decisions.md)). Hidden on paired devices.

### Choosing the agent

The New session dialog (`SpawnDialog`) starts its form with an **Agent** select
(`ui/src/components/AgentPicker.tsx`, reusable by any "new session" entry
point): Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode, Gemini CLI — only those whose binary the
daemon found (`*_available` on `/v1/status`), and hidden entirely when there is
one. The picker starts on the **default agent**, never on the last one used
(owner, 2026-10-11: one Codex session made every later New agent Codex):
Claude Code until *Make default*, beside the Agent label, makes the current
choice the default (`localStorage` `caprock.spawn.defaultAgent`, per
browser); a default that is no longer installed falls back to the first one
found. The model field follows the agent: Claude's and
Gemini's fixed lists — Claude's is the newest model of each family, each label
naming its exact version (*Opus 5.5*, never a bare *Opus*), opening on the
newest Opus, and a test fails the build when `pricing.json` carries a newer
model of a family than the list offers — Codex's own catalog from `GET /v1/agents/models` with its
configured default named, OpenCode a free-text `provider/model`. The
permission choices keep Claude's words and each says what it becomes for the
chosen agent, or that the agent's own config decides.

**Settings → New sessions → Start in** (`SpawnModeSetting`) is the mode both
new-session dialogs (`SpawnDialog`, `NewAgentSheet`) open on, stored by the
daemon (`spawn_permission_mode`), so the app, a browser and the phone agree.
Unset, the dialogs open on **Bypass · never asks** (the owner's decision, 2026-10-07; it was *Accept edits*); a bypass start from a paired phone is still confirmed inline. The daemon also applies
it to a start that names no mode — the project terminal's **New session
here**, a quick chat — and to a continue whose session recorded none. A mode
picked in a dialog is used for that session only; the preference changes only
in Settings.

A spawned Codex or OpenCode session whose first message has not linked yet
says the agent writes nothing until the first message, which Caprock links to
the session then; once linked, the header shows the agent's own id next to the agent's name.

### 1-click terminal

Owner request (2026-10-02): from where you see the work, one click and you are
typing in it. Two places carry it:

- **A Projects row** has a full-size primary button under its figures. The
  first version (PR #137, 2026-10-02) was an 11px `>_` at the row's edge that
  opened a menu; nobody found it. The button now says what it will do, chosen
  from the sessions in that project (`projectAction` in
  `ui/src/components/ProjectTerminal.tsx`):
  - **Terminal** — a live session Caprock holds is running there: opens the
    newest one's terminal (`#/session/<id>?tab=terminal`), no menu.
  - **Pick up in a terminal** — the newest live session was started
    elsewhere: branches it into a new process (`claude --resume --fork-session`)
    when its transcript allows, else opens its page, which says why not.
  - **New session here** — nothing is live: starts one in the project's
    directory (`POST /v1/agents`).

  A small **⋯** beside it keeps the full menu: Caprock's own live sessions
  (**open**), others that can be carried on (**continue** when ended,
  **branch** while running elsewhere) and **+ new session here**. Sessions
  that cannot be carried on are left out; their reason is on their own card.
  The list comes from `GET /v1/sessions?dir=`, the row's own grouping
  ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)). The buttons are 36px tall, reachable by
  Tab, and wrap under the row at 390px.
- **A session card on Now** for a running session Caprock started opens its
  terminal directly, rather than the timeline with the terminal a tab away.
  Ended cards keep **continue**.

Rule 7 holds throughout: a session Caprock did not start is never typed into;
continue and branch start a second process on the same history. On a paired
device, which may not start processes, the menu offers only Caprock's own live
sessions.

### Open in my terminal

Owner request (2026-10-04): typing in the web terminal is uncomfortable. The
session's title row carries **Open in Ghostty ↗** — named for the terminal it
will open — with **▾** for the rest, beside **Open repo ↗**: both open this
session's work somewhere other than here, and the title row is on every tab,
the Terminal tab included. A row in a Projects row's `>_` menu carries a small
**↗** for the same thing. What it may do comes from the server
(`open_terminal`, [03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)):

- **An ended session** opens with one click, resumed in its folder.
- **A session Caprock is running** is never stopped by one click. The click
  opens the menu, which offers **Move to …** (stops it here, then carries the
  same conversation on there) and, for Claude Code, **Fork into …** (a branch
  under a new id; this one keeps running). On a Projects menu row the ↗ turns
  into **move?** and takes a second click.
- **A session running somewhere else** can only be forked (Claude Code), or the
  button is disabled with the reason beside it.

The menu also lists the installed terminals; picking one makes it the default
(`settings.terminal`). Hidden on a paired device and for agents with no resume
by id (Gemini).

## The terminal

A real terminal for sessions Caprock started, and the reason the first person
to use Caprock full-time stopped opening the Claude Code IDE. Never for a
session started elsewhere (rule 7).

**The terminal is dark in every palette.** Its palette is Dark Warm's —
`TERMINAL_THEME` in `ui/src/components/Terminal.tsx`, the dark tokens written
out — and its container is `bg-term-bg` with a `border-term-border` hairline;
the page around it stays Book or Light. TUIs draw for a dark ground: on paper,
Claude Code's dim text and status line were grey on cream and unreadable
(owner report, 2026-10-04). The palette used to be read from the theme tokens
once, when the terminal opened, so switching theme with a terminal open left it
in the other theme's colours — paper on a dark page. A light/dark toggle
re-themes nothing now, and a test toggles the theme both ways with a terminal
mounted. What does move it is temperature (2026-10-11): each app palette names
a dark terminal (`dashTerminal` in `lib/themes.ts`) — Dark Warm's for the warm
palettes, Dark Cold's for Dark Cold, Light Cold and Light Side, Dark Pastel's
for Dark Pastel, Dark Side's for Dark Side — and `dashTerminalTheme` follows a palette switch with a terminal
open; `--color-term-bg` carries the same ground per palette in `tokens.css`.

**The keyboard first (2026-10-04).** Opening a session's terminal froze the
tab and typing lagged (owner report). A profile on a copy of his database with
a busy session found three things in the way, all now out of it:

- **WebGL ran before the socket.** Creating the WebGL context took 1.5 s in his
  Chrome and linking its shaders another 0.4 s in a headless profile, all
  synchronous, all before the socket was created. Now the terminal opens, takes
  focus and connects at once; the WebGL renderer is swapped in after the first
  output, once nobody has typed for 1.5 s (`WEBGL_QUIET_MS`). The DOM renderer
  draws until then.
- **The socket waited for the session's figures.** The terminal mounted only
  after `GET /v1/sessions/{id}` answered — 3.3 s on a busy daemon in one run.
  On the Terminal tab it now mounts before the detail arrives, assuming the
  session is Caprock's; the daemon holds sockets only for processes it started
  (rule 7), and the panel corrects itself when the detail lands. One element
  serves both layouts, so the detail arriving re-renders it rather than
  dropping the socket.
- **The figures were refetched on every live event.** On the Terminal tab the
  detail (about 500 KB for a long session) is polled every 30 s instead.

Measured in headless Chrome against that copy, with a fake `claude` printing
4000 coloured lines and a 12 fps spinner: before, keys typed on open went
nowhere until the terminal was clicked, the first echo came at 1.2–1.6 s, and
a key's dispatch blocked for up to 200–290 ms; after, keys typed on open reach
the session, the first echo comes at 0.4–0.8 s (one cold-cache run at 2.9 s),
and a key's dispatch blocks for 13–94 ms.

**It says when it is starting.** A resumed session can take half a minute to
print anything, and a black panel cannot be told from a broken one. Until the
first bytes arrive the panel says "Starting the session… N s"; after 30 s with
nothing (`START_TIMEOUT_MS`), or when the socket closes first, it says so and
offers **Retry**, which reconnects. The first output also triggers one fit and
one size message, so the TUI draws for the real size.

### Projects in the browser

The browser and installed web app show **Projects** near the top of Home,
before the dashboard figures, at tablet width and wider (Android tablets
included): it does not rely on the desktop app's sidebar. **Not on the
phone** (under `sm`), neither on the phone home nor on its full dashboard:
0.135.0 put it there too, and the owner asked for the 0.134.0 phone Home back
(2026-10-19, translated: "Why do I see in the mobile version all the projects
I haven't used in ages? … put it back the way it was"). The phone reaches its
work through Continue and *All sessions*.

`components/BrowserProjects.tsx` reads the computer's saved projects, including
those without sessions, plus live work in unlisted repositories. Archived
projects stay hidden. A project expands into its live and recent sessions;
one tap opens the session's Chat view. Recent history is read only for the
expanded project, including each member of a multi-repo project. Live
sessions are read without the recent-history page cap, assigned to the
closest project, and merged over recent history by id. Chats remain on Chats.
This is navigation only: it never starts or types into a process. Failed
reads show a retry rather than pretending the project is empty. When a tablet
becomes narrow, the kept phone home consumes only its own share or shortcut
parameters; it never rewrites the open session's address.

### The phone home

What Caprock opens to at phone width outside the app workspace — the
home-screen web app above all (owner, 2026-10-13, translated: "I really miss
a Caprock app on the phone with quick access — to start new chats, save
stuff, and so on"). `#/` renders `components/PhoneHome.tsx` under `sm`
(`useNarrowScreen`); the full Now screen moved to `#/now` (*Full dashboard*
at the bottom, *All sessions* on Continue). Two layouts were drawn at 390×844
and the owner picked the second: the actions docked at the bottom where the
thumb is, not as tiles at the top. **The dock is no longer docked**
(owner, 2026-10-19, translated: "until voice input works on the phone in any
language, we won't push that button … chats separately — a tab or a
collapsible list"): the same field now sits inside the folded **Chats**
section, and the screen is otherwise what 0.134.0 showed. No figures and no charts: the sessions
list, the projects, the Inbox and the release plan are all it reads.

- **One compact bar, not the dashboard header** (owner, 2026-10-13: it
  should feel like an app, not the desktop dashboard squeezed).
  `components/PhoneBar.tsx` replaces the Shell's header on this screen only:
  the mark, *Caprock* and the connection state on the left, one ⋯ on the
  right holding Dashboard (`#/now`), Settings, Feedback and Get premium.
  The setup strip, the star strip, the share toast and the site footer are
  not drawn here either. Every other screen, and every desktop, keeps the
  full header.
- **Waiting on you** — the first session Caprock started that waits on you,
  with `PermissionPrompt` (no key hints: no terminal on the page) and
  **Open**; `· n` when more wait. The card's words wrap anywhere
  (`overflow-wrap: anywhere`): a long tool name, path or *always* answer
  set the page's width on the owner's Android phone (2026-10-19), and there
  one wide line widens the layout viewport, so even fixed bars ran off. The
  e2e clip test checks every element at 360, 390 and 412 px, and
  `ui/e2e/phone.spec.ts` every phone screen (§ From a phone, *Nothing
  wider than the screen*).
- **Continue** — `continueList` (`lib/phonehome.ts`): waiting, then working,
  then open, then ended, newest first within each, the last two days unless
  waiting or working, at most five; one tap into the chat. Chats are left
  out (`GET /v1/sessions?chats=exclude`): they have their own section. A
  live row's ⋯ holds End (§ From a phone, *End from a list*); an ended row
  keeps its chevron.
- **Chats** — one row, *Chats · n*, folded by default, above Continue.
  Unfolded: *All chats · n* to `#/chats`, the newest three chats (§ Chats),
  each with its title, model and age (a live chat's ⋯ holds End, as on
  Continue), then the dock below. Always drawn, so New chat is reachable
  before the first chat exists.
- **Releases** (`components/PhoneReleases.tsx`; owner, 2026-10-15,
  translated: "In the mobile app I want to see my releases' progress bars,
  to understand what's going on") — one repository's release plan
  ([§ Release plan](#release-plan)) at phone size: the next version, *n
  merged · PR #N in CI (2/3 ✓) · … · last vX … ago* (the release line), a row per
  piece of work with its four-step bar and stage word, then what is not in
  a release yet. A row opens its pull request on GitHub; an agent's row,
  its session's chat. A
  chip picks the repository when the board names several (the last pick
  is kept per viewer, `caprock.phone.releaseRepo`; else the first on
  GitHub). A footer says where the rows come from and how old they are
  (*Pull requests from GitHub, read 3m ago*, *GitHub not connected on your
  Mac: agents only*, *Not refreshed: …*) with **Refresh**. No timer:
  `GET /v1/work` and the repository's `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/ci/stats` are
  read on opening, on release news (a live `github` frame, or `reset`), on
  *Refresh* and on a pull; the kept Pipeline answer (`stats:<root>`) is
  drawn at once meanwhile, and an answer the daemon is still composing
  (`refreshing`) is asked again at most three times, 1.5 s apart. A daemon
  that refuses the board draws no section.
- **Pull to refresh** (`usePullToRefresh`, `lib/phone.ts`) — in the
  home-screen app only (`display-mode: standalone`), where the browser
  offers none: a pull of 64px at the top of the page (the finger travels
  twice that) reads Releases and the Inbox again, with *Pull to refresh* /
  *Release to refresh* above the first section. In a browser tab its own
  pull reloads the page, which reads everything anyway.
- **Saved** — the Inbox (§ The Inbox on the Mac), three rows then *All n*,
  the first image as a thumbnail, × to delete on a controller.
- **Chats row** — folded by default, with a **+ New chat** button (44px) at its right: one tap unfolds the section and focuses the field below, so a new chat is one tap from Home. Unfolded it lists the newest three chats and *All chats* (`#/chats`, the same list the desktop sidebar's Chats shows). Continue lists only active or recent (two days) sessions, waiting ones first, each saying *waiting for you* / *working* / *idle* with its age; a tap opens the session's chat.
- **The dock** — inside the unfolded Chats section, in the page's flow (it
  was fixed at the bottom of the screen until 2026-10-19): the model chip
  (vendor · model, § Chats), a project chip whose first choice is *No
  project*, a camera button, a field that grows to five lines (*Ask
  anything, or jot something down…*, or *Ask in <project>…*), and **Save**
  / **New chat** (**Start chat** once something is typed or a project
  picked). With no project — the default — the button starts a chat at once
  (`POST /v1/agents {chat: true, agent, model, prompt?}`, photos through
  `POST /v1/paste` and quoted into the prompt) and opens it at
  `#/chats/<id>`; an empty field starts an empty chat, one tap. With a
  project, words start the session there (`{cwd: project root, agent,
  subscription, prompt}`) and an empty field opens *Start in a project*.
  *Save* opens the *Save* sheet with what was typed and picked. The model is
  the one Quick chat remembers (`caprock.quickchat`); the project is
  remembered per viewer (`caprock.phone.dock.project`). A phone the owner
  has not let control sees a line saying where that is granted instead of
  the dock.
- **Start in a project sheet** — project list (six, then *All n projects*),
  the agents installed and ready as a four-wide grid (a phone gets only the
  agents Caprock knows; the daemon refuses it a custom one), *Runs on* when
  the agent has more than one sign-in (DeepSeek among them, with its note),
  an optional first message. A chat needs none of it: the daemon picks a
  chat's folder, and a controller may start one (ADR-071, amending ADR-034).
- **Save sheet** — text, up to four photos (downscaled as the chat's are),
  *Save to* chips: *Inbox*, four projects, *More…* as a native select.
  `POST /v1/inbox`.
- **From outside the page** — `manifest.json` names two shortcuts
  (`#/?open=chat`, `#/?open=save`) and a GET share target (`/?title&text&url`);
  the home reads either once and puts the address back to `#/`. Both need
  an installed web app, which Android Chrome makes only for an `https`
  origin and iOS never: on today's `http://` LAN or Tailscale origin they
  do nothing (OQ-12), and the docs say so.
- **Install hint** — once, in a phone's browser rather than the home-screen
  app (`display-mode: standalone` / `navigator.standalone`): Share → Add to
  Home Screen on iOS, ⋮ → Add to Home screen on Android; *Got it* keeps it
  away (`caprock.phone.installHint`).

**The Inbox on the Mac.** What the phone's Save (or another app's Share)
keeps is listed in the Quick chat chooser under *Saved*, newest first: the
text cut to two lines, the first image as a thumbnail (fetched with the
device token on a paired device, since an `<img>` cannot send it), and
project or *Inbox*, the phone's name and age. *Start a chat* starts a quick
chat on the agent, model and sign-in picked above; its first message is the
item's text and then each file's path, quoted as a pasted path is, in the
item's project folder when it has one, else in a chat folder. The item stays
after the chat starts. *Delete* takes two clicks and removes the item's
files too. The list re-reads on the live `inbox` frame and after a `reset`,
and draws nothing when the Inbox is empty. There is no sidebar row for it:
the chooser is where a chat starts. Code: `components/InboxList.tsx`,
`lib/inbox.ts`.

### From a phone

**One PTY, two screens.** A phone on the Terminal tab watches at the
desktop's size until it types, then the PTY is the phone's size until the
desktop is focused, clicked or typed into again, or the phone goes out of
sight (locked, in the background, the tab left), which hands the desktop its
size back at once; the chat never resizes the PTY (it mounts no terminal). See
[21-app.md § Terminal protocol v2](21-app.md#terminal-protocol-v2), *Size*.

**Chat first** (owner's report, 2026-10-11, translated: his partner on an
iPhone found the terminal laggy, "no chat on the phone, no access into that
session", and wanted chat and voice input "like Orca — that's just basics").
Walked at 390×844 on a preview stand, the chat existed but was eight panels
of scrolling away (the session cards come last on Now), behind a row of five
12px tabs under a header two rows tall, and a session started outside
Caprock showed no field and no reason. So:

- **Now starts with the running sessions** on a phone (`PhoneSessions` in
  `screens/Now.tsx`, under `sm` only): 56px rows — project, title or
  *waiting for you* (amber), the badge — one tap into
  `#/session/<id>?tab=chat`. A session Caprock did not start says *read
  only* on its row. The cards further down are unchanged.
- **A session is the whole screen** (`Shell`: on the session route under
  `sm`, outside the app, the page header, the setup and star strips and the
  site footer are not drawn; `main` pads by `safe-area-inset-top` only).
  Continue and branch move to the chat's footer.
- **One 44px bar** (`components/PhoneSessionBar.tsx`, every tab under `sm`;
  owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "When I scroll, a lot of space is taken by
  the header — project name, work mode etc. — and these tabs Terminal, Chat
  and so on … I want the header to take minimum space because I need to read
  this chat"). It was three rows — project and title, the controls, then
  `PhoneTabs`, 133px — and on the relay page, under its 45px machine bar,
  the conversation started 178px down a 390×844 screen. Measured
  2026-10-14 on a preview stand (headless Chromium, 390×844 and 360×780,
  mobile emulation, a local relay): now 44px, and 0 while the bar is
  slid away; the machine bar is not drawn while connected. Now: back **‹**, the title
  (one line, ellipsis), a `HealthDot`, a second dot only when the connection
  is not *Live* (`readLink`), and the views as one segmented control,
  **Chat | Terminal | ···**, the third a menu of Changes, Timeline and
  Answers that shows the chosen one's name. A tap on the title opens the
  **sheet** under the bar: project, the `Badge`, model, cost,
  `ConnectionState`, the controls (permission mode, pause/resume/kill,
  relay), then what the desktop shows above its tabs (ids, branch,
  subscription, folder, Remove, terminal and repository links, activity),
  and the paired computers when there are several (`lib/machineswitch.ts`,
  handed over by the remote page). The figures panel stays off Chat and
  Terminal.
- **The bar hides on scroll in the chat** (`useHideOnScroll`, `lib/phone.ts`):
  on Chat the bar is drawn *over* the conversation, which keeps its first
  44px free (`ChatView` `topInset`), so sliding it away moves nothing. The
  reader scrolling down past the first 44px hides it; scrolling up, reaching
  the top, or the 20px strip left at the top brings it back. Only a scroll
  within 500 ms of a touch, a wheel or a key counts: the chat following a
  streaming answer to the bottom never hides it. A permission card sits
  below the bar (it pads by the bar's height, and the wrapper collapses when
  no prompt waits). On the other tabs the page scrolls and the bar stays
  at the top (`position: sticky`, its background reaching up over the
  safe area; owner, 2026-10-15: the way back was only visible after
  scrolling up); its sheet and view menu open under it wherever the page
  is scrolled. Back is a 44px target.
- **End** (owner, 2026-10-15: end the current session quickly): a red
  *End* at the bar's right end, only for a session Caprock started and
  still holds (`owned`, not ended, not detached — rule 7) on a device that
  may control it (`POST /v1/agents/{id}/signal` is on `controllerRoutes`
  only, so a viewer phone, and a relay phone holding the viewer role, sees
  none). It opens one bottom sheet — *End <title>? The conversation is
  saved and can be continued.*, **Keep it** / **End session** — which
  sends `end`, as closing a desktop tab does: SIGTERM, so Claude Code
  writes its transcript out and `--resume` works, then a kill only if the
  process is still running after the grace period (03-contracts.md §
  `POST /v1/agents/{id}/signal`); never the immediate `kill`. The
  session's row turns ended on the live frame. An error stays in the
  sheet. A live session that cannot be ended here is never silently
  without End (owner, 2026-10-16): the title's sheet opens on one line,
  *End · <why>* (below).
- **End from a list** (owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "From the phone I
  can't end a session — e.g. I have some trial chats and I can't end them
  if I want"; `components/PhoneEnd.tsx`). Every live row on the phone
  home's Chats and Continue, on All sessions (`#/now`'s *Sessions · tap to
  chat*) and on the Chats screen has a ⋯ where its chevron was. Its menu —
  a sheet at the bottom under `sm`, a popover wider — has **End
  session…** first; a swipe left on the row (60px or more, mostly
  sideways) opens the same confirm. That confirm is the bar's sheet above,
  and it sends the same `end`. The row then reads *ended* at once
  (`useEndedHere`) rather than on the next read; All sessions, which lists
  live sessions only, keeps the row there, ended, for as long as the
  screen is open. A chat stays in Chats, ended, and can be continued
  (§ Chats). One chat's own ⋯ (`screens/Chat.tsx`) has *End this chat…*
  first. `endState` decides, the same for every place:
  - **Ended:** nothing to end, so no End and no reason; a row with nothing
    else to offer has no ⋯.
  - **Started outside Caprock** (`owned` false): *Started in your own
    terminal — Caprock never stops it (rule 7).* This comes before the role
    check, since allowing control would not change it. There is no End and
    no swipe for it, and the daemon refuses it anyway (`agents.Manager.End`:
    not owned).
  - **Detached** (Caprock started it, its terminal is gone): *Caprock no
    longer holds its terminal after a restart, so it cannot end it from
    here.*
  - **A viewer device** (ADR-034): *This phone can only watch — allow
    control on your computer: Settings → Use on your phone → Allow control.*

  The reason is the menu's one line, and a swipe on such a row opens the
  menu, never a confirm. Nothing here can signal a process Caprock did not
  start (CLAUDE.md rule 7).
- **Nothing wider than the screen** (owner, 2026-10-21, translated: "on
  the phone there is still horizontal scrolling"). Two causes the earlier
  checks could not see, because neither widens the page in a desktop
  browser:
  - **A field under 16px.** iOS zooms the page in when such a field takes
    the focus and leaves it zoomed, and the zoomed page pans sideways: the
    phone home's Repository and Project pickers were 14px, most Settings
    fields and Cost's cap 12px. Under `sm` or on a touch screen every
    input, textarea and select is 16px (`design/tokens.css`, unlayered, so
    it beats the `text-[12px]` utilities and `.input`).
  - **A box that scrolls sideways.** A chat file's Markdown viewer was
    1,512px wide inside a 360px phone: in `ProseDoc`'s grid one long link
    or path set the column's width (its implicit track is `auto`, and a
    word's min-content survives `break-word`). Its column and its list
    items' are `minmax(0, 1fr)` now. A reply's table no longer fits the
    width by breaking each cell into two-letter lines (the reply's
    `overflow-wrap: anywhere` was inherited): it keeps its words whole and
    scrolls in its own frame, as code blocks do.

  `ui/e2e/phone.spec.ts` holds every phone screen to it at 360, 390 and
  430 px in a phone profile ([10-infrastructure.md § E2E](10-infrastructure.md#e2e-make-e2e)).
- **The chat fills the screen** (`useFillViewport`, `lib/phone.ts`): its
  height is the visual viewport's less its top, re-measured when the
  viewport changes (the keyboard rising) and when anything above it does
  (a permission card), so the field sits at the bottom and above the
  keyboard. Tool lines are 44px tall under `sm`. A permission prompt's card
  sits above the tabs with its 48px buttons, so answering is one tap.
- **The field is the desktop chat's** (`TerminalKeys`): the **Message…**
  field on a row of its own, then **Keys** (the six raw keys, folded),
  **Photo**, the dictation language chip, the microphone and **Send**. Send
  is the queue, as on a desktop (`POST /v1/agents/{id}/queue`); the offline
  hold (*Will send when connected*) and the `/` and `@` pickers are kept.
  The quick replies are not shown on a phone. The session's ··· menu
  (`PhoneSessionBar`) also offers **Keys** (a panel under the bar that stays
  while they are pressed, each sent as raw input to a session Caprock
  started, never queued) and **Voice · EN** (the language list).
- **No walkie-talkie bar** (owner, 2026-10-21: removed, unused and in the
  way). The one-row bar of 0.128.0 — a speaker switch for auto read-aloud,
  a **+** sheet (Photo, New chat, Dictation language, Report a bug), a
  one-line field and a hold-to-talk microphone — and the latest answer's
  card with **AI;DR**, **Read aloud**, **Continue** and **Stop** are gone,
  with their client code (`lib/holdToTalk.ts`, `lib/readAloud.ts`,
  `lib/tldr.ts`). The latest answer is a message like any other. New chat
  is on the phone home; Report a bug is ⌘K on a computer only. The daemon
  keeps `POST /v1/events/{id}/tldr` ([ADR-072](08-decisions.md)) for other
  clients; no screen calls it now.
- **No field says why** (`chatFooter`, `screens/Session.tsx`): a viewer
  phone is told where control is granted (Settings → *Open Caprock on your
  phone* → *Allow control*); a session started outside Caprock
  can be read and not typed into (rule 7), with **Branch here to type** — a
  second process on the conversation, the original left running; an ended
  or detached one offers **Continue here**. Continuing or starting a session
  on a phone lands on its chat (`ContinueSession`, `SpawnDialog`).
- **Voice input** (`lib/speech.ts`, the microphone in `TerminalKeys`,
  `voice`): on a phone or tablet chat only (narrow or `pointer: coarse`).
  **The language is the viewer's** (`lib/speechlang.ts`; owner, 2026-10-14,
  translated: "speech recognition is in English for some reason; I speak
  Russian — we need multi-language"): the Web Speech API cannot detect it
  and was given `navigator.language`. A chip left of the microphone says it
  (**EN**, **RU**; on a phone also *Voice* in the session's ··· menu,
  above); a tap moves through the browser's `navigator.languages`,
  one per base language (`ru` becomes `ru-RU`), plus any picked before, and
  past the last opens a menu of those and fifteen common BCP-47 tags (en,
  ru, uk, de, fr, es, pt, it, pl, tr, ja, zh, ko, ar, he). The choice is kept
  per viewer (`caprock.speech.lang`, and `caprock.speech.langs` for picks
  outside the browser's list) and used for every dictation. A browser naming
  more than one language gets the chip in the accent and one line under the
  field, once (`caprock.speech.langSeen`).
  It uses the browser's speech recognition (`SpeechRecognition`, or
  `webkitSpeechRecognition` in Safari): continuous, with interim results
  shown in the field after what was typed, rebuilt from every result each
  time because Safari can repeat a final one. Tap again to stop; nothing is
  sent until Send. While it listens a line says *Your browser's speech
  service turns it into text* — Safari sends the audio to Apple, Chrome to
  Google; Caprock records nothing and gets only the text (rule 4: the user
  starts it, every time, and is told where it goes). Browsers allow speech
  recognition only in a secure context, and a phone reaches Caprock over
  `http://` on a Wi-Fi or Tailscale address, which is not one; there the
  button says, in one line, to use the dictation key on the keyboard. A
  refused microphone, no speech or an unreachable service get their own
  line. Serving Caprock over https on the Tailscale name, which would make
  the button work on the phone, is not built ([12-risks.md](12-risks.md)
  `OQ-12`).

  **One language at a time** (owner, 2026-10-19, translated: "voice input
  uses the phone's language — I want all languages"). The Web Speech API
  listens for exactly the one `lang` it is given and cannot detect a
  second one in the same recording, and Caprock has no transcription of
  its own (no local model; the daemon never receives audio), so mixing
  languages in one message is not possible here. What there is: the
  language is always named while it listens (*Listening in Russian*, and
  on the phone's status line), it is one tap away (the chip, the + sheet's
  **Dictation language**, the session's ··· **Voice**), the last choice is
  remembered per viewer, and the chip cycles through this viewer's
  languages. English terms inside Russian speech usually come through in
  the Russian model; a sentence in the other language needs the switch.
  The choice is per origin: the relay (`m.caprock.dev`) and a LAN address
  each remember their own.

  **Never lost** (owner, 2026-10-19, translated: "messages dictated through
  the microphone sometimes don't get sent and get lost"). Every path from
  the microphone to the daemon keeps the words until the daemon has them:
  - *The service ends by itself* (Chrome on Android ends a "continuous"
    session after a pause, or on an abort it did not get from us):
    `startDictation` starts it again and keeps every word heard (at most 40
    restarts, three in a row that hear nothing, never on a fatal error or a
    hidden page). If it does stop while the mic is held, the words stay in
    the field and the release (or the tap) sends them.
  - *Interim words with no final*: they are in the field and are sent; a
    later event with fewer results, or a blank final, never wipes them; a
    result list that starts over keeps what the old one held; a final that
    repeats the one before (Chrome on Android) is not said twice.
  - *Send while it listens* (the desktop-style bar): it stops, waits for the
    last words (at most 3 s) and sends once, with them.
  - *A send the daemon does not take* (an error, a closed relay, a throw):
    the message becomes a draft row, *Not sent — Caprock did not confirm
    it*, with **Retry** and **Edit**; photos that did not go keep their
    chips in the draft. Nothing is put back into a field that already
    holds something else.
  - *The page going away mid-send* (a phone discarding the tab, a relay
    reconnect): a message on its way is kept in `sessionStorage` until the
    daemon confirms it, and comes back as a draft, *it may not have
    arrived — check the chat before you retry*, never sent again by
    itself. What is in the field is kept too (`caprock.keys.draft.<id>`).
- **A light terminal on a touch device** (`Terminal.tsx`, `light` =
  `pointer: coarse`): no WebGL, no blinking cursor, `PHONE_SCROLLBACK`
  (2,000 lines, not 10,000), and output through `coalescedWriter`
  (`lib/termwrite.ts`): the first write after a quiet spell goes at once,
  later ones are joined and handed over at most every `PHONE_WRITE_MS`
  (100 ms); every write's callback still runs once parsed, which is what
  the socket's flow control counts. xterm is mounted only on the Terminal
  tab, as before, and unmounted when Chat is chosen. Measured 2026-10-11 on
  a preview stand: headless Chromium at 390×844 with mobile emulation and a
  4x CPU throttle, a synthetic busy session (a status line redrawn at
  12.5 Hz plus a log line every 320 ms — not Claude Code), a full 256 KB
  ring, two runs each. The terminal socket carried ~1.8 KB/s steady, the
  live socket ~0.63 KB/s, and the attach snapshot ~282 KB. Main-thread time
  over 10 s steady: 3.24 / 3.15 s before (WebGL), 1.09 / 1.15 s before with
  WebGL refused, 0.59 / 0.63 s after; attach (12 s) 4.7 / 4.1 s before,
  1.8 / 1.3 s after, longest task 305 / 268 ms before, 167 / 75 ms after;
  xterm renders 125 → 98 per 10 s. The Chat tab: 14 ms per 10 s and no
  terminal bytes. Headless Chromium draws WebGL in software, so the WebGL
  share is larger there than on an iPhone's GPU; on a real phone only the
  direction is known. A desktop is unchanged.

A phone the owner let control sessions ([ADR-034](08-decisions.md)) gets the
controls a viewer does not: **+ New session** on Now, the Terminal tab,
pause/resume/kill, continue, and approve/reject on Tasks. `useCanControl`
(`ui/src/lib/useCanControl.ts`) asks `GET /v1/pair/me` on load, every 15 s, on
focus and on every screen change, so a phone that loses control stops drawing
them; the daemon refuses them either way.

- **New session** on a phone is the machine's dialog: the path field (16px, no
  autofocus, so the keyboard does not cover the picker), **Recent** and
  **Browse** (rooted inside home), and Advanced (create the folder, worktree).
  The daemon refuses a folder outside home. Every permission mode is offered;
  **Bypass** asks once, inline, before starting: "The agent won't ask before
  running commands or editing files. Start?" with **Back** and **Start in
  bypass** (ADR-034). Not a browser `confirm()`: on a phone that is dismissed
  unread, and some in-app browsers suppress it.
- **The keys bar** (`TerminalKeys`) sits under the terminal: a 16px field (iOS
  zooms into anything smaller), full width on a row of its own — one line
  idle, growing to five, then scrolling; with the focus it glows
  (`.composer-field`, `tokens.css`: its border in the accent at 60% and a 3px
  ring at 16%, 85% and 26% once it holds text, `data-filled`; border and
  shadow only, so its size never changes, and no transition under reduced
  motion; owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "a light highlight on the chat input
  line so I understand I'm actively focused in it"); at rest it carries a hint
  of the accent, 4% in its fill and 22% in its edge, 7% fill with the focus,
  on the terminal slab's own ground in the app's Chat view (owner, 2026-10-15,
  translated: "a light, slight highlight of the chat input field, one that
  doesn't draw much attention"; off or stronger with the terminal's band,
  § Where you type) — with **Photo** and **Send** under
  it, and Esc, Tab, ↑, ↓, ⏎ and Ctrl+C as 44px buttons (two rows of three
  below `sm`, where "Ctrl+C" filled its button in a row of six), because a
  phone keyboard has none of them and Claude Code's menus are answered with
  them. Send types the text, a line break inside it as
  ESC CR, then Enter 80 ms later — in one write a TUI reads the pair as a paste
  and keeps the Enter as a new line. It is always there on a phone and on any
  window narrower than `sm`; the terminal is 52vh there so the bar stays in
  view. The phone does not focus the canvas, which would raise the on-screen
  keyboard over the output. **Offline** (the terminal not live), Send holds
  the message in a list above the field — *Will send when connected*, with
  **Edit** — and sends it on reconnect if no permission prompt waits;
  otherwise it stays as *Not sent — a permission prompt is waiting* (or *the
  session ended*) with **Send now**. The six keys, and Send on an empty
  field, are refused while not live, with a notice that keys are never
  queued ([21-app.md § Phase A](21-app.md#phase-a--resilience-and-starting-work-p0)).
  On the Chat tab a held message goes to the daemon's queue on reconnect
  instead, and a message sent while the agent works waits there as a
  bubble (*Sends when Claude finishes*) until the turn ends (§ Session Detail, Chat;
  [ADR-053](08-decisions.md)). `/` and `@` open the command and file
  pickers above the field, in both places.
- **Photo** in the keys bar opens `<input type=file accept="image/*" multiple>`
  — no `capture`, which would make iOS open the camera only; without it the
  picker offers the camera and the library both. A photo larger than 2048 px
  on its long side or 1.5 MB is drawn at 2048 px and re-encoded as JPEG 0.85
  (`lib/downscale.ts`; a 12 MP photo is 3–6 MB and the paste limit is 10 MB),
  then goes the way a dropped file does: `POST /v1/paste`, and its quoted path
  pasted into the session, where Claude Code shows it as `[Image #N]`
  (§ Paste or drop files). In the chat (desktop and phone), which has no
  terminal to paste into, the photo is an **Image N chip** over the field —
  a thumbnail, *Image 1*, *Image 2*, and an × that takes one out — never its
  path in the text, which stays the person's own words. On Send the photos
  go first, `POST /v1/agents/{id}/input {paths}`, where the daemon writes the
  same bytes `pathInput` does (one bracketed paste per image when the session
  asked for paste, 150 ms after each), and then the words through the queue.
  Photos with no words are followed by Enter. A message held offline keeps its
  photos (shown as `[Image 1] …`; **Edit** puts them back as chips, without
  the thumbnail).
- **Permission prompt buttons** (`PermissionPrompt`, ADR-035). When an owned
  Claude Code session waits on a permission prompt, a card names the tool and
  shows its command or file, with **Yes**, the second option when Claude Code
  offers one (its label: *Yes, and don't ask again*, *Yes, allow all edits
  this session*, *Yes, and allow this folder*), and **No**, each 48px. Under
  the terminal on the Terminal tab, and above the tabs on every other one,
  because a phone opens on Timeline. A viewer sees the question without
  buttons. It goes away when anything answers the prompt, the terminal
  included. Each button names its key — **Y**, **A**, **N** — and beside them
  *Keys work from the terminal · ↵ Yes · Esc No*: the card and the terminal's
  own menu are one question, not two. Y, A and N answer from the asking
  session's terminal (marked `data-term-session`) and are kept from it;
  Enter and Esc there go to Claude Code's menu. It never takes focus. Above
  the tabs on the session screen — no terminal of the session on the page,
  which is where a phone answers — the keys and the line are left out
  (`keys={false}`).
  - *A prompt settled out of sight.* A request that timed out, or one a
    check denied without a menu, sends no hook that clears it, and the card
    stayed until the turn ended. A button answered on a screen with no menu
    now drops the prompt (409, the card goes), and **×** hides it, typing
    nothing (`choice: "dismiss"`).
  - *Queued prompts.* Claude Code shows one dialog at a time and queues the
    rest; the card shows the oldest — the one on screen — and *· N more
    waiting* when others are queued behind it.
  - *Refused answers.* The daemon reads the menu on the session's screen
    before it types and picks the option by its text; when the option is not
    there (auto mode's dialog has only *Yes* and *No*), it types nothing
    and answers 422. The card stays, with *that option is not
    on the prompt — answer in the terminal* under the buttons.
  - *Keys.* `Y` or Enter is Yes, `A` the second option (only when offered),
    `N` or Esc is No, drawn on the buttons (*Yes ↵*, *… A*, *No Esc*; the
    hint is not part of the button's name). Never while focus is in the
    terminal, a field or an editor — on the Terminal tab, where the card sits
    under the terminal, the terminal keeps its keys. Enter and Esc only with
    focus on the page or in the card; none while another dialog is open, from
    a hidden card, or from any but the newest card on the page.
- **Ask the agent from the diff.** On the Changes tab of a live session
  Caprock started, a controller phone can tap a line: a bar at the bottom
  offers **Ask the agent**, which opens the Terminal tab with the keys bar's
  field holding "In <file> around line N: " and focused (`lib/draft.ts`).
  Nothing is sent until Send. A viewer's lines are not tappable, and the
  machine itself types into the terminal directly, so it is not offered there.
- **Commit from the session** (`SessionCommit.tsx`). The Changes tab opens
  with an *Uncommitted* panel for the session's worktree: the project whose
  root holds the session (longest wins) and git's name for its worktree
  (`worktreeKeyOf`), never a guess — a worktree the project does not list
  says to commit on the machine, and a repository not listed offers *Add as
  a project*. Rows are 44 px: tap to stage or unstage (a tick shows staged;
  nothing ticked commits everything), then the shared `CommitBox` (message,
  *Use the agent's summary*, Commit, Commit & Push) and `RemoteActions`
  (Fetch, Pull, Push). A viewer sees the list and a line saying where
  committing happens. The branch-against-trunk diff below is unchanged.
  Below it, the worktree's pull request (`PullRequestCard`, WP-19): the
  same state as the desktop strip, readable by a viewer; a controller also
  gets *Open a pull request* (the same form, 44 px fields). The clone mode
  of *Start work* shows the repository picker once GitHub is connected on
  the computer.
- **Start work** (WP-15, `#/start`, `components/NewProject.tsx`). A
  controller phone gets a thumb-sized *+ Start work · clone, new project,
  worktree* row on Now. The page has three modes — **Clone** (an `https://`
  or `git@host:path` address and *Clone into*, a folder under home),
  **New project** and **Worktree** — then **Start an agent here**, which opens
  the spawn dialog and lands on the session's Chat. A clone in progress is
  kept in localStorage with its `op_id` and followed again after a drop or a
  reload (`lib/startwork.ts`), never sent twice. A viewer is not offered it,
  and the daemon refuses it.
- **Control taken away mid-session** closes the socket with 1008 and the
  terminal says so instead of reconnecting.
- **Where it is set up** (`components/Pairing.tsx`): Settings, right under
  Appearance in the app. One sentence that says whether a phone is
  connected, one large *Connect a phone* button with dark text on amber — it
  turns on access from anywhere, the one way in since 2026-10-14 — and the
  rest folded under *How it works* (§ Use on your phone, rebuilt plain on
  2026-10-18). The palette's *Open Caprock on your phone* opens Settings
  scrolled to it (`openPhoneSettings`, section id `settings-phone`).
- **On the home screen** (`ui/public/manifest.json`, the `apple-*` tags in
  `ui/index.html`): standalone, the favicon's mark as the icon
  (`ui/icons/*.svg` → `scripts/make-icons.sh`), `theme-color` = the header's
  `--color-panel` of whichever of the seven palettes is on (`panel` in
  `lib/themes.ts`), kept in step by `lib/theme.ts`.
  `viewport-fit=cover` with the status bar `black-translucent`: the sticky
  header pads itself by `safe-area-inset-top`, so its colour runs under the
  clock, and `body` pads the left, right and bottom insets. iOS gives a
  home-screen app its own storage, so it starts unpaired; the pair screen
  detects standalone (`display-mode: standalone` / `navigator.standalone`),
  asks for the typed code — a scanned QR opens Safari, not the app — and names
  the device "iPhone · home screen". WebKit counts a home-screen app's days of
  use apart from Safari's for its seven-day storage cap, so the token lasts
  while the app is used.
  **No service worker:** figures must never be served stale, and a LAN or
  Tailscale `http://` origin is not a secure context, where none can register.

## Chats

A chat is a session Caprock started in its own chat folder, for asking
something rather than working on a repository ([ADR-071](08-decisions.md);
the daemon side is [03-contracts.md § Chats](03-contracts.md#chats-http)).
It is read as a conversation and never as a terminal, and it is listed apart
from the work: not on Now, the phone's Continue or the sidebar (there only
while it has a tab, a permission prompt or waits on you).

- **One tap to start.** *New chat* on the Chats screen, the phone home's
  dock, ⌥⌘N and *Quick chat* in the app, and the Now screen's Quick chat
  button all start a chat at once on the remembered model and open it. The
  **model chip** beside them (`components/ChatStart.tsx`, *Model: Claude ·
  Opus 5.5. Change*) opens *Model for new chats*: the vendors as a radio
  row, that vendor's models as a list. The choice is kept per viewer in
  `caprock.quickchat`, shared with the app's chooser; until one is picked it
  is the first agent ready and its default model.
- **The list** (`#/chats`, `screens/Chats.tsx`) — *Chats* with the count,
  newest activity first, each row the chat's title (its name, else its first
  message, at most 80 characters), the model and, while it is doing
  something, *answering…* or *waiting for you*, and its age. A search field
  (debounced 250 ms, `GET /v1/chats?q=`). *Archived · n* opens
  `#/chats?archived=1`, which has *← All chats*. Each row's ⋯ holds
  *End session…* for a live chat (or the one line saying why it cannot be
  ended here; § From a phone, *End from a list*), *Rename*, *Archive* (or
  *Move back to Chats*) and *Delete…*, which asks again as *Delete for good
  — and its files*. An ended chat's row says *ended* after the model and
  stays in the list. On a phone *New chat* and the
  model chip sit in a bar fixed at the bottom, a messenger's place for them;
  on a desktop they head the list. The desktop's nav has *Chats* after Work;
  the phone bar's ⋯ has it first. Empty: *No chats yet.* / *No archived
  chats.*
- **One chat** (`#/chats/<id>`, `screens/Chat.tsx`) — a bar with ← (*All
  chats*), the title (tap to rename), the model and its state, *Files · n*
  and ⋯ (*End this chat…* first while it is live — or why not — then
  rename, archive, delete with the same confirm), then ChatView with
  its field at the bottom. A permission prompt is answered above the
  conversation. The field says *Message…*, not the terminal's *Type · /
  commands · @ files*, and the quick replies leave out the two defaults that
  act on a repository, *Run tests* and *Commit* (`forChat`, by the default's
  text, so a reply the user wrote stays); the raw keys stay folded behind
  *Keys*, for the agent's own menus. An ended chat says its conversation and
  files are kept and offers *Continue this chat*, which resumes it in its own folder (`resume`,
  no fork). A file the reply names opens in the viewer.
- **Files** (`components/ChatFiles.tsx`) — *Files · n* lists what the chat's
  folder holds, newest first, with kind, size and age. A file opens in a
  full-screen viewer named after it: Markdown rendered by the reader the
  dashboard uses for any `.md`; HTML in an iframe with `sandbox=""` (no
  scripts, no same origin) and a CSP `<meta>` in its `srcdoc`; PDF in the
  browser's own viewer, with *Open in the browser's viewer*; images shown;
  text as text. **Download** saves any of them, on a phone too; every read
  carries the device token (`X-Caprock-Device`), so it works paired.
- **Imported chats** ([ADR-074](08-decisions.md)). On the machine (not on
  a paired device) the list has *Import from Claude.ai or ChatGPT…* under
  its head, and *How do I get the file?* beside it, which unfolds three
  lines: where each service's *Export data* is (Claude.ai: Settings →
  Privacy; ChatGPT: Settings → Data controls; the link arrives by email),
  and that the .zip or the conversations.json in it is read on this
  computer, nothing is sent, and a newer export adds only what is new. The
  button opens the file picker (`.zip`, `.json`), then says *Importing…*;
  the result is one line, *Read 12 conversations from ChatGPT: 3 new, 1
  with new messages, 8 already here.* (`importSummary`), or the error. An
  imported chat is a row like any other with the service's mark (Claude's
  spark for Claude.ai; OpenAI's Blossom for ChatGPT) and name
  (*ChatGPT · <model>*), searchable, renamable, archivable and deletable.
  Opened, it has no *Files*, no field, and says *Imported from ChatGPT. A
  copy to read and search here; carry the conversation on in ChatGPT
  itself.*; its delete reads *Delete this copy (it stays in ChatGPT)*. A
  phone reads them as it reads any chat.
- **In the app** a chat tab opens on ChatView (its hint line and the ended
  overlay come with it); the Terminal toggle still shows the terminal. The
  palette has *Chats*, which opens the list.
- **Who.** A paired viewer reads chats and their files; a controller also
  starts, continues, ends, renames, archives and deletes them. A viewer's
  ⋯ on a live chat holds only the line that says where to allow control.

## The app workspace

The terminal-first layout the desktop app opens on (WP-04, WP-06, WP-11 of
[22-app-plan.md](22-app-plan.md); the spec is [21-app.md § What the user
sees](21-app.md#what-the-user-sees)). It is on at `#/app`, with `?app=1` on any
URL, or inside the Tauri shell (`window.__TAURI_INTERNALS__`); `APP_ROUTE` and
the detection are in `ui/src/lib/appmode.ts`. Once on, it stays on for the tab,
so the dashboard's own links (`#/cost`, `#/session/…`) open inside it. The
browser dashboard and the phone are untouched: `main.tsx` loads either the
dashboard or the app as its own chunk, and every app style is scoped under
`<html data-app>`.

- **New project, tap-tap-tap** (owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "I still
  don't understand how to make a multi-repo project … I want big: quickly
  click through and the project is created with everything"). ⌘O, *Add
  project*, the empty page's *Add a project* and *New project…* in New agent
  open a three-step wizard (`ProjectWizard.tsx`, rules in
  `lib/projectWizard.ts`) in big type; the full spec is in
  [21-app.md § Projects](21-app.md). In its folder field a half-typed name
  (`~/dev/cap`) searches the folder before it: matches first by prefix, then
  anywhere in the name, marked; ↑↓ move, Enter picks, Tab goes inside
  (owner, 2026-10-15). *Put several repositories in one project* on its
  first step turns the rows into checkboxes, kept across folders, with *2
  selected · Continue* (owner, 2026-10-18, translated: "I still can't
  understand how to make multi-repo projects"; [ADR-077](08-decisions.md)):
  one project of them with no folder of its own, the first ticked its home
  repository, named on the next step. The Add project sheet (clone, a new
  empty folder) is one link away in its footer, and is what opens without
  the projects API; *From a template…* sits beside it (not on a paired
  device).
- **Add a repository…** in a project's menu (sidebar ⋯, right-click,
  Shift+F10), hinted *several in one* or *N in it*, and *Add…* on a
  multi-repo project's page: `AddRepoSheet.tsx`, the wizard's folder list
  with checkboxes, sending `PATCH repos` (ADR-077; [21-app.md §
  Projects](21-app.md)).
- **New project from a template** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "I have a
  template repo, Corpus. I create a repo from it, feed it to an agent with a
  brief, and it builds the project — docs first. Build it into Caprock so I
  use it and it doesn't get in other people's way."; [ADR-065](08-decisions.md),
  contract in [03-contracts.md § Project templates](03-contracts.md)).
  `TemplateProjectSheet.tsx`, rules in `lib/templates.ts`, the job in
  `lib/useTemplateJob.ts`. Opened from the wizard's footer or ⌘K *New
