  project from a template…*; no shortcut of its own. Three steps in the
  wizard's look (`StepsHeader`):
  - **Template.** Saved templates as cards (name, `github.com/o/n` or the
    folder, *brief → path*). With none: "No templates yet. A template is a
    GitHub template repository or a folder on this computer. Caprock copies
    it into a new project, puts your brief in it and starts an agent there."
    and the add form open. *+ Another template*: *GitHub template* (owner/name,
    a github.com URL or a `git@` address, read as `owner/name` in the page)
    or *A folder* (*Choose…* in the app); *Look up* runs inspect on that
    click, with the line under it "Reads TEMPLATE.md and README.md from
    github.com/o/n with your gh login." It answers *Template repository ·
    public · has TEMPLATE.md*, *o/n is not a template repository. On GitHub:
    Settings → Template repository.* or *Not found, or your gh login can't
    see it.*, then fills *Brief goes to* (with where it came from: TEMPLATE.md,
    named in it or the README, or a guess), *First message to the agent*
    (`{brief}` is the path) and *Save as a template: [name]*, ticked; the
    template is saved (`POST /v1/templates`) only once the project is made.
    The agent picker is under the chosen template.
  - **Project** ("Name it"). The name (from `suggested_name`, or `new-<name>`
    numbered past the parent's folders), the parent from
    `settings.default_folder` with place chips, the path, *Private*
    (default) or *Public* on the GitHub path, and one line on how it will be
    made from `GHStatus`: on GitHub as `login/name`, then cloned; gh missing
    with a local copy known (the template's folder, or a clone in the
    default folder from inspect, which runs no gh then) — made locally,
    "Install gh and run `gh auth login` to have one made."; gh signed out —
    *Check again*, or *Make it locally* when a folder is known, never on its
    own; neither — an error. A taken name or one the daemon would refuse is
    said under the field (`GET /v1/browse/stat`).
  - **Brief.** A big field, "Paste it, or drop a .md or .txt file here." — an
    HTML5 drop read with FileReader in a browser; in the app the window's
    native drop arrives as `caprock:drop-paths` and the shell reads the file
    (`read_dropped_text`, [21-app.md § Dropping a file](21-app.md)). *When
    you press Create* is always in sight: on GitHub `gh repo create
    login/name --template o/n --private` and what it does, the clone, "Writes
    your brief to <path> and commits it. Not pushed.", "Starts <agent> there
    with: "<prompt>"", then "Sent to GitHub: the name and visibility. Your
    brief stays on this computer until you push."; locally the copy, `git
    init` and a commit, the brief, the agent, and "Nothing leaves this
    computer." (rule 4). *Create* (⌘↩, through `keys()`) waits for a brief.
  - **After Create** the same plan is ticked off and the footer follows the
    op frames ("Creating the repository on GitHub…", "Cloning… 42%",
    "Writing the brief…", "Starting Claude Code…"). Esc hides the sheet; the
    app keeps the job, reads a missed frame back from `GET
    /v1/projects/ops` every 2 s while it runs, and on `done` selects the
    project, starts the agent (`POST /v1/agents` with the prompt, `{brief}`
    replaced), opens its tab and toasts "name is ready — github.com/login/name"
    with the daemon's notes. A refused create says "Nothing was created."; a
    clone that failed after the repository was made says it is on GitHub,
    that the folder was removed, offers *Try the clone again* (`POST
    …/ops/{id}/retry`) and *Open on GitHub*, and "Caprock never deletes a
    GitHub repository; delete it there if you don't want it."; an agent that
    did not start offers *Start an agent*, New agent prefilled. A failure
    while the sheet is hidden is a toast. The clone progress of Add project
    and Start work finds its own operation by `op_id`, so a `template` op
    never shows there.
  - **Settings → Project templates** (`TemplatesSetting.tsx`, beside Saved
    prompts and commands; the machine only): the list, *Edit* (sends only
    what changed, `""` to clear) and *Delete* (nothing on disk or GitHub
    touched).
- **Subagents run on** (`lib/subagentModel.ts`; New agent under Permissions,
  Claude Code only; the phone's *Start in a project* sheet as chips): *Same as
  the session* (default, sends nothing), *Haiku*, *Sonnet* or *Opus*, sent as
  `subagent_model` (§ 03-contracts, Subagent model). The last choice is kept
  per project in this browser (the project id on the desktop, its folder on
  the phone; the phone's dock start reads it back). The session's header says
  *subagents: Haiku* when one was chosen. Beside the Week card, a note says
  *Subagents this week cost $X on Opus 5.5; on Haiku 5.5 about $Y (estimate at
  list prices …)* only when the daemon sends `subagent_saving` (a difference of
  a dollar or more, subagents on a pricier model).
- **What a project's agents are told** (`ProjectInstructions.tsx`): two
  rows, *Project instructions* (Caprock's, "Set · 12 lines" or "None") and
  *Repository's own* (`CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, members' too), each a click
  from being read; in New agent under Permissions and on the project's page.
  The project row's tooltip ends with the same state in words
  (`instructionsSummary`). New agent asks *Repository* under Project in a
  multi-repo project: the home repository (*ai-crm · home*) or a folder
  project's folder first, then each other one (ADR-077; it named the members
  in a line, *3 repos: api, web, infra*, until 2026-10-18). In such a project
  each session row in the sidebar names its repository before its branch.
- **Sidebar** (`Sidebar.tsx`, `ProjectRow.tsx`, `SidebarFooter.tsx`, model in `lib/sidebar.ts`).
  Under the list and above the footer, the Second Brain card (§ Second Brain).
  The Today strip (`TodayStrip.tsx`, `lib/today.ts`): today's spend, agents
  running and waiting, and the Claude 5-hour and weekly windows as thin
  bars — spend opens Cost, a window Cost's plan limits, *Running* Now,
  *Waiting* the first session waiting on you (the model's `inbox`, which
  the tray, ⌘J and the palette also read). Three states, per viewer: the
  full card (the default, for a viewer who never chose); one line, by its
  chevron — *$33.79 · 11 running · 2 waiting · 5h 6% · 7d 74%*, the waiting
  part only when something waits and amber, each window coloured as its bar
  would be; a click on the line unfolds it — and hidden, by the × that
  shows on hover (always on a touch screen) on the card and on the line.
  Its menu (right-click, Shift+F10) offers *Full card*, *One line* and
  *Hide*. Settings → Appearance → *Today card in the sidebar* brings it
  back, and while it is hidden the list's heading carries an amber count of
  what waits, which opens the first (a user, 2026-10-15, translated: "can
  this be hidden? it's in the way"; `lib/todayCard.ts`,
  `caprock.app.today-fold` and `caprock.app.today-shown`). It has no Share
  button (owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "remove the Share button there"):
  sharing stays in ⌘K and on Pipeline. **There is no *Waiting on you*
  block** (owner, 2026-10-11: "not needed at all — everything is already
  visible in Projects", translated): what waits is marked on its own row
  under its project instead — an amber dot with a halo (`bg-warn`,
  `.app-dot-waiting`) and the state word *waiting* in bold amber, or *asks*
  at a permission prompt (from `permission` frames, so the mark lands within
  a second); the project row keeps its amber count. No fill and no edge: a
  waiting row once had both, as the tab in front does, and a user could not
  tell which tab she was in (2026-10-15, translated: "which tab am I
  working in?"). Attention is the dot and the word; selection is the fill.
  `SessionNode.waiting` in `lib/sidebar.ts` is true for a permission prompt
  or a turn that ended in the last 12 h, the same rule as the project's
  count; older turns are not marked and stay on the Dashboard. A project
  folded by hand still lists what waits in it (and nothing else). Then the projects as an
  accordion (owner, 2026-10-09: "which one is active, which to pick,
  unclear … a simpler interface", translated). A project row is its name, a
  green dot with `live` (sessions and shells running in it) and the waiting
  badge, nothing else. Every project is open unless folded by hand
  with its chevron, and only a project with rows has a chevron. **Its rows
  are every agent session in it** (owner, 2026-10-13, translated: "when
  will this *+1 more* go away … show only the agent sessions, don't move
  anything, don't add extra elements"; `agentRows` in `ProjectRow.tsx`):
  working, waiting or idle, with no *+N more* line, no *Fold N back* and no
  peek of a quiet project. Shells and file tabs have no rows (the strip
  holds them), an ended session drops off, and a row hidden by hand is
  gone until *More…* (below) shows it. Earlier the same day (FB-053:
  "way too much stuff, I want only active sessions there") an open project
  listed only its active rows and folded the rest behind *+N more*, which
  hid the idle rows the owner steers by; before that it was one row per
  project (`pickLead`, 2026-10-12). Rows are 38px (`h-[38px]`), the title
  14px medium in the text colour (a user, 2026-10-15: the 28px rows at
  12.5px could not be read), with no agent mark — the agent is in the
  tooltip (`agentGlyphLabel`). Each says its state as a dot (`RowDot`, 8px)
  and a word: working green, the dot breathing (`.app-dot-working`, still
  under reduced motion); waiting amber; idle faint. The word gives way to
  the title on a row narrower than 300px for *working* and 220px for
  *idle*, and on the tab in front (container queries on the row); *waiting*,
  *asks* and *looping* always say it, and the dot always stays. A tab's key
  is the footer's readable chip (`.app-kbd-key`). A tab row
  is labelled by `tabLabels` (`lib/tablabels.ts`), the function the strip
  uses too: an agent by `sessionTitle`, a muted branch only when it is not
  the project's `branch`. The title wins over the branch
  (`TitleAndBranch`; owner, 2026-10-10): the branch truncates to nothing
  first, and the row's tooltip carries both in full. Muted
  (`data-live-row`) come the project's sessions that Caprock started
  (`owned`) and that are live but in no tab (`SessionNode.open` false),
  and the live sessions another terminal started (not `owned`, such as a
  "Context recovery" run by hand); all rows share the one fixed order
  below. Such a session, quiet for days, reached the sidebar only through
  the live list since 2026-10-09. A
  session like that, started days ago and quiet since, reaches
  the sidebar only through the live list (`/v1/sessions?active=true`): the
  full list is the 200 most recently active, and a live frame patches only
  a session already held. `useWorkspaceData` asks for the live list at
  mount and on the debounced tick, one request at a time, and always
  applies the answer — it used to wait for the first tick and drop any
  answer a newer tick overtook, so beside a busy agent (a tick a second) an
  answer slower than a second never landed and "Context recovery" was
  missing from the fold (2026-10-10). A click opens a row through `openSession`, a tab for one Caprock
  owns and its details page otherwise (rule 7). **Rows keep their places**
  (owner, 2026-10-13, translated: "when I pick a session in a project from
  the list, it moves up … fixed positions, I orient visually by them";
  `lib/rowOrder.ts`): a row is keyed by the session it shows (a tab by
  `tabRowKey`, its naming leaf), so it keeps its place when it is opened as
  a tab, and it is ordered by the order set by hand, then `started_at`,
  then the key — never by the strip, the front, a state or an activity
  time. Until then tabs came first in strip order, so a muted row picked
  from the list jumped above the others when it became a tab. A status
  update never moves a row, a new session lands last, and an ended one
  leaves no gap. The order is set by hand by dragging a row onto
  another (pointer events, as the strip's tabs, since the desktop shell
  takes HTML5 drops; the click that ends a drag opens nothing) or with
  ⌥⇧↑ ⌥⇧↓ (Ctrl+Shift off macOS, where Alt+Shift arrows are the app's
  chords) on a focused row, which keeps its focus (in a terminal they do
  nothing, never reaching the session as Down);
  it is kept per project in `caprock.app.row-order` (localStorage, this
  viewer's; the flat list's order under `*`) and survives a restart.
  **Flat or Tree** (owner, 2026-10-13, translated: "top I see 1, 2, 3; on
  the left I see 4, 2 … group by projects or not"): Settings → Appearance
  → *Sidebar* or the switch beside the heading, both worded **Projects**
  (Tree) | **Tabs** (Flat) (`ListSwitch`, `data-group-switch`, a
  radiogroup "List sessions by"; `lib/sidebarMode.ts`,
  `caprock.app.sidebar-grouped` 'on' = Tree, 'off' = Flat, said on window
  so both agree at once). The switch was a bare 13px folder icon until
  2026-10-14 (owner, translated: "make a switch with words, like this way
  or that way"). **Who gets which.** 0.116.0 read an absent key as Flat
  and so moved every viewer who had never touched the toggle off Tree; a
  user wrote (translated): "a project folder with its worktrees inside is
  more convenient; I can't quickly switch between projects". Now an absent
  key is resolved once and written down: Tree when this browser already
  holds any key the app writes only after use (`EXISTING_VIEWER_KEYS`: the
  tabs `caprock.app.workspace.v1` and prefs `caprock.app.ui`, both since
  2026-10-05, hidden projects and rows, row order, terminal settings,
  folds, a chosen theme), Flat for a browser with none of them. Written at
  the first read, so the tabs the app keeps a moment later never flip it.
  **Flat** is one
  list headed *Sessions* (`flatRows`): the rows with a tab in the strip's
  order, each with its tab's ⌘N and its project's short name in the
  project's colour (`data-row-project-mark`; no status dot and no project
  dot since 2026-10-14, as on the tabs — the row's state word stays), then
  *Recently closed* (below), then the sessions running with
  no tab, under a quiet *Running, no tab* line, without a digit, in their
  own kept order (`FLAT_ORDER`). **Running, no tab keeps only what the
  user started himself** (owner, 2026-10-20): titled, active in the last
  day. The rest folds into one closed row at the bottom, *Background · N*
  with a quiet *scouts, ops, helpers* (`data-background-toggle`, an amber dot
  when one in it waits on you); a click opens it and shows those rows as
  before, and open or closed is kept per browser (`FOLDS_KEY`, `background`).
  Background is one pure rule, `isBackgroundSession` in `lib/sidebar.ts`:
  never a session asking a permission; else one in a temporary folder
  (`inTempDir`: /var/folders and /private/var/folders, macOS's $TMPDIR, /tmp,
  /var/tmp, Windows' AppData\Local\Temp) that no listed project holds — the
  daemon never lists a temp folder by itself (03-contracts.md § Seeding), so
  one listed was added by hand — one another tool started with a
  scripted brief (not started from Caprock, no name, named only by a first
  prompt over 400 characters or of four lines or more), one with no title at
  all 15 minutes after it started, and one not working that nothing happened
  in for 24 hours. The tabbed rows, the running ones and the background ones
  are three views (`visible`) of the one flat order, so a row moved in any
  of them keeps it whole. Tree is unchanged: under a project every agent
  session stays a row.
  **Recently closed** (`components/RecentlyClosed.tsx`,
  `lib/recentlyClosed.ts`; owner, 2026-10-20): the last tabs closed, newest
  first, at most five, each its title and a quiet age (*2 min*, *1 h*), in
  Flat between the tabbed rows and *Running, no tab*, in Tree after the
  projects; its last row, *Show all in History*, opens `#/history`. Hidden
  when empty. Only agent conversations go on it — a session's or a watched
  session's pane, never a shell (it ended with its tab), a file or a browser
  tab — when a tab or pane closes by its ×, ⌘W or the strip
  (`noteClosed` in AppShell's `finishClose`, and a watched session's own ×).
  Kept in localStorage (`caprock.app.recently-closed`, the newest 20) so it
  survives a reload and a restart; a session with a tab again is left out,
  and an entry is dropped once reopened. A closed tab whose process still
  runs is listed here only, not again under *Running, no tab*, unless it
  waits on the user, when its row there says so. A click, or **⇧⌘T** for the newest
  (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+T off macOS, as in a browser; *Reopen closed tab* in the
  palette), brings it back (`reopenPlan`): a session whose process still
  runs gets its tab again, as a click on its row does (its terminal, or its
  conversation read-only when another terminal started it, rule 7); an
  ended one is continued with `--resume` under its own id, in the
  permission mode it last ran in, as *Continue here* does; one that cannot
  be continued opens read-only with its reason; one the list no longer
  carries and whose folder is unknown opens its session page. Nothing is
  started on a guess: when the list does not say it can be continued (it
  no longer carries it, or carries it without its `resume` answer yet, as
  in the poll right after it ended), its own page (`GET
  /v1/sessions/{id}`) is asked first and the plan made from that, so a
  session with no transcript opens read-only rather than a start the
  daemon refuses with a 400 (Linux CI, 2026-10-21). A continue refused
  even so opens it read-only and says why in a toast. ⇧⌘T with nothing
  closed says *No closed tab to reopen.*
  Dragging a tabbed row there (or ⌥⇧↑ ⌥⇧↓) moves its tab in the strip
  (`onOrderTabs` → the `order` action, the moved tabs taking the places
  the same tabs held, so a shell or file tab keeps its own). **Tree** is
  projects with their sessions under them, in each project's kept order;
  each row still shows its tab's ⌘N, so the numbers are not sequential
  down the tree. **Tabs are the navigation** (owner, 2026-10-14): ⌘1–⌘9
  is the strip's Nth tab and the strip is in the order the tabs were
  opened — not the sidebar's. From 2026-10-13 to 2026-10-14 the strip
  followed the sidebar and ⌘N was the sidebar's Nth top-level item (a
  project when grouped, a session when flat), with N drawn only on the
  tab it brought; the owner could not tell which key reached which
  session ("not every tab shows the key combination", translated), and a
  first fix that gave projects ⌃⌘1–⌃⌘9 was turned down the same day:
  projects have no digit. A click on a project row opens the project's
  page (the `project` action with `view`), its tabs left in the strip. Projects were already stable (pinned,
  the hand-set `sort`, name). Each row ends in a state
  word from its `Dot` — *working* (`text-ok`), *waiting* (`text-accent`,
  the only amber in the list: it needs you), *looping*, *idle*, *done*. **Closing is one sign, ×**
  (owner, 2026-10-09: "unclear how to close projects on the left, or their
  parts", translated). A tab row's × — always on the tab in front, on
  hover or focus elsewhere, and always on a touch screen (`pointer-coarse`)
  — calls `onCloseTab`, the very callback the
  strip's × uses (the `close` workspace action; ⌘W is the same action
  for an unsplit tab): the tab goes, and an agent keeps running and
  reappears as a muted row — unless Caprock started it and the viewer
  chooses to end it (below, *Closing an agent's tab*). **Every nested row can be got rid of** (owner,
  2026-10-11: "in the project list with nested items nothing can be done —
  if I opened something else inside the Caprock project, I can't close it
  or hide it afterwards", translated). Until then a muted row offered only
  ■ for what Caprock started and nothing at all for a session from another
  terminal, and a tab row's × was a 12px glyph in a 22px target. Now a row
  with no tab has a × that **hides** it from the list (*Hide … from the
  list*): nothing is signalled, so it is the one action offered on a
  session another terminal started (rule 7), and on what Caprock started it
  sits beside ■. Hidden rows are listed in *More…* (below), where a click shows
  one again; they had a *N hidden · show* line of their own and then a
  place inside *+N more* until 2026-10-13, which the owner counted as
  clutter. A hidden row stays hidden
  while it works. Ids and when they were hidden live in this browser's
  storage (`caprock.app.hidden-sessions`, the newest 300). A hidden row
  comes back by itself when it next waits on you — a permission prompt
  always, a turn that ended after it was hidden (`rowHidden`,
  `lib/sidebar.ts`). Closing an agent's tab never stops the agent (a quick chat is the one exception: [21-app.md](21-app.md), *Closing a quick chat ends it*): stopping
  is only ever ■ or *Stop the session…*, behind the cockpit's
  confirmation; the tab's menu has *Close tab and hide from the list* for
  one that should leave the sidebar in one step. Row buttons are 26px, rows
  30px, the project row 32px; the room they need is padded on hover, focus
  and touch so they never sit on the title. **A shell's tab closes the shell** (owner,
  2026-10-09: "close means really close", translated): `requestClose` in
  `AppShell.tsx` reads `GET /v1/shells` at that moment and `planClose`
  (`lib/closeShell.ts`) splits the closing panes' shells into idle ones,
  killed with the tab through the same `signal` kill as *Stop the shell…*,
  and busy ones, which open `CloseShellConfirm.tsx` — *Shell 1 is running
  claude. Close and stop it?* with *Stop and close*, *Keep running, close
  tab* and *Cancel*. Only shells on the daemon's list are signalled (rule
  7); a pane close (⌘W in a split, a pane's ×) follows the same plan. The
  tooltips say it: *Close shell (⌘W)* on a shell, *Close tab (⌘W) — keep
  the agent running or end it* on an agent Caprock started, *— the agent
  keeps running* on one another terminal started. **Closing an agent's
  tab** (owner, 2026-10-14, after a user wrote, translated: "chats don't
  disappear when I close them"): the closed tabs' agents ran on, idle, and
  read as open chats. Nothing ends or hides by itself; instead
  `closeAgents` (AppShell) runs after the quick chats' half and before the
  shells': `planAgentClose` (`lib/closeShell.ts`) lists the agents in the
  closing tab or pane that Caprock started, still running, not shells and
  not quick chats, and which of them are answering. None → the tab closes
  as before. Otherwise `lib/agentClose.ts` (`caprock.app.agent-close`, per
  viewer: `ask` by default, `keep`, `end`) decides. **One decision at
  most** since 2026-10-15 (owner, translated: "I never understand whether
  data is kept and which button to pick. It must be very simple — one
  button"): under *ask* an agent that is idle (none in the plan's `active`:
  working, looping, waiting on you, or a permission prompt open) ends with
  its tab, no question, and a toast says *Closed. The conversation is
  saved — reopen it from History or the sidebar.* (`AGENT_SAVED_TOAST`).
  One that is working or waiting opens `CloseAgentChoice`: one short line
  (*“title” is still working.*, `agentStillWorking`) and two buttons side
  by side (stacked below `sm`), each with a few words under it
  (`agentCloseChoices`; owner, 2026-10-17, translated: one button "may be
  the wrong choice", then: "a lot of text, and it's unclear which button is
  recommended"): **Keep it running** — *It works on in the background.* —
  the recommended one, drawn filled in the accent and in focus (⌘W then
  Enter; an idle agent beside it in a split still ends), and **Stop it**,
  outlined — *Continue later from History.* (*them* for several). Each line
  is its button's accessible
  description; Escape cancels; no checkbox. Settings
  → *Closing a tab* (`AgentCloseSetting`, app only: *Ask while it works*,
  *Keep it running*, *End the agent*) is for always keeping or always
  stopping; *End the agent* still asks once more about one answering
  (`CloseChatConfirm` with `agent`). Ending — and a quick chat's end — sends
  `signal` **`end`**, not `kill`: SIGTERM first (resumed if paused), so
  Claude Code writes its transcript and the session stays continuable with
  `--resume`, and SIGKILL only if it still runs after 5 s
  (`agents.Manager.End`). An agent another terminal started is never in the plan,
  so never asked about or signalled (rule 7). **Running, no tab**: in the
  Flat list a quiet heading (`data-untabbed-heading`, `SessionList`'s
  `untabbedHeading`) sits where the tabbed rows end — after *Recently
  closed* — so a closed tab's agent reads as running in the background, not
  as an open chat; with only background sessions left it heads their
  *Background · N* row. Tree has
  none: under a project the tabbed and untabbed rows share one kept order.
  **A quick chat with no tab** (owner, 2026-10-20: a chat whose tab was
  lost sat under *Running, no tab* for as long as its process ran, beside a
  newer one, both called *Quick chat · …*) is listed only while it works,
  while it asks for something (a permission prompt or a question: not at
  its prompt, `atPrompt`), and for 10 minutes after its last answer
  (`chatListed` in `lib/sidebar.ts`, `CHAT_LINGER_MS` in
  `lib/quickchat.ts`) — every chat waits on you once it has answered, so
  *waiting on you* alone kept it forever. Past that it is on the Chats screen
  and in the palette. Its row says what it is about (its title, else a few
  words of its first prompt, else *Quick chat · <model>*), then its start
  time (*09:40* today, else *19 Oct 09:40*; the title gives way to it) and
  the model when there is room (`chatRowSuffix`); a chat's tab row carries
  the model only. Every bulk close — a project's *Close tabs*, its ×,
  *Hide*, *Remove from list* — ends the quick chats Caprock started in the
  closing tabs as ⌘W does (`planTabsChatClose` in `onCloseProjectTabs`):
  idle ones at once; ones still answering after one question once the tabs
  are gone (`CloseChatConfirm` with `closed`: **Stop**, **Keep running**,
  Escape keeps them running). Agents and shells keep their own rules; the
  menu's hint says *the chat ends* or *chats end, agents run on*. A project's × (*Close project*) first
  calls `onStopProjectShells` (AppShell): every live shell of the project,
  tabbed or not, goes through `planProjectClose` against a fresh
  `GET /v1/shells`; idle ones are killed, and busy ones get one
  `CloseShellConfirm` listing them all (*alpha: Shell 1 is running claude,
  Shell 2 is running npm. Stop them and close?* — *Stop and close*, *Keep
  them running, close tabs*, *Cancel*). Cancel closes nothing; otherwise it
  calls `onCloseProjectTabs` and, unless `keepsProject(node, stopped)` (live
  work beyond the shells just stopped, or waiting work), hides it; agents
  are never stopped; closing the project in front moves the front to the next tab,
  else the next listed project. A hidden row shows the open eye (*Show …
  in the list again*) instead. ■ on a muted row Caprock owns, and *Stop
  the session…* / *Stop the shell…* in a row's menu, open
  `StopConfirm.tsx`, the cockpit's confirmation, which sends `kill` through
  `/v1/agents/{id}/signal`; a session Caprock did not start gets neither
  (rule 7). Every nested row has a menu (`RowMenu.tsx`) on a right-click,
  Shift+F10 or the context-menu key: on a tab, *Close tab* / *Close shell*,
  *Close tab and hide from the list* for an agent, and *Stop…*; on a row
  with no tab, *Open as a tab* (or *Open in a tab, read-only* for another
  terminal's, which opens its watched tab, [21-app.md](21-app.md)), *Hide from the list* or *Show in the list again*, and
  *Stop…* for what Caprock started. No key but ⌘W closes anything; Delete and Backspace do nothing in the sidebar. Ended
  sessions are not listed; the model still holds every worktree and
  session for the palette, Changes, Open file and the project's page. The tab in front is the only
  selected row in the sidebar (`aria-current`, `.app-row-selected`: an
  accent tint, a solid 3px accent edge and a bold title); the current
  project's name is bold (`data-current`), with no edge. Order
  (`groupProjects`): pinned first, then the hand-set `sort`, then by name —
  never by activity, so a click never moves a row — and *Outside projects*
  last. With more than `FOLD_ABOVE` (8) projects, what is not in play
  (live, waiting, a tab open — `tabbed` — pinned, current) leaves the
  list; with eight or fewer all show. Hidden projects leave it too, even
  while something runs in them; the current project stays listed (last)
  and one waiting on you comes back (owner, 2026-10-11). **Every open tab
  has a row**, in Tree and in Flat (owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "the
  tabs in the top strip don't match what's on the left"): a project with a
  tab open, or a session row with its tab open, is listed even when hidden
  by hand (`groupProjects`, `rowHidden`). Hiding a project closes its tabs
  (the menu's hint says *closes its tabs* when it has any) and *Close tab
  and hide* closes a session's, so this only shows what was hidden earlier
  and opened again — from *More…*, a link or a restored workspace. The
  hidden mark is kept, not cleared: when the last tab closes, the row goes
  back behind *More…* by itself. All of them sit
  behind **one quiet *More…* row** at the end (`data-sidebar-more`; owner,
  2026-10-13: "one button like *More…*", translated), which replaced the
  *More projects* and *Hidden* folds: it opens a menu of the other
  projects (click to open), then hidden projects and hidden session rows,
  each `title · project` (click to show again). Its tooltip counts them
  (*7 more projects, 2 hidden*). The fold chevron at
  a project row's left (20×26) folds it by hand. Hover or focus shows
  *New agent*, *New shell*, an eye that hides the project in one click, a ⋯
  that opens the project menu and the project's × — on a touch screen the
  ⋯ is always beside the + instead —
  (`ProjectMenu.tsx`; also right-click or Shift+F10): hide, close its tabs,
  *Clean up copies…* (a repository only), open in an editor, remove from
  Caprock after a confirmation.
- **Clean up copies** (`CopiesSheet.tsx`, `lib/copies.ts`; the project menu
  or ⌘K, for the project in front; 2026-10-11, from the Orca gap analysis).
  A sheet listing `GET /v1/projects/{id}/copies`: one row per worktree, the
  main checkout first — a checkbox, the branch (or *detached at* the short
  commit) in mono with the path under it (relative to the main checkout when
  inside it), badges, the last commit's age and
  the size, right-aligned. Badges: *main checkout*, *yours* (not Caprock's),
  *folder missing*, *locked*, *live* (accent; the tooltip names what runs),
  *N changes* and *N unpushed* (warn), *merged* and *pushed* (ok), *N on no
  branch* (danger), *git error*. A size is *…* while measured (the sheet
  polls every 1.5 s while `pending`), *≥* when the walk hit its cap; the
  header says *N copies · total on disk*, *so far* while measuring. The
  checkbox is disabled, with the reason under the path, for the main
  checkout, a copy not Caprock made, a locked one and a live one
  (`blockedReason`). *Select merged and clean* picks those that lose
  nothing. The footer: *Also delete merged branches* (off by default),
  *Close*, and *Remove N · size* in danger red. When a picked copy has
  uncommitted changes, unpushed commits or commits on no branch, *Remove*
  first replaces the list with a red-bordered panel listing each such copy
  and exactly what it would lose (`lossOf`), with *Back* and *Remove
  anyway*; only those copies are sent with `force`. The result line says
  how many were removed, the space freed and the branches deleted; a
  refusal is shown per copy with the daemon's reason. Under the list,
  *Recently removed* (`GET …/cleanup` `history`): the branch, when, how
  (*automatically*, *from the cleanup line*, *here*), the space freed and
  *Restore* while `restorable`. A *stale* badge (muted) marks a copy with no
  commit for 14 days and no pull request. Opened from the cleanup line's
  *Review*, the finished copies arrive ticked (`preselect`).
- **Cleanup line** (`CleanupLine.tsx`; 2026-10-13). On the project's empty
  workspace, between the actions and *Recent* (and, until the deck went on
  2026-10-20, over its Branches list in a smaller type): *N copies done · size · Clean up · Review*
  (Auto: *· removed in 18 h · Clean up now*), 12.5 px muted with accent
  links. *Clean up* swaps the line for *Remove N copies (size)? Branches
  stay 7 days. Remove · Cancel* — no modal. After a removal: *Removed N
  copies · size freed · Undo ×*; × hides it for this viewer (localStorage).
  Nothing when the mode is off or nothing is finished. It asks again when
  the project's worktrees change, and every 1.5 s only while sizes are
  measured. Settings → *Worktrees* (`WorktreeCleanupSetting.tsx`): *Finished
  copies* Off · Ask · Auto.
  At the bottom, outlined: *Dashboard*, then *Settings* with a gear and
  *phone, Telegram* beside it, and the theme. ↑ ↓ move between rows. The background is translucent so the shell's
  window material shows through on macOS. The workspace sets
  `data-caprock-chrome` on `<html>` ([app/README.md](../app/README.md) §
  Title bar): the sidebar header (or, with the sidebar hidden, the tab strip)
  clears the traffic lights by `--caprock-traffic-lights-inset` and carries
  the drag regions, so the shell adds no padding or strip of its own.
- **Projects** come from `GET /v1/projects` and `project` frames
  (`lib/projects.ts`, [03-contracts.md § Projects and
  shells](03-contracts.md#projects-and-shells-desktop-app-wp-05-wp-07-wp-08)),
  flattened by `fromApiProject`: git's branch (none for a folder or a detached
  checkout; a detached worktree reads `detached @ <sha7>`), changed files,
  ahead/behind, worktrees, today's cost. The daemon lists repositories only, so
  live sessions in any other folder (~/Downloads/caprock) sit under **Other
  folders** at the bottom, one row per folder, named apart from each other and
  from the listed projects by the shortest path suffix. ⌘O adds a folder
  (`{path}`), a new one (`{create}`, `git init`) or a clone (`{clone}` with an
  `op_id`; the sheet shows git's phase and percentage from `op` frames and
  selects the project when it is listed). Shells have no session row: the
  sidebar lists them from the `shells` frame on `/v1/live` (pushed when the
  list changes; `GET /v1/shells` on mount and once a minute as the fallback)
  and after ⌘T, which
  sends `project_id` (or the worktree's `cwd`). A daemon without these
  endpoints gets a list derived from where sessions ran (grouped by repository,
  as the cost roll-up groups them), plus folders added in the app (kept in
  localStorage); clone, a new folder and shells then say they need a newer
  daemon instead of failing quietly.
- **Tabs** (`TerminalTabs.tsx`, model in `lib/tabs.ts`). One strip for every
  project since 2026-10-09: `ws.tabs` in the order opened. A session tab is
  named by its session (`tabLabels`, as the sidebar names it), its
  project's short name after the title in the project's colour (a hue
  hashed from the project id, `projectHue`). **No dots** since 2026-10-14
  (owner, translated: "the indicators tell me nothing at all, literally
  zero — you can honestly remove them"): the status dot and the project
  dot that led every tab are gone, and a tab shows state only when it asks
  something of you — an 8px amber dot with a halo before its title while
  the session waits on you (a permission prompt, a question, a stop for
  input: the sidebar's `waiting`, `bg-warn app-dot-waiting`, pulsing slowly
  with `.app-tab-dot-pulse`, still under reduced motion), a red one
  (`bg-danger`) when it loops or failed (health `looping`, `error`), the
  word in the tooltip and `aria-description` (`data-tab-alert`); working
  and idle show nothing on the tab. **Only the tab in front looks
  selected** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "I don't understand where I
  am, it's impossible" — the waiting tabs wore a 3px accent bar along their
  top, the same as the tab in front's, so four tabs looked selected): the
  bar, the panel's fill and the bold title are `.app-tab-front`'s alone,
  and a state never draws a bar or a fill. The tab in front is at least
  232px wide and its title keeps 16 characters (`min-w-[16ch]`), so it
  never reads *ca…*; there the project's name gives way first
  (`shrink-[4]`) and, under 28px, is dropped rather than cut to a letter
  (`data-gone`). The others may shrink to 92px, their titles to 4ch.
  The agent has no mark on the tab, and the
  project, the agent and the branch are in the tooltip (*Fix the login
  loop · Claude Code · main · atlas — ⌘1*), the project in the accessible
  name too (owner, 2026-10-13, translated: "I have only four tabs and
  already can't see the 4th one's title. I don't care which agent it is …
  the session name is very important"). From 2026-10-11 to then the tab
  read the repository's name and only a second tab of the same project
  added its title, faint; before that the title led with a 10ch project
  chip. Since 2026-10-14 the project's short name follows the title,
  faint (`data-tab-project`): fixed, never edited; the session's name is
  renamed by a double-click. The project name is whole first (owner,
  2026-10-14, translated: "the repo name on the right doesn't fit — it must
  fit, at least scroll inside on hover … give it more space. The session
  name is fine"): it has no cap of its own (it was `max-w-[9ch]`), and the
  title, `flex-1` from a `min-w-[4ch]` floor, is cut before it is. Tabs are
  `flex-1` from `basis-[220px]` between 112 and 340px (were 200 and 260),
  so a few tabs spread wide. Only when even that floor is not enough is
  the project cut, with an ellipsis; then `TabProject` measures the text
  against its box (a ResizeObserver, never on hover), marks it
  `data-overflow` with the hidden width in `--tab-project-shift`, and under
  the pointer the text slides that far inside its own box and back
  (`tab-project-scroll`, tokens.css, ~40px/s): the box never changes size,
  so nothing in the strip moves. With reduced motion it stays cut. The
  tooltip always has the whole title and project. Measured 2026-10-14 in
  the headless shell at 1280 and 1600 wide with 3, 6 and 9 tabs: at 1600
  three tabs show *uncensored-chat-app* and *creatos_frontend* whole (they
  read *uncensor…* and *creatos_f…*), six still do except the longest; at
  9 tabs (112px each) a project keeps 40–47px and scrolls; hovering any
  tab moved no tab's box by a pixel. The tab's ⌘ key (`digits`) and its × share
  one slot at the right end (`data-tab-end`): the key, compact
  (`.app-kbd-sm`), at rest and the × over it on hover; on the tab in front
  the key with the × to its right, so every tab shows its key. At
  1440×900 with the inspector open, six tabs still show 14–16 characters
  of each title (measured 2026-10-13). A file tab keeps the file name. It was one strip per project, and picking another
  project emptied it — the first tester read that as his terminals closing,
  and Vova as his tabs vanishing (both translated). Picking a project in the
  sidebar opens its page since 2026-10-14 (the `project` action with
  `view`); ⌥⌘← ⌥⌘→ (the `project` action) bring its last tab to the front
  (`activeByProject`), or show its empty state with the strip intact;
  picking a tab makes its project current. Cycling (⌃Tab, ⇧⌘[ ]) and
  drag-to-reorder run along the whole strip; closing the tab in front hands
  the front to its strip neighbour (right, else left), whatever its project.
  A stored workspace from the per-project strips has the same shape and
  loads as it is. Pinned at the strip's left, while open, a **Dashboard** tab
  (`ws.dashboard`), folded to its icon and its key — ⌘0, or ⌘, while it
  reads Settings (`stripKey`; Ctrl+Shift+0 and Ctrl+Shift+, off macOS, drawn only while Ctrl is held: the **Keyboard** item below) — drawn always on a Mac,
  in front or behind, as a session tab's ⌘N (owner, 2026-10-14,
  translated: "I must always see the key combinations next to the tabs");
  its name is in the tooltip and its × shows only while it is in front
  (2026-10-13, to give the session tabs their width). The inspector's strip
  button draws ⌘I beside its icon: ⌘D, ⌘0 or ⇧⌘D, the sidebar's *Dashboard* and any dashboard route
  (`#/cost`, `#/settings`, `#/session/…`) open it, it shows the screen last
  shown in it, reads *Settings* on the settings route, and closes like a tab
  (back to the workspace). After it, while open, the pinned **Pipeline**
  tab (`ws.pipeline`: its glyph and ⌘P, § The Pipeline) and then the
  **Mail** tab (`ws.mail`: its envelope and ⇧⌘M, § Mail; in a browser, or a desktop shell too old to open Mail in its own window). A tab is a tree of panes, so split panes (F15) added a layout, not
  a format: ⌘E a new shell beside the focused pane in its folder, ⇧⌘E below
  it, ⇧Enter in the palette a session beside it. Up to four panes; each has a
  header (dot, agent, title, ×) and the focused one an amber ring while the
  others dim; ⌘[ ⌘] move the focus (palette off macOS), the divider drags or
  takes ←→↑↓ (5% a step, 10% minimum), ⌘W closes the focused pane and the
  last one the tab. The tab's title, the inspector and the strip follow the
  focused pane. The tab in front is cut from the terminal's own slab, under
  a 3px accent top edge, and
  framed by a hairline that opens into it (`.app-tab-front`; the strip's
  bottom rule is painted under the tabs, `.app-strip`), since with Match app
  the slab is the strip's own colour. Tabs are kept in localStorage and restored on relaunch;
  the terminals reconnect and the daemon repaints them. Every open terminal
  stays mounted (switching paints at once); a tab out of sight for 30 s drops
  its socket and catches up when shown. The three tabs hidden last keep their
  WebGL context (`KEEP_WEBGL_HIDDEN`), so going back and forth never swaps the
  renderer; a tab past that lets its context go and gets one back in `show()`,
  before the first paint. After the tabs:
  *New agent* (+) and *New shell* (>_); at the right end the view icon (an
  agent tab only) and the inspector. The view icon is one quiet 28px icon
  button, no word and no border (owner, 2026-10-20, variant A; a worded
  *Chat* toggle from 2026-10-14, then worded *Chat | Terminal* pills): a
  chat bubble over the terminal, a terminal glyph over the chat, named
  *Show as chat* / *Show as terminal*; it has no key (§ Chat or Terminal).
  The strip's *Deck* button went with the session deck on 2026-10-20.
  The chat, Changes and a comparison cover the tab in front
  (`TerminalStack`'s `covered`): its panel is hidden as for a tab behind
  another — output held, PTY size given back, no WebGL canvas drawn under
  the cover — and comes back through the same `show()` as a switch. A
  project with no tab open shows its name and three rows instead: *New agent*
  ⇧⌘N, *New shell* ⌘T, *Add a project* ⌘O. Closing a tab
  (⌘W, the ×, a middle click) never stops the session — except a quick chat, which ends with its tab ([21-app.md](21-app.md)); *Stop the session…* in
  the inspector asks first. A tab can also hold a file (*File tabs* below):
  a target of kind `file` whose key is `file:<project>:<worktree>:<path>`,
  so the same file opens once; it is never split, and a split asked from
  it opens a tab of its own.
- **Terminal** (`TerminalPane.tsx`). xterm.js on the dashboard's own
  `TermClient` (`lib/termv2.ts`, WP-03), the same client as `Terminal.tsx`.
  The app adds two things to it: `suspend()`, which lets a hidden tab's socket
  go and keeps the offset so `wake()` resumes from the last byte, and a
  `protocol` getter for the status strip. A pane never yet laid out — a tab
  restored behind another — writes nothing and sends the daemon no size until
  it is shown and fitted: its xterm is at the default 80×24 until then, and
  the replay it opens with, parsed at a width it was not drawn at, left
  Claude Code's prompt and status rows smeared and repeated in the
  scrollback, which no later reflow undoes. What arrives meanwhile is held
  (as for any hidden pane) and written at the real size. Keys, copy, paste and dropped files are the
  dashboard's own (`lib/xtermInput.ts`, moved out of `Terminal.tsx`
  unchanged). WebGL is swapped in late as in the dashboard, and re-created after
  a context loss the next time the tab is shown. A switch shows the terminal
  as it was (owner, 2026-10-14: the font looked redrawn for a moment): a tab
  with output that has no WebGL gets it synchronously in `show()` (a layout
  effect) and is drawn in that frame, never first by the DOM renderer, whose
  glyphs and cell height differ; the glyph atlas is rebuilt only when the
  window comes back into view, the pixel ratio changes, or a tab hidden over
  five minutes (`LONG_HIDDEN_MS`) or across a wake is shown (#541); and a
  tab whose box did not change is not refitted. **The atlas is shared**:
  xterm's WebGL addon keeps one per face, size, palette and pixel ratio
  for every terminal, and `clearTextureAtlas()` forgets only the caller's
  cell model, so one tab rebuilding it left every other tab drawing cells
  it had not redrawn since from glyph positions now holding other glyphs
  (owner, 2026-10-14: rows of "ι ɤr ·r ɤrrง" in one tab, right in the
  rows just redrawn; for ~2 s after closing a tab and after the chat).
  Every rebuild goes through `clearGlyphAtlas` (`lib/termfont.ts`), which
  clears the model of every live terminal in the same task — reproduced
  in WebKit (a tab hidden past five minutes shown, then its neighbour) and
  gone with it. Every other show — a switch, the tab in front closed, back
  from the dashboard or the chat, a resize while hidden — re-sends the atlas
  to the GPU from its canvas without rasterising (`reuploadGlyphs`, the
  addon's own `setAtlas`; one `texImage2D` per page), in case WebKit let a
  hidden canvas's texture go; an addon without those insides rebuilds
  instead. **A page merge garbled the tab in front for good** (owner,
  2026-10-15, on the release with all of the above: the whole screen of
  the active tab drawn from the wrong cells of the atlas, letters clipped
  and overlapped, layout and colours right, until font +/-). The addon
  (0.19) uploads atlas page `i` to texture unit `i` when the page's
  `version` differs from the one last uploaded there, and that version is
  a counter per page. At its page cap (16 in WebKit on Apple GPUs) the
  atlas merges four pages into a new one, which starts at version 1 and is
  never written again; on the second merge the new merged page lands at
  the index the first one held, also at version 1, so the unit keeps the
  old page's pixels while every glyph of the four most-used pages points
  into it. Five Claude Code tabs share one atlas and fill it with their
  colour pairs; in WebKit at 2× it took ten screens of distinct colours.
  `healAtlasMerges` (`lib/termatlas.ts`, on every WebGL addon, the
  dashboard terminal's too) listens for the merge
  (`onRemoveTextureAtlasCanvas`, fired by every addon sharing the atlas):
  it marks every unit stale in the same call, so the frame's own upload
  sees it, and redraws the viewport once. Reproduced in Playwright WebKit
  with two terminals and the installed addon — screens after the second
  merge differ from a fresh terminal's without it, match with it; fixed
  upstream in the 0.20 betas by a global version counter. The window back
  in focus after `FOCUS_HEAL_MS` (30 s) in the background re-sends the
  pane's atlas too: a cheap self-heal, never a timer. **A lost WebGL
  context is never drawn on** (users, 2026-10-20: after switching tabs or
  apps the terminal kept stale rows — Claude Code's subagent list at a row
  count it had left — and the quick-reply row had a hint stuck over it,
  until a restart). The addon (0.19) hears `webglcontextlost`, waits 3 s for
  a restore and only then reports the loss, so every frame of output in
  between went to a dead canvas still showing the screen of the moment it
  died; a context WebKit lets go without the event was never reported, and
  the pane stayed frozen for good; and a restore rebuilt in place, which in
  Playwright's WebKit deleted the old context's objects through the new one,
  a console error each. Now `lib/termgl.ts` hears the event on the addon's
  canvas and the pane drops the addon at once — xterm's DOM renderer draws
  that frame, a fresh context follows after `WEBGL_QUIET_MS` — and a context
  gone silently is asked for (`isContextLost()`, one boolean, no timer) on
  every parsed write of the pane in front, on show, and when the window
  comes back into view or into focus, a quick ⌘-Tab included; the
  dashboard terminal (`Terminal.tsx`) does the same. Reproduced in
  Playwright's WebKit and Chromium with `WEBGL_lose_context` (the silent
  case by stopping the event above the canvas): before, the pane was still
  on the lost context 0.6 s after the loss and after a blur and focus;
  after, it never is (`ui/e2e/redraw.spec.ts`). The key hint over Claude
  Code's input box (above) goes when the window loses focus: the pointer
  leaves with the window and no `mouseleave` comes, so it stayed over the
  box and the quick replies under it. Un-splitting remounts the pane that stays, which starts on a
  fresh renderer. The palette, face, size,
  line height and cursor are Settings → *Terminal* (F21, `lib/termprefs.ts`,
  `lib/termthemes.ts`): every open pane subscribes and sets `term.options` in
  place, never re-created; the default palette is *Match app*: Caprock
  (`APP_TERMINAL_THEME`, a warm 16-colour set on the dark panel `#211f1d`,
  the strip's colour — on the page's `#1b1b1a` it read as a window on top
  of the window) in the dark theme — Slate (`SLATE_TERMINAL_THEME`, a cool
  set on `#1f2023`) when the dark theme is Slate — and Paper's ink on the light panel
  (`#f4ecdd` paper, `#ffffff` white) in the light one; a `MutationObserver`
  on `<html data-theme data-tone data-dark>` repaints every pane when the app's theme
  moves. A stored `caprock` without the `v: 2` marker reads as Match app:

  place, never re-created; the default palette is *Match app*: the app
  palette's own terminal (`terminal` in `lib/themes.ts`) on that palette's
  panel — Dark Warm (`APP_TERMINAL_THEME`, a warm 16-colour set on
  `#211f1d`, the strip's colour — on the page's `#1b1b1a` it read as a
  window on top of the window), Dark Cold (`SLATE_TERMINAL_THEME`, a cool
  set on `#1f2023`), Dark Side (`DARK_SIDE_TERMINAL_THEME`, a neon purple cursor
  and a violet-leaning set on `#15111d`), Book's ink on `#f4ecdd` and on
  `#ffffff` in Light, and Light Cold's and Light Side's own sets on their
  panels; a `MutationObserver` on `<html data-theme data-tone data-dark>`
  repaints every pane when the app's palette moves. A stored `caprock` without the `v: 2` marker reads as Match app:
  before it every save wrote the whole set, so the old default and a choice
  of Caprock cannot be told apart. The font defaults are the JetBrains IDE terminal's —
  JetBrains Mono 13 px, line height 1.2, weights 400 and 700, no ligatures.
  The face is `lib/termfont.ts`: every subset, regular and bold, is asked
  for before a pane opens (and at boot in app mode), and a face finishing
  its load re-measures the cell (xterm measures only on a font option
  change), clears the WebGL atlas and refits; the dashboard terminal does
  the same. A cell no monospace face draws — wider than 0.9 of the
  font size or narrower than 0.4 (`implausibleCell`) — is measured again on
  the next frames, five times at most, checked after `document.fonts.ready`,
  when the window comes back into view and when a pane is shown (owner,
  2026-10-14: after an update restarted the app every glyph sat in a cell
  about three times too wide until font size + then − re-measured). `.xterm` in `tokens.css` sets `-webkit-font-smoothing: auto` —
  xterm rasterises warm-up glyphs on a detached canvas and the rest on one
  inside `:root`'s `antialiased`, and WebKit drew the two in different
  weights, so digits, capitals and punctuation came out heavier than the
  letters beside them at random — and turns `liga`/`calt` off. The slab around the panes (the tab in front, the
  pane headers, the padding) takes the palette's ground through
  `--app-term-bg` on `<html>`. Under Match app (`data-term-match`) the slab
  keeps the app palette's own ink, since the terminal sits on that palette's
  panel; a named palette gives it Dark Warm's ink, or, light (Book, Light
  Cold, Light Side), `data-term-tone="light"` and Light's ink. Every
  palette, dark ones included, gets xterm's 4.5:1 `minimumContrastRatio`
  (`TERMINAL_MIN_CONTRAST` in `lib/termthemes.ts`; the dashboard and phone
  terminal too), so a TUI's dim grey stays readable on a light ground and
  text stays readable on whatever a program paints behind it. Dark ones were
  at 1 until 2026-10-14: Claude Code's own text selection (its fullscreen
  mode) paints the highlight in ANSI blue (`dark-ansi` theme; cyan in
  `light-ansi`) and keeps the default ink, and a dark palette's blue is
  light — ink on it was 1.2–2:1 in every dark palette (Dark Pastel 1.65:1,
  owner: "can't read a thing", translated). xterm's own selection was
  already readable: ink on `selectionBackground` is 7.5:1 or more in our
  palettes, 4.6:1 in Catppuccin Mocha, 4.1:1 in Solarized Dark (its
  upstream base02, now lifted to 4.5 by the floor).
  Nothing else on the page subscribes per byte: the sidebar reads the live
  socket through a one-second debounce.
- **Where you type** (`lib/terminputbox.ts`; owner, 2026-10-15, translated:
  "a light, slight highlight of the input field, one that doesn't draw much
  attention"). In a Claude Code agent tab (not a shell, not another agent)
  the rows of Claude Code's input box — between the two `─` rules, starting
  with the `❯` prompt — sit on a faint band: the terminal's background lifted
  toward its ink, 5% on a light palette and 7% on a dark one, as xterm
  decorations (under the text and the selection, anchored to buffer lines, so
  the band scrolls with the box). Found again on each parsed write batch, a
  resize or a palette change from the last 40 rows of the buffer only; the
  decorations are re-registered only when the box moved, grew or changed
  colour. It draws only on cells with the default background: a cell Claude
  Code coloured or inverted (its cursor) keeps its colours, and the
  decoration's element takes no pointer events. Anything it is not sure of —
  no rule pair, no prompt under the top rule, a numbered menu row, a box over
  30 rows, the alternate screen — draws nothing. The DOM renderer (no WebGL)
  stops each row's band where its text ends.
  **One switch, with a key** (owner, 2026-10-17, translated: "a toggle to
  turn the input field highlight on/off … some simple key combination, so
  there's a hint where needed"): Settings → Terminal → *Highlight where you
  type (⌥⌘G)*, on by default, with *Subtle* (the lift above) or *Stronger*
  (9% light, 12% dark) while it is on; `highlightInput` and
  `highlightStrength` in the terminal prefs (`lib/termprefs.ts`, this
  browser's localStorage, every pane and window at once). It is also the
  keys bar's and the Chat view's field tint (`.composer-field`, `data-input-hl`
  on `<html>`: `off` or `stronger`; the focus glow stays when it is off,
  since it says where the keyboard is). **⌥⌘G** (`INPUT_HIGHLIGHT_KEY`;
  Ctrl+Alt+Shift+G off macOS, as Quick chat's Ctrl+Alt+Shift+N, since
  Ctrl+Shift+G is Changes) flips it from anywhere, a terminal included,
  and says so in a toast: *Input highlight off · ⌥⌘G to turn back on*. Not
  ⌥⌘I, the first thought: every Mac browser takes that for its inspector
  before the page sees it. ⌘K has *Toggle input highlight*. The hint is
  where the eye is: with the pointer over the input box, the key is written
  at the right end of the box's top rule (*⌥⌘G hides the highlight*, or
  *… highlights where you type* while it is off, the box still being found
  then), faint, on the terminal's own ground, taking no pointer events; the
  keys bar's field has it in its tooltip.
- **An agent's update command** (`lib/termupdate.ts`,
  `components/UpdateNoticeBar.tsx`; owner, 2026-10-17, translated: "I'd
  update, but it's inconvenient to even copy the line. Let's at least: copy
  it and open a terminal next to it — simple, I'll do it myself"). When a
  terminal shows an agent's update notice with a command, a small bar sits at
  its bottom right: *Codex 0.162.0 available · Copy command · Open a terminal
  · ×*, the command in its tooltip. **Copy command** puts the exact printed
  command on the clipboard (*Copied*, or *Could not copy*); **Open a
  terminal** opens a plain shell tab in the session's folder, in front
  (`caprock:open-shell-beside`, answered by the workspace with its *New
  shell*), and types nothing into it — the user pastes and presses Enter;
  **×** hides it for that agent and version (this browser's localStorage; a
  notice without a version, for a day). Nothing printed in a terminal is ever
  run (rule 7's spirit). The matcher is small and explicit: Codex's
  `Update available! A -> B` followed within three rows by `Run <command> to
  update.` with `codex` in the command (backticks around it dropped); Claude
  Code's `Update available! Run: <command>` and `Auto-update failed · Try
  claude doctor or <command>`, with `claude` in it. A command comes back whole
  across wrapped rows: rows xterm wrapped are joined as they are, rows the
  agent word-wrapped itself (Codex's history, in its `│` box or not) with a
  space, or with none after a single word cut at the row's edge — a URL
  longer than the width — unless the next row starts with a shell operator.
  A word exactly as long as the wrap width cannot be told from one broken
  there, and joins without the space. Cheap: on parsed write batches, at most
  every 500 ms, over the last 30 rows of the normal buffer; the watch stops
  at the first notice. Triple-click already selects a whole wrapped line in
  xterm 6 (`getWrappedRangeForLine`), so copying any long command by hand
  needs nothing more.
- **Find** (F16, ⌘F; `TerminalFind.tsx` over `@xterm/addon-search`, the
  release paired with xterm 6.0.0). ⌘F dispatches `caprock:terminal-find`;
  only the focused pane of the tab in front opens its bar, at its top right:
  the query (searched as you type), *n of m* (*1000+* past the highlight
  limit; *No results*; *Invalid pattern*), **Aa** match case, **.\***
  regular expression, ↑ ↓ (⇧Enter, Enter) and ×. Every match is highlighted
  in a tint of the palette's own yellow, the current one stronger with the
  cursor colour as its border. Esc in the field closes it, clears the
  highlights and gives the keyboard back to the terminal; Esc in the terminal
  stays the program's. Each pane of a split has its own bar.
- **Inspector** (⌘I, `Inspector.tsx`), open by default (`cockpit` in
  `caprock.app.ui`; the older closed-by-default `inspector` flag is not
  read). Beside an agent it is the **agent cockpit** (`Cockpit.tsx`,
  `lib/cockpit.ts`; [21-app.md § What the user sees](21-app.md#what-the-user-sees)):
  title, then where it runs — the project's name as the sidebar shows it,
  `· branch` only when that is not the project's default (main or master
  when git did not say), and the full path on hover, never a path cut to
  fit (`lib/projects.ts` `sessionPlace`); the permission prompt's buttons; the agent's
  character and state, with **three figures** at the right of that row
  (owner, 2026-10-12: "put it to the right of the agent, then the chart is
  wide"; then "let several numbers be changeable"): the **headline** big,
  and two short lines under it in 13px, the value then a short word and a
  ▾ (*$4.08/h burn ▾*, *4.01B tok ▾*). The hero is **one grid that never
  changes shape** (`.cockpit-hero` in `design/tokens.css`; owner,
  2026-10-13: "restore the awesome look — both when work is going on and
  when it waits"): the agent's row (avatar; the agent, its model on a line
  of its own so the figure never cuts it, and *started 6d ago*) with the
  headline at its right; a detail row: under the portrait, centred, the
  state line (dot and word in the state's colour; a long word wraps onto a
  second line in a narrow inspector, *Idle* puts how long ago under it) and
  the permission-mode chip under that, its menu unchanged (owner,
  2026-10-14: "status and mode can both go under the portrait — then the
  figures get better visibility", translated), with the two smaller figures
  at the right taking the rest of the row; then the subagent chips. The
  detail row is tall enough for a two-line state and the chip, so a state
  change recolours the ring, dot and state line and changes the chips;
  nothing moves. A
  container query steps the avatar and headline down for a narrower
  inspector (32px from 420px, 28px from 340px, 24px under; a figure of
  eight characters or more, *$1,197.22*, one step smaller). The
  **portrait is Claude in its session's face** (`SessionFace.tsx`,
  `lib/faces.ts`; owner, 2026-10-14: "make at least 6–9 variants of this
  little guy, just for fun, so you can change it, and they get assigned,
  e.g. randomly", translated): eight looks of the same pebble (`Body` in
  `Characters.tsx` with a `face` — hard hat, beanie, headphones, crown,
  wizard, tuft, bandana, propeller cap), bodies from the Dark Pastel set,
  each with its own expression; a session's face is an FNV-1a hash of its
  id, so it is the same in the hero, the Now screen's live list and on a
  phone. A click on the portrait opens a 4×2 menu of the faces and *Use
  the default*; the pick is kept per viewer (`caprock.faces.sessions`, the
  newest 300) and Settings → Appearance → *Claude's face* sets the default
  (`caprock.faces.default`: *A face per session*, or one face for all).
  **Rubber ducks** (owner, 2026-10-14: "a whole series of rubber ducks that
  sit next to you; it's basically a programmer thing", translated) are
  faces too: eight bath-toy ducks in the same 80×80 box (`DuckBody` —
  classic, debugger in glasses, on-call in a headset, party, pirate,
  hacker in a hoodie, sleepy in a nightcap, rainbow), each named in its
  tooltip. **Pumpkins** (owner, 2026-10-16: "a collection of pumpkins
  for Halloween", translated) are a third group the same way: eight in the
  same box (`PumpkinBody` — jack-o'-lantern, witch, vampire, ghost, black
  cat, mummy, monster, lantern), the eyes where the pebble's are, on a
  *Pumpkins* tab of their own and a pool of their own (*A pumpkin per
  session*). They never join `FACES` or the faces-and-ducks pool, so no
  hashed session changes face. The menu has three tabs, *Faces*, *Ducks* and *Pumpkins* (arrow keys go
  round the tabs and, Down/Up, into and out of the grid); it opens on
  the tab of the face worn. The random pool stays the eight faces —
  `FACES` is never reordered or extended, so the FNV-1a hash lands every
  session on the face it had before the ducks — and the default adds two
  pools, *A duck per session* and *Faces and ducks* (and later a third,
  *A pumpkin per session*), beside every face and
  duck as a fixed choice. **"try a duck?"**: idle or waiting on you, with
  no permission prompt up and no key pressed for 8 seconds, a session not
  already in a duck shows a small speech line out of the portrait (panel
  ground, muted text, the tail pointing at the portrait; "started …"
  steps aside under it). Clicking it opens the menu on *Ducks*; × closes
  it; a key, 30 seconds unanswered or a state change hides it. At most
  once a day; never again once the ducks have been opened or chosen, or
  after two ×s (`caprock.faces.duckHint`, per viewer; storage that throws
  reads as done, so a broken store never nags). In October the same line says
  **"try a pumpkin?"** with a pumpkin, opens the *Pumpkins* tab, skips a
  session already in a pumpkin, and keeps its own record
  (`caprock.faces.pumpkinHint`, the same rules), so a viewer done with the
  ducks still hears about the pumpkins once (owner, 2026-10-16).
  Storage that throws or holds garbage reads as nothing chosen. Other
  agents keep their own marks and their portrait is not a button. The
  defaults are burn rate, tokens and cost (owner, 2026-10-13: what a
  session is doing now, then what it has added up to; a stored choice that
  is exactly the old cost-first default takes the new one, any other stays).
  A quiet *burn rate ▾* under the
  headline, and each small line itself, opens the same menu (name, value,
  and what it is on a second line) of **Cost**, **Burn rate** (dollars an
  hour: what the calls in the hour ending at the newest call cost, or over
  a younger session's own span, at least five minutes; summed from the
  calls the cockpit already holds, never from a new endpoint; none with
  fewer than two priced calls), **Tokens**, **Cache hit rate**, **Turns**,
  **Time working** (start to last activity), **Last call** and **Tool
  calls**. One another figure shows is marked *· shown*, and picking it
  swaps the two (`pickHero`), so none is shown twice. The three are
  remembered per viewer (`hero` in `caprock.app.cockpit.spend` in
  localStorage, `lib/spendview.ts`; a stored `headline` from before stays
  the big one; owner: "the number is cool but says nothing; let me switch
  what matters more to me"). Left of *burn rate ▾*, the headline **against
  the viewer's usual** (`HeroUsual.tsx`, `lib/usual.ts`; owner, 2026-10-13:
  "is this above average or below, and by how many percent — super
  compact", picked from four forms — text, a bullet strip, a range dot, a
  mini histogram — on a local compare page): *▲ 38%*, *≈ usual* (within
  15%), *▼ 55%*, or a multiple from twice (*▲ 4.5×*); for cache hit rate,
  points (*▼ 9 pts*, *≈* within 2). The baseline is `usual` on the
  summary ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)): burn rate against the
  median active session-hour, cost and tokens against the median session
  (*already more than* — a running session is set against finished ones),
  cache against the 30-day rate; turns, time, tool calls and the last call
  have none and show nothing. **Amber only in the viewer's top fifth** (or
  cache 3 points or more under): the owner's own active hours run from
  cents to $68 with a $3 median, so 4.5× the median is only the 83rd
  percentile, and a percentage of the median alone would cry wolf; below
  usual stays muted, since a tone means act. Hovering says it whole: *38%
  above your usual $3.00/h (the median active session-hour, 30 days) ·
  higher than 56% of your session-hours*. With too little history it is
  absent and nothing moves. Under 340px a long name beside it steps down to
  its short word (*▼ 9 pts cache ▾*): the long one cut the agent's name to
  *Claude Co…*. Below it, in the order
  the people using it look (owner, 2026-10-12: context, what the subagents
  are doing, what is happening now and the plan limits matter):
  *Now*, the loop warning, one line when another live session works in
  the same checkout — the Live pulse's chip, a warning when both edited a
  file in the last half hour ([§ Live pulse](#live-pulse)) — then **Context** and **Plan limits** (moved up
  from the bottom; owner, 2026-10-14: "you moved the context window size
  too far down — it's needed info; make it compact or like now, plan and
  limits too", translated), then **Spend** (owner, 2026-10-13: "I like
  looking at it", picked from directly under the agent, under *Now*, and
  where it was), then *Subagents* and *Tasks*. Context and Plan limits
  each have two looks, switched on the section label (a chevron button,
  `aria-expanded` when full) and remembered per viewer in `localStorage`
  (`caprock.app.cockpit-looks`, `lib/cockpitlooks.ts`), **compact** until
  one is opened: Context is one line, the label, a 5px bar and *62% · 124.0k
  / 200.0k*, what the next call re-reads on hover; Plan limits is one line,
  *5h ▮ 41% · 05:40  Week ▮ 24% · Mon 13:00*, each window a 22px bar and
  its percentage in the full bar's tone, the reset clock giving way first (a weekly one to its weekday under 22rem, then cut) when the
  line is narrow and never wrapped, the reset and any run-out forecast on
  hover. **Full** is the meter with its line and the plan chip with a
  `WindowBar` per window, as before. Both read the same data either way.
  Spend opens unfolded on
  its **Chart** by default (owner,
  2026-10-12, when #476's Numbers default hid it: "where did you put the
  chart?"; the figures beside the agent carry the numbers now). The header
  is the fold toggle (a chevron, the whole row clickable, Enter or Space,
  `aria-expanded`), remembered per viewer; folded, it is one strip
  (`FoldSpark`): the chart's own bars 20px tall, then three figures in
  13px — the chart's metric first and bold, then cost, tokens, turns or
  time working, whichever the period carries, *$1,320.49 4.35B tok 9,672
  turns*; the third gives way under 340px (a container query). It shows
  the period and metric the chart shows, and its button is named for a
  screen reader (*Spend $1.84 · 950.1k tok · 12 turns*). It replaced a
  faint one-line sentence a 330px cockpit cut off (owner, 2026-10-13: "a
  more noticeable line with metrics"; picked over three labelled chips,
  which were twice as tall and lost the bars). Unfolded, the header row carries a period chip
  `session · today · 7d · 30d` (remembered, `period`) and a two-way icon
  switch **Chart | Numbers** (three bars and *123*, each named on hover and
  for a screen reader; remembered, `view`). The owner's words (2026-10-12):
  "you can choose what you see — the chart or the numbers — and which ones,
  and that makes it compact"; 0.111.0's *Compact | Full* had dropped the
  chart for a spark of a couple of bars. A stored `view` from then reads
  sensibly: *full* as Chart, *compact* as Numbers. The stored choices carry
  a version (`v: 5`), and each step of `migrateSpendPrefs` applies only to
  what was stored before it: one without a version, written while Numbers
  and folded were the defaults, cannot tell a pick from a default, so it
  opens unfolded on the chart once and any pick after that holds; before
  3, a hero that is exactly the old cost-first default takes the new one;
  4 added the chart's metric (`chartBy`), which an older choice lacks and
  reads as cost, as does any value the chart does not draw; 5 added burn
  rate to that menu, and a version 4 choice, picked from the three before
  it, is kept exactly as stored — there is no step for it.
  **Numbers** is one row of three large figures; each label (*SPENT ▾*) opens
  a menu, as wide as the block, of what can sit there — Spent, Burn rate,
  Tokens (input with cache reads and writes), Cache hit rate, Last call,
  Time working, Turns, Tool calls — each with its value for the period.
  Picking one another figure shows swaps the two, so no metric is ever
  shown twice (`pickSlot`), and the three are remembered (`slots`;
  Spent, Tokens, Cache until changed). For *session*, the three figures
  beside the agent are greyed in the menu (*beside the agent*) and a slot
  holding one shows the next metric not already shown (`shownSlots`): with
  the default burn rate, tokens and cost, *Last call · Time · Cache*. A Caprock-wide period is
  another figure, so Spent stays; burn rate, time working and the last call
  are a session's own and read as a dash there. **Chart** is the period by
  time, as wide as the cockpit and under 96px tall
  (`SpendChart`, `SpendScrub.tsx`): the period's figure, a *cost ▾* after
  it, and whose it is on the top line (*$29.51 cost ▾ 7 days · all
  sessions*), then the bars, 56px, and the first and last stretch under
  them. The ▾ picks what the bars measure — **Cost**, **Burn rate**,
  **Tokens** (input with cache reads and writes) or **Turns** (model
  calls) — in the hero's own menu style, each with the period's figure,
  remembered per viewer (`chartBy`, cost by default; owner, 2026-10-13:
  "switch its parameter, like cost and tokens", then "for some total spend
  matters more, for some burn rate, someone measures tokens"). **Burn
  rate** per bar is the stretch's cost over its *active* time, not its
  length — over its length it would only be cost drawn again: the
  stretch's active session-hours (a clock hour in which one session made
  five or more priced calls costing more than nothing, the very hours the
  usual's `burn` is the median of) and what they cost, `activeCost /
  activeHours`. A stretch with no active hour — idle, or an hour of one or
  two calls — has no rate and draws the faint stub of an empty bar, never a
  $0/h bar; its readout is *—* and *no active hour*. The top line's figure
  is the period's every active hour's cost over every active hour
  (`periodBurn`), which is not the hero's burn (the last hour of activity)
  and is said so in the menu's hint. For *session* a burn chart's bars are
  an hour at the finest (`timeBars(calls, 60)`), since a 5-minute stretch
  holds no whole hour. Time working and the last call are single figures,
  and the cache hit rate sits above 90% in every stretch of a working
  session, so its bars all stood the same height (dropped after the
  compare page). **The pace line** (owner, 2026-10-13: "it's a chart, but
  it's unclear — pace"): a faint dashed level across the bars, labelled
  at its right end in 10px (*usual $3.00/h*, *avg 1.50M*; `paceOf`,
  `lib/usual.ts`). For burn rate it is the viewer's usual, every active
  session-hour's cost over every active hour of the last 30 days
  (`usual.burn.mean`) — the mean, not the median the headline's delta
  sets a single hour against, because a bar is itself the mean of its
  hours and the distribution is skewed: on the owner's 30 days
  (2026-10-13) the median was $3.82/h and the mean $8.25/h, and against
  the median every day read two to four times usual. With fewer than 20
  such hours, or from a daemon that sends no mean, the period's own rate
  (*avg*). For cost, tokens and
  turns it is the period's average stretch over the stretches with any
  activity — an idle night is not a slow hour — because no 30-day usual
  per stretch exists (the daemon's are per session and per session-hour,
  and a stretch is neither). Fewer than two stretches with a value: no
  line. A line above every bar raises the scale to it. Bars above the line
  stand at the stronger opacity, bars below it at the muted one; amber
  only for a bar a usual puts in the top fifth of the viewer's own hours,
  the headline delta's own rule (`barPace`) — above an average is never
  amber, since it is nothing to act on. The folded strip draws the same
  line and tones. At rest no bar is singled
  out. Hovering a bar, or ←/→ (the first step lands on the newest stretch
  with any), reads that stretch on the same line: its figure takes the
  big figure's place in its own format (*$12.40*, *$4.14/h*, *950.1k*,
  *1,204*), a short date beside it (`barLabelShort`, *Sun 11 Oct
  · 06–12*) and at the right where it stands against the line, short
  enough for a 330px cockpit beside the date (*38% above usual*, *2.4×
  usual*, *about avg*; amber for a top-fifth bar) with the whole sentence
  on hover and for a screen reader (*38% above your usual*;
  `paceSentence`), or *of $1,299.44* when there is no line. The top line
  is a fixed 18px with an 18px line box for all it holds, at rest and under
  the pointer alike, so reading a stretch never moves the bars or anything
  below them by a pixel (owner, 2026-10-14: at rest the ▾ stood it 23px
  tall against 19px on hover, a 4px jump); for the session, how many
  calls and the three tools they asked for most are its tooltip and what a
  screen reader hears. Leaving the chart, or focus leaving it, puts the
  total back. The stretch's figure used to follow the total, the scope and
  a full date at the line's right, and in a 330px cockpit it was the part
  cut off (owner, 2026-10-13). For *session* the bars are every priced call
  (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/calls`, read once each time the chart is shown,
  plus any held call made since) summed by time — 5 min for a short
  session up to a day for a long one, the smallest step that keeps it to
  48 bars, hour steps and longer starting on the local hour (`timeBars`),
  so an idle stretch is an empty bar (owner, 2026-10-11: the per-call
  scrubber with token counts was "not informative and not convenient").
  today, 7d and 30d are Caprock-wide, from that range's
  `GET /v1/stats/summary` — totals, `savings.hit_rate`, turns, tool calls,
  and the projects' sparks (24 hours, 7 or 30 days) summed bucket by bucket
  into the bars (`periodFigures`, `lib/spendview.ts`), the sparks' `cost`,
  `input`, `turns`, `active_hours` and `active_cost` arrays alike; a
  daemon from before them sends cost only, and the chart then says it
  cannot draw that metric by time rather than drawing zeros. Today's is the
  summary the window already polls; 7d and 30d are read only while the
  Spend section shows them (unfolded or folded to its strip), every 30
  seconds. No new endpoint and no new query. Until a
  summary answers, the figures are dashes and the chart says *Reading 7
  days…*; a summary with no spark says so (*This range's summary has no
  spend by time to draw*) and a period with nothing spent says *Nothing
  spent*, never an empty dashed line. Everything in it has `min-w-0` down
  the chain and truncates, so it fits a 324px inspector with no sideways
  scroll. Then
  *Recent tools*, the uncommitted changes and the rest. In detail: *Now* — the open `tool.pre` without its `tool.post` (joined
  by `tool_use_id`, else the oldest open call of that tool; not "running"
  past 15 minutes) with its elapsed time, *Thinking* since the last call
  ended, or the narrated phrase; *Tasks* under it (`SessionTodos.tsx`,
  `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/todos`): the task list the agent keeps for itself,
  one row per task in 14 px type — an amber spinner on a tinted row while
  in progress, showing its `active_form` ("Running the tests"), a green
  check when done, a hollow circle still to do, a struck-through line when
  cancelled — and *3 of 7 done* in the header (cancelled tasks count as
  neither). Over eight tasks it folds to eight rows starting two before the
  first unfinished one, with *Show all N*. Not drawn at all when the
  session kept no list. Read on open, every 20 s while the window is
  visible, and a second after a task call of the main thread arrives on
  the live socket; *Background agents working · N* in the
  state line and *Now* while the turn has ended and subagents still work
  (`activity.background`; the state line itself says *Background agents*,
  the full phrase on hover, at most two lines under the portrait), which is not waiting on you
  and never in the inbox, ⌘J or a waiting badge; on the hero's last row,
  across its whole width, *N working* and then
  one mini-avatar per subagent from `/subagents`
  (`subagentChips`): its type's initial in a tint fixed per type
  (general-purpose, Explore and Plan one each, any other by a hash of its
  name), a pulsing ring while it works, accent while it waits on you, muted
  once finished; in the order they started, so a chip keeps its place
  while others start and finish (a finished one stays where it was, muted),
