blocks and the Pipeline page showed the same. Its parts went to the
Pipeline (a PR's jobs, steps, failed log and *Investigate*; the default
branch and the Actions bill, *Default branch* below) and to the cockpit's
CI rows (the per-PR actions, the agent cost and agent; § CI in the cockpit).
Its *Branches* view went with it. ⌘B is free: matched by no command on any
OS (`lib/appkeys.ts`), listed nowhere. Its kept state, `caprock.app.deck`,
is ignored; *Reset the view* still clears it and `caprock.deck.*`. The
poller and the small parts other views used moved out: `usePoll` and its
cadences (`CI_RUNNING_MS`, `CI_IDLE_MS`, `PRS_PENDING_MS`) to
`lib/usePoll.ts`; `CIMark`, `DeckSkeleton`, `ExpandToggle` and
`connectGitHub` to `components/CIParts.tsx`. History is in
[21-app.md](21-app.md) § Session deck — removed.

What the CI views share: checks summed up in one short group
(`components/CheckSummary.tsx`, `prChecks` in `lib/ci.ts`: *✗ 1 failed ● 1
running ✓ 8 · 5m*, *✓ all 12 · 6m*, every check on hover), the GitLab-style
mini pipeline (`components/MiniPipeline.tsx`, `jobNodes` in `lib/ci.ts`: a
circle per job, `.pipe-node`, a click opening it), a job's state mark
(`CIMark`: a turning amber ring while it runs, `.ci-spin`), each run and
job against the median of the workflow's last five successful runs
(`vsMedian`, `runVsMedian`, `jobVsMedian`): *+18%* coloured past +20% or
below −10%, *≈ 3m left* or *+12% over median* while running, nothing with
fewer than two past runs; and the runner cost (`lib/runnercost.ts`:
*$0.31*, *$0 · public repo*, *unpriced*, *≥$0.42*, the rate table's version
and source in the tooltip).

**The Pipeline's Default branch panel** (`components/PipelineMain.tsx`, at
the foot of the page; the deck's *Main* view until 2026-10-20): a line per
workflow on the default branch — its mark, name (a link to the run), mini
pipeline, time and cost — and, for a failed job (the first, or the one
picked on a mini pipeline), its step, the end of its log
(`…/ci/jobs/{job}/log?view=main`) and *Investigate*. Its header says what
GitHub billed for Actions this month (`usageLine`; *token settings ↗* when
the token may not read billing). Asked while the page is in front
(`main:<repository>`, the usage per owner); drawn nothing while GitHub is
not connected or the folder has no GitHub repository.

**It opens on what it last knew** (owner, 2026-10-13, translated: "for a
while the PR data is loading and nothing is shown, it looks frozen and
empty"). Each view's last answer is kept (`lib/deckCache.ts`): the PRs and
a PR's CI per session, Release and Main per repository, Branches per
folder, in memory for the app's run and in localStorage through `lib/swr`
(group `deck:`, the newest 24), so opening a view or a fresh start of the
app draws rows at once while `usePoll` asks again.
The header says how fresh they are, quietly, in one line and always with a
time (`components/Freshness.tsx`; owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "we need
an indicator that it's updating — like 'wait' — so I understand whether
this data is real or cached"):

- **Fresh** — *Updated just now*, *Updated 12 s ago*, ticking, from the
  answer's `fetched_at` (the Pipeline: `read_at`, when GitHub was last
  read).
- **Being read again** — *Saved 5 h ago · refreshing…* while what is shown
  is the copy kept from before and not yet re-confirmed this time, *Updated
  2 min ago · refreshing…* while the daemon composes a newer one
  (`refreshing`, asked again 1.5 s later); a faint pulsing dot beside it
  (still under reduced motion).
- **Not refreshed** — *Couldn't refresh: <reason>. Showing data from 6 min
  ago. Retry* when the read failed, the daemon's newest composition failed
  (`refresh_error`), or GitHub refused the list it keeps (`error` with
  `read_at`); the rows stay. The age is in words (`agoText`); the exact time
  and the raw reason are in the tooltip. In a narrow header (`compact`)
  the reason moves to the tooltip. *Retry* asks again at once.
- **Couldn't reach GitHub** (owner, 2026-10-15: *Couldn't refresh: could
  not reach GitHub (api.github.com): context deadline exceeded. Check the
  connection; Caprock tries again later · showing data from 17:27* said
  nothing he could act on). When the failure is on the way to GitHub or
  GitHub's own — a timeout, no route, a 5xx (`isReachFailure` in
  `lib/githubOutage.ts`) — the line is *Couldn't reach GitHub. Showing data
  from 6 min ago. Retry*, the raw error in the tooltip, and then whose
  fault it is: *Checking GitHub Status…*, then either *GitHub reports:
  Partial outage — <the incident>* with *Open githubstatus.com* (the
  incident's page, through the app's `open_external` like every outside
  link), or *GitHub reports all systems operational — likely your
  connection.* The daemon reads the status page (`GET
  /v1/github/statuspage`, kept five minutes), never the browser, and only
  while GitHub is connected; the dashboard asks it only after such a
  failure ([ADR-066](08-decisions.md)). In `compact` headers it is a few
  words (*GitHub reports an outage ↗*, *likely your connection*) with the
  sentence in the tooltip.
- **Watch GitHub Status** — a tick at the end of that line, off by default
  and this viewer's (browser storage). Ticked, the dashboard asks the
  status page every five minutes while GitHub is connected, and the status
  bar (the app's status strip; the header beside *Live* in a browser)
  shows a small amber dot and *GitHub* — red for a major or critical
  outage — only while GitHub reports an incident; a click opens it
  (`GitHubStatusDot` in `components/GitHubOutage.tsx`).

Never a bare *updating…*: on 2026-10-14 it sat for hours over a board from
the afternoon, merged pull requests still in *CI running* and three releases
missing, because every composition overran its deadline and nothing said
so. The Pipeline draws its rows faint (`data-prs-unconfirmed`)
while they are not re-confirmed — the copy kept from before, an answer that
could not be refreshed, or a card whose checks the daemon could not read
again in time (`stale`). The CI/CD section on Now says the same in its
panel header. A read whose answer lost what the
shown one had (an error with no release, no runs, no PRs per branch) is
not taken over it. The very first read of a view ever draws three grey
rows in the list's shape (`DeckSkeleton`), never an empty area. Nothing is
asked while the window is hidden; it asks once shown. The daemon keeps the
composed answer too ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md) § the deck's
answers are kept), so the read itself is a local one.

**The Pipeline** (owner, 2026-10-13; renamed from *PRs & CI* and made one
list on 2026-10-14; a page of the dashboard since 2026-10-15 — owner,
translated: "Here everything reads very, very badly. I'd build this into the
Dashboard as a separate page, opened there — Pipeline, simply in the
Dashboard's style … Pipeline is part of the Dashboard, just a separate
page"; and later that day, translated: "I want the Pipeline shown in the
dashboard too, and as a separate page with its own tab — among the system
tabs, next to the dashboard"). It is the route
`#/pipeline[?repo=<folder>&of=<session>&pr=<n>]` — `pr` opens that pull
request's detail beside the list as the page opens (a Work row's title
links there) — (`screens/Pipeline.tsx`, drawn by
`components/PipelineView.tsx`), in the dashboard's nav between Week and
Memory. In a browser it is a page of the dashboard, kept mounted once
opened like the other screens (`KEPT_SCREENS`). In the app `#/pipeline` is
the **Pipeline tab**, a pinned system tab right after the Dashboard tab and
before Mail (`pipeline` in `TabStrip`), folded like them to its glyph
(`PipelineIcon`) and its key (⌘P; Ctrl+Shift+P off macOS, while Ctrl is held), its × shown while it
is in front. The dashboard's nav entry, Home's *Open Pipeline →* and a Work
row's title are links to `#/pipeline…`, so inside the Dashboard tab they
switch to the Pipeline tab: the page is drawn once, there, never inside the
Dashboard tab (`pipelineShown` in AppShell excludes it from
`dashboardShown`). The tab is kept mounted while it is in the strip and,
hidden, asks nothing (`ScreenShown`, PipelineView's `visible`); it is kept
across restarts as `pipeline` in the stored workspace, as Mail's is, and
brings back the address last shown in it. ⌘P (⇧⌘B until 2026-10-15, still
working, no longer shown), the sidebar footer's *Pipeline*, the palette's
*Open Pipeline* or the cockpit's *Pipeline →* (and a cockpit row's *Log in
Pipeline →*, with `pr`) bring the Pipeline tab to the front
(`openPipeline` in AppShell): for the agent in front (`of`), else where it
was left. From it, ⌘P, *Back ⌘P* in its head (`ExpandToggle collapse`,
`data-prs-back`) or the footer's button go back to the tab or screen before
it — however it was reached, the nav and Home's card included — else to the
terminals (`pipelineBack`); its × does the same and takes it out of the
strip. Ctrl+Shift+P off macOS (the older ⇧⌘B is Ctrl+Alt+Shift+B there,
where Ctrl+Shift+B was the deck's until 2026-10-20). A Pipeline
*pane* tab stored before 2026-10-15 (`kind: 'prs'`) is not restored as a
pane: the Pipeline tab takes its place and opens at that Pipeline
(`pipelineInStore`, `parseWorkspace` in `lib/tabs.ts`); an app opened at
`#/pipeline`, the Dashboard tab's address for a day, opens in the Pipeline
tab.

**Which repository.** The page shows one, picked in its head beside the
title — the dashboard's filter pills, a green dot on one with an agent
running, the first five as pills and the rest in a *N more…* list
(`RepoPicker`). The repositories are the git repositories an agent session
worked in (`pipelineRepos` in `lib/pipeline.ts`; shells have no pull
requests), each read through its newest agent session, since the daemon's
routes are a session's. Only a pipeline is listed (owner, 2026-10-15,
translated: two "caprock" entries, the second "This folder is not a git
repository with a remote"): a folder outside git — no `repo_root`, such as a
copy in Downloads, a quick chat's folder or the paste folder — never is, and
`GET /v1/repos/remotes` (`lib/useRepoRemotes.ts`, asked again only when the
set of folders changes, kept in this browser) drops a repository with no
remote or one on another host and folds clones of one GitHub repository into
one entry (its tooltip names owner/name and every folder). A folder macOS
guards is not read and stays listed. Two entries that still read the same
are told apart by owner/name, else by parent folder and name. The *N more…*
list is the same filtered list. Shown: the one the address names (any
folded clone finds its entry), else that of the agent it was opened from,
else the one shown last (`caprock.pipeline.repo`), else the first; opened
from an agent in a folder that is no pipeline, the page says so and leaves
the pick to the picker. The app hands the page the sessions it holds, the worktrees an agent
can be started in for a fix, a toast and the way back
(`lib/pipelineHost.ts`); in a browser the page reads the session list itself
every minute.

**In the dashboard's style.** On top, a *Flow · owner/repo* panel of Stat
tiles (`FlowFigures` in `components/PRBoard.tsx`, the tiles Home's Today
row uses): *In flight* (with CI running, drafts), *Needs a fix* (red when
any), *Ready* (green when any), *Throughput · 14d*, *CI median*, *Opened →
merged*, *Merged → released* (an estimate), *Agent cost*; eight across from
1180px of panel, four, then two. Each figure says what it means in a card
under its tile on hover or keyboard focus (`Stat hint`, `data-stat-hint`),
never the native tooltip, which opened under the pointer over the very
figure it explained.

**The Flow row is three cards** (owner, 2026-10-18, variant B of a mockup:
ten equal tiles read as a wall; `FlowCards` in `components/PRBoard.tsx`,
`AgentsCard` in `components/AgentFigures.tsx`), one main figure each; Home's
CI/CD panel keeps its five tiles (`FlowFigures`). The paragraphs below say
what each figure means; here they sit in three cards, stacked on a narrow
panel (under 760px, so a phone neither scrolls sideways nor clips):

- **Now** — with nothing in flight, red or waiting, a green dot and *All
  clear* over *0 in flight · 0 red · 0 waiting*. Otherwise the most urgent
  count big (*needs a fix* in the danger colour first, else *in flight*,
  else *ready*) over the other two as a muted line. Each count keeps what
  its tile had: `data-stat="flight|fix|ready"`, a toggle for the list's
  filter, its pull requests in the hover card.
- **Speed · 14 days** — throughput (*3.2/day*) over a sparkline when there
  is history, then the mono line *CI 8m · PR→merge 9m · merge→release
  ~1h*; each part carries its old sentence as a title, the card's hover the
  four, with *estimate* on the release lag.
- **Agents · 14 days** — the agent cost, a sparkline when there is history,
  then *$13.85/h · 2 now · peak 7*: the last 24 hours' cost per agent-hour
  and the team now and today's peak, the latter a button for the team
  chart. The hover keeps *at API list price · estimate*, tokens per hour
  and the team's definition.

A card has no "not enough history" line of its own: a sparkline is drawn
only from three earlier days, and when the throughput or cost one is
missing the row says so once, small, under it (`data-flow-missing`). The
words *usual*, *above usual* and the other series (CI, opened to merged,
release lag) are no longer drawn on the page.

**A count opens the pull requests behind it** (owner, 2026-10-15,
translated: "I see the red 'NEEDS A FIX 4' figure, but I can't see which
PRs those are or whether they were fixed"; `lib/flowbucket.ts`). *In
flight*, *Needs a fix* and *Ready* are buttons (`Stat onClick`,
`aria-pressed`). Their hover or keyboard-focus card lists up to five of
the pull requests the tile counts — number, title cut to one line, why
(*CI red*, *Conflict*, *Changes requested*; *CI running*, *Draft*, *CI
green* for the others) — then *and N more*; *Needs a fix* adds *N fixed
in the last 24h* when the page saw that many leave it. That is noted per
repository from the answers the page already reads
(`caprock.pipeline.fixSeen:<repo>`, `noteFixes`): a pull request seen in
Needs a fix and then in another stage left it then; one closed is not
fixed, one red again is back in. No read is added for it, so it counts
only what this browser saw. A click filters the list to the tile's pull
requests, shows the chip *Needs a fix · 4 ×* over it (`data-prs-filter`)
with *across the repository*, and scrolls the list into view. From *This
session* the list widens to *Repository* first — the others' pull
requests are where a count's usually are — and narrows back when the
filter clears, by a second click on the tile or the chip's ×. In that
view each row's CI pill says why (*CI red* with the failed checks named in
its tooltip from the card's `failed`, *Conflict*, *Changes requested*),
beside the labels — a landing label among them — and the author as on any
row.
A *Share* pill in the head draws these figures as a card to save, copy,
post or send to Telegram — anonymous unless *Show repo name* is ticked for
that share (§ The share sheet, *The Pipeline card*).

**Next release** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "I'm missing progress
bars to the release here"). Under Flow, a foldable *Next release* panel
(`PipelineRelease` in `components/ReleasePlan.tsx`, `fold`
`pipeline-next-release`) draws the page's repository's release plan
([§ Release plan](#release-plan)): the next version with its bump and why,
the release's stages as pills (merged → release PR → CI → tag → builds →
published → site, § Release plan, *The release status*), then a row per pull
request merged since the last tag, open, or agent at work with no pull
request yet, each with its stage bar and word, what it waits for, its
agent cost, its CI per OS, its CI time and its age as the panel's width
allows, and under the rows one line for the last release. A row opens its pull
request's detail beside the list, as the list's rows do. It reads the
page's own Pipeline answer and `GET /v1/work` (for the tag, the changelog
entries' kinds and the agents) — once as the page comes in front, again
only on a `github` or `reset` frame (`changesRelease`), nothing while the
page is hidden. Deploys may join it in the Teams version later (owner,
2026-10-15); nothing of them is drawn now.

**The figures in dynamics** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "I want the
figures in dynamics, like everywhere else in our app — the metric, plus a
small chart, and above/below the average indicator"). Under each figure
with a history, the Today cells' line and word (`FlowTrend` in
`components/PRBoard.tsx`, drawn by the same `TrendLine` as `TodayTrend`;
`lib/flowtrend.ts`): an 18px faint line of the window's 14 local days,
today a dot at its right end, a day with no sample a gap (a day with one
neighbour-less sample a small dot), and under it *usual*, *above usual
+38%*, *below usual −30%* or *2.4×*. The series are the daemon's
(`board.flow.series`, measured from what the board already read; no new
GitHub request). How today is set against the previous 13 days:

- **In flight** — the open count now against the median of each earlier
  day's count at its end (from the list's opened, merged and closed
  stamps); a dashed usual across the line.
- **Throughput, Agent cost** — today so far against the earlier days cut
  at the same time of day (`by_now`), over the days with a merge, so a
  morning never reads low against whole days; no dashed line, since the
  line is whole days and the usual is by this hour. Agent cost's series is
  the cost of the pull requests merged each day.
- **CI median, Opened → merged, Merged → released** — today's median
  against the median of the earlier days' medians, over the days with a
  sample; the dashed usual across. With no sample today the word is *no run
  yet today*, *no merge yet today* or *no release yet today*, never
  *below*. Merged → released is counted by the day the release was
  published, so a merge not yet released sits in no day.

With fewer than three earlier days with data the word is *not enough
history* and nothing is compared. Colour says better or worse only where
one direction is: a shorter CI run, merge or release lag and more merges
are green, the other side amber; *In flight* and *Agent cost* stay muted
either way. Hovering a day says it (*Tue 13 Oct · 30m, median of 3
merges*); hovering the word says the basis in full (*5m 00s today · vs the
median of the daily medians, last 14 days: 7m 10s, on the 13 days with a
successful run*). **Needs a fix** and **Ready** have no line: whether a
pull request was red or green on a past day is not in anything read, a
check's state being its head commit's now.

**The agent figures** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "In Pipeline I'm
missing the cost of an hour of work. I'd also like to see roughly the size
of this agent team on a chart: how many there are and were … like
contributors"). Two more tiles end the Flow row, which goes from eight
columns to two rows of five, then ten from 1280px of panel
(`components/AgentFigures.tsx`, `lib/repoagents.ts`, from `GET
/v1/pipeline/agents` for every folder of the picked repository, read once a
minute while the page is in front, kept as `agents:<folders>`). They are
measured from this machine's own sessions, not from GitHub:

- **Cost / agent-hour** — *$6.10/h*: the last 24 hours' cost per active
  agent-hour, at API list price, labelled *at API list price · estimate*.
  An agent-hour is the burn rate's own rule: one session's clock hour with
  five or more priced calls, its subagents included (`usual.burn`). It is
  the big figure rather than the cost per wall-clock hour because four
  agents at once are four hours of work, not one hour four times as dear;
  how many ran at once is the team tile's to say. The wall-clock rate is in
  the tile's hint. Under it the line of the last 15 days' rates (a day with
  no agent-hour a gap; the dashed level the usual) and the caption *▲ 35%
  vs 14-day avg*, *▼ 12% vs 14-day avg* or *≈ 14-day avg* — the last 24
  hours against the 14×24 hours before them, each pooled (all the hours'
  cost over their count), muted: a cost has no better direction here. With
  fewer than three of those days having an agent-hour, *not enough
  history*; with no agent-hour in 24 hours, *no hour of work in 24h*. The
  secondary line: tokens per agent-hour and in all, *1.45M tok/h · 3.00M
  in 24h*.
- **Agent team** — *5 now*: main sessions and subagents with a turn in the
  last ten minutes, then *2 + 3 sub · peak 7 today* (sessions + subagents
  now; the day's total in its tooltip), the peak being the most agents with a turn in any five minutes today.
  The line is distinct agents a day over 15 days, the caption *avg 24 ·
  peak 66* — the mean over the earlier days anyone worked, and the
  busiest of them; *not enough history* below three such days. Hovering a
  day splits it by agent kind (*Claude Code 1 + 36 sub · Codex 1*). The
  tile is a button: pressed, an **Agent team · last 31 days** panel opens
  under the Flow panel (`data-team-chart`), a stacked bar a day — sessions
  in the accent at the bottom, subagents in a lighter accent above, a 2px
  gap between — a legend in its header, a × to close, the hovered day's
  split over the bars, and the same figures as a table for screen readers.

Then a **Pull requests** panel, the three scopes as the
dashboard's pill switch in its header (`tabRows`, kept per viewer under
`caprock.prsTab.scope`) and a column header (*Pull request · Open → CI →
Merged · In step · Agent cost*). A row (`data-prs-row`,
`data-stage`): the number, the **whole** title at 14px (never clipped),
label chips (a dot in GitHub's colour, from `BranchPR.labels`), the author,
*this session*; the three steps as pills (`JourneyPills` in
`components/Journey.tsx`, `data-journey-step`, `data-step-state`), each
state told by its shape as well as its colour: a step passed is text with a
green ✓, the step it is on a filled pill in its tone (a ✗ when it failed, a
turning ring while CI runs), a step still ahead a dashed outline; then how
long it has been on that step and its agent cost. Below 820px of list the
title takes its own line and the steps and cost go under it. The steps and
the order come from one pure function (`journey`, `journeyOrder` in
`lib/journey.ts`): in flight — fix, CI running, ready, draft — then merged
and not released, then released (ten, then *Show N more released*,
`data-prs-more`), then closed. **Merged is the last step everywhere**
(owner, 2026-10-20, translated: in the CI/PR progress bars the final status
should be merged — a merged pull request's grey last block read as
unfinished, or closed): a merge, released or not, is the filled *Merged*
pill, and whether it shipped is a quiet word after the pills
(`releaseNote` in `lib/journey.ts`, `ReleaseNoteWord`,
`data-release-note`): *in v0.132.0* once a published release holds it;
a fainter *not released* while it waits, where the repository cuts
releases (`board.ships_by` `release`; the hover names the predicted next
version, or the last release); nothing where it ships on merge or where
nothing read says. A pull request closed unmerged stays distinct: its last
pill reads *Closed*, its CI step never filled. A **Releases** panel under
the list.
Captions and secondary text are the dashboard's muted grey at 12px, not the
faint grey the tab used.

A click on a row opens its detail **beside** the list from 1100px of page,
held in view as the page scrolls (`sticky`), and under it on a narrower one
(`data-prs-detail`, `role="dialog"`): large pills, the four force actions
(`lib/usePRForce.ts`), the job × runner grid (`jobGrid` in
`lib/prstats.ts`), the timeline, the steps and log tail of the chosen job;
× or Esc closes it, and its CI is polled only while it is open. Checked in
a browser at 390, 1280 (the app, sidebar open) and 1440 px, light and dark.
It polls through `usePoll` (`lib/usePoll.ts`) and keeps its answers in
`lib/deckCache` (`stats:<repository>` beside `prs:`, `ci:` and `release:`), so it opens
on what was last known; nothing is asked while it is not in front or the
window is hidden.
The whole description is in [21-app.md](21-app.md) § Pipeline.

**Investigate** (owner, 2026-10-11, translated: "I see CI failed on #428 — I
want, right from here, one button to add it to the queue for
investigation"). Under a failed job's log on the Pipeline — a PR's
detail, and the Default branch panel — one button queues a message into the session in front through the
daemon's queue (`POST /v1/agents/{id}/queue`, [ADR-053](08-decisions.md)): it
types in at the prompt, else when the turn ends. The message names the PR
(number, title, link), the workflow, job and failing step, the job's link,
the last 40 lines of the log tail (cut from the top to stay under 12 KiB, so
the error at the end stays), and ends "Investigate why this CI step failed
and fix it; push to the same branch." (`investigateMessage`). From the
Default branch panel it names the default branch, with the run's link, in
place of the PR, and ends "Investigate why
this CI step failed and fix it." A toast says
*Queued for <session title>*, or *Sent to …* when it typed in at once, or
why it was not queued. The button is drawn disabled, its tooltip saying why,
for a session Caprock did not start (rule 7: never type into a process
Caprock did not start), one that ended or detached, and on a paired device
that may only watch (`useCanControl`).

**Force actions on a PR** (owner, 2026-10-12; on the deck's rows until
2026-10-20). The Pipeline's PR detail offers *Fix conflict with agent*,
*Update branch*, *Fix CI with agent* and *Re-run checks*, each shown only
when it applies and drawn disabled with the reason in its tooltip; a
cockpit CI row of the session's own PR offers the one it needs (§ CI in the
cockpit). A click turns the button into a confirm — the sentence saying who
gets what, *Confirm* (focused, Enter) and *Cancel* (Esc) — and a confirmed
action shows its progress in the row (*agent fixing…*), kept per session
for the app's run (`prProgress`) and gone once GitHub shows it done
(`progressStands`). The fix goes to the Caprock-started agent on the
branch, else (Fix CI) the session in front, else a new agent
(`onStartAgent`, from AppShell through `PipelineHostContext`, opening its
tab) in the branch's checkout or a new `pr-N-fix` worktree at the PR's
head; the messages are `fixConflictMessage`, `investigateMessage` and
`fixCIMessage`. A refusal is a toast naming the token permission
(`forceErrText`). The whole description is in [21-app.md](21-app.md) § CI
in the inspector and the Pipeline, The actions.

### CI in the cockpit

Beside an agent the cockpit has a **CI** block under Changes
(`components/CockpitCI.tsx`), owner 2026-10-13, translated: "move some
CI/CD indicators into the right part — try making the indicator switch
between views right away, we'll see what sticks". It is one of the
cockpit's sections, so it folds on its label and moves in Arrange mode
like the rest (§ Inspector). Three views, switched by three small tabs in
its label (*PRs · Ring · By OS*), each named in full on hover and to a
screen reader (owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "OS mode is unclear"), and
kept per viewer (`readCockpitCIView`, `caprock.cockpit.ciView`):

- **PRs** (the default) — the repository's pull requests as thin progress
  bars toward a release (owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "roughly the
  current size, showing PRs as progress bars toward release; scrollable;
  we see PR statuses and CI statuses; from there you can expand to a bigger
  block, and open the separate page"): each row its number, its title, how
  long it has been on its step, and under them the three steps as a bar
  (`JourneyBar`, `data-journey-bar`, its `aria-label` naming every step),
  the step it is on coloured — the CI step in its check colour, red, amber
  or green — in the Pipeline's order and from the same `journey`. Above
  the rows one faint caption line names the steps over their columns,
  *open · CI · merged* (`JourneyLegend`, `data-journey-legend`, the
  bar's own grid; owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "here it's
  not clear what the stages are, but it's almost perfect"), so no row
  grows; each segment has its own tooltip — the step, its state in one word
  (done, running, failed, waiting; stopped where a closed PR stopped), what
  the step says when that differs (*CI red*, *Closed*) and, on the step it
  is on, a landing-queue label (*CI: failed — CI red · landing: queued.*),
  over a hover target taller than the 4px it draws. It reads
  the Pipeline's answer (`…/ci/stats`, kept as `stats:<repository>`) while
  the block is shown unfolded. It lists only what is in flight (owner,
  2026-10-16, translated: "on the right it sometimes shows too many PRs,
  e.g. for caprock-web"; `inFlight` in `lib/journey.ts`): the open pull
  requests and the ones merged since the newest published release, still
  heading to one; released and closed-unmerged ones are dropped. A
  repository that publishes no release — caprock-web pushes its changelog
  syncs straight to master and has no release, so all of its 48 listed
  merges counted as "merged, waiting for a release" — has only its open
  ones in flight. With nothing in flight it says so in one quiet line,
  *Nothing in flight · last release v1.4.0, 1h ago* (or *· no release
  published*; `data-cockpit-ci-quiet`), over the three newest merges as
  context. **Merged is the last step** (owner, 2026-10-18, translated:
  "why isn't merged the final state — there's one more step after it";
  then 2026-10-20, translated: in the CI/PR progress bars the final status
  should be merged — the grey last block read as unfinished, or closed):
  a merge, released or not, fills every segment in the merged colour
  (`data-journey-full`) and its hover says *Merged: the last step*;
  released is never a segment. Whether it shipped is a quiet word beside
  its age (`releaseNote`, `ReleaseNoteWord`, `data-release-note`): *in
  v0.132.0* once released; a fainter *not released* where the board says
  the repository cuts releases (`board.ships_by` `release`), the hover
  saying it ships with the next release (*v0.133.0* where predicted, else
  after the last); nothing where it ships on merge (`merge`: no release
  published and no version tag in 180 days, both read — caprock-web, a
  site deployed on every merge) or where nothing read says (`ships_by`
  absent: a read failed, an older daemon) — never a wait. A pull request
  closed unmerged keeps its CI segment empty and its last one muted,
  *Closed*. Where work ships on merge the quiet line reads *Nothing in
  flight · no releases here: work ships when it merges*
  (`data-cockpit-ci-quiet` `merge`; `journey`, `shipsOf`, `STEP_KEYS` in
  `lib/journey.ts`). The list scrolls inside the block (176px,
  `data-cockpit-journey`); past four rows *Expand* makes it 440px, kept per
  viewer (`caprock.cockpit.pipeline.tall`), and *Pipeline →* opens the page.
  **A row of the session's own PR** (matched by number in `prs:<session>`,
  which the block asks itself while unfolded — `…/prs`, every 20 s while a
  check runs, else every minute — since the deck that used to fill it went
  on 2026-10-20) carries a line under its bar: grey, its agent cost
  (`board.cards[].spend`, `costText`, the sessions in the tooltip) and who
  is on it (the live agent on its branch, from the host's worktrees, else
  the agents the board names); then the **one action it needs**
  (`data-cockpit-ci-action`, `quickActions`): *Resolve conflict* (red
  outline), *Update branch*, *Fix CI* — *Re-run* when Fix CI cannot be
  offered — a 16px outlined accent button, disabled with the reason in its
  tooltip on a device that may only watch; a click turns the line into the
  confirm (*Ask … to fix #9's CI and push?* · *Confirm* · *Cancel*), and
  once confirmed the row says *agent fixing…* until GitHub shows it done
  (`lib/usePRForce.ts`, the Pipeline's own). Beside it, on a failing row or
  one with an action, a quiet *Log in Pipeline →* (`data-cockpit-ci-log`)
  opens the Pipeline on that PR (`#/pipeline?of=…&pr=N`), where its failed
  job's log and *Investigate* are. A PR of another session has no action
  here; the Pipeline's detail has them.
- **Ring** — the newest PR's checks as one ring (passed green, failed red,
  running amber) with *10/11 · 5m 09s*, what failed or runs, and the run
  against its median when that PR's CI was read.
- **By OS** — the newest PR's checks by runner, one line each: *Linux ✓
  passed 1m 48s · 2 checks*, *Windows ● running 5m 08s* (`osRollup` in
  `lib/ci.ts`: per OS the worst state among its checks, their count and
  the longest one's time). It reads the OS from the checks the PR row
  already carries — the job's runner when that PR's CI was read, else the
  check's name (*go (windows-latest)*) — so it shows something without a
  PR being opened; it used to be a job × OS matrix that, until then, only
  said *#582's jobs show once its row is opened in the deck*. Checks that name no OS are left out, and when none does it
  says *#582's checks name no runner OS*.

**Hovering a PR's checks** (owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "you can't move
the mouse here — on hover a modal opens and you can't see anything"): the
list of every check opens only after the pointer has rested on the summary
for 600 ms (`BESIDE_DELAY_MS`, CheckSummary's `beside`), never while the
pointer passes over the rows; it stands outside the panel, left of the CI
section (`data-checks-edge`) and level with the row, so it never covers
the list; it takes no pointer and closes the moment the pointer leaves the
summary. Folded, the CI
section's label carries the newest PR's number and its check summary in
place of the tabs.

The *Ring* and *By OS* views draw the session's own newest PR when it was
read with its checks (`lib/deckCache`, `prs:<session>` and `ci:<session>:<pr>`,
redrawn when either is told something new, `onDeckAnswer`). Without one
they used to be an empty box (owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "in CI/CD on
the right, the Ring mode is empty"): now they draw the newest PR in flight
of the same `…/ci/stats` answer the *PRs* view reads — the newest open one,
else the newest merge since the last release (`flightLead`) — and ask that
PR's checks themselves (`…/ci?pr=`, kept as `ci:<session>:<pr>`) while
shown unfolded, saying *Reading #12's checks…* until they come. With
nothing in flight they say the same one line as the *PRs* view. Each says
how old it is; the block is absent only when neither the session's pull
requests nor the Pipeline's answer has anything with GitHub connected. A clock ticks only
while a check runs and the window is shown. A click opens the Pipeline.

## Kept figures (stale-while-revalidate)

The slow figures show the last answer this browser saw while the fresh one is
read (`ui/src/lib/swr.ts`, the `cache` option of `useApi`). Owner report,
2026-10-04: Now sat on dashes while the daemon was busy.

- **What is kept** — Now's Today, plan limits, all-time and Projects; the
  Lifetime screen; History; and per session, the header figures (events and
  files dropped), for the 30 most recently viewed sessions; the CI views'
  answers (`lib/deckCache`), the newest 24 (`deck:`, § CI on the Pipeline
  and in the cockpit). Never event streams or terminal data: those are about now.
- **How it is marked** — "updated N min ago · refreshing" beside the panel,
  and nothing about now (health, burn, controls, the active count, the
  timeline) is drawn from a kept copy.
- **Limits** — one entry per endpoint and query, the latest answer only; an
  answer over 200,000 characters is not kept; the total stays under 1,000,000
  by dropping the oldest. The key prefix carries a version, and entries under
  another version are deleted on load. Every storage access is in try/catch,
  so a private window or a full quota only means no copy.

**The socket carries two things, told apart by frame type.** Binary is what the
user typed, written to the PTY byte for byte. Text is a control message —
today only `{"resize":{"cols":N,"rows":N}}`. See
[03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md).

**Size is sent, not assumed.** `fit()` resizes the canvas and nothing else, and
for a long time nothing told the daemon: `Resize` was declared on the agents
interface and called from nowhere, so a PTY kept the size it was born with —
120×40 by default — for its whole life. Claude Code lays its menus out to the
terminal size, so on any other window it drew an interface for a screen the
user did not have, and arrow keys moved a selection nobody could see. Reported
as *"only Enter works"*, which is what that looks like from outside.

**Every newline key sends `ESC CR` (`1b 0d`)** — Shift+Enter, Option+Enter,
Ctrl+Enter and Ctrl+J all send it. It is what a terminal sends for Alt+Enter,
and what Claude Code's own macOS instructions tell people to bind Option+Enter
to.

Getting there took four wrong answers, and the reason they survived is worth
more than the value itself: **three of them were tested on an empty prompt,
where a newline key that submits and a newline key that adds a line look
identical.** With text already typed, the difference is the whole feature.

The four attempts, in the order they were made:

- **CSI u** (`ESC [ 13 ; 2 u`) is what a terminal sends *after* negotiating the
  kitty keyboard protocol. This one writes into a PTY directly and never
  negotiates, so the sequence did nothing at all.
- **A bare line feed** (`0x0A`, what Ctrl+J sends) is read by Claude Code as
  *submit*. The user who reported it described the tell exactly: on an empty
  prompt it looked like a newline, because there was nothing to submit, and the
  moment he typed anything the same key sent his message.

The lesson is cheaper than the two rounds it cost: when a product configures a
terminal for itself, read that configuration instead of inferring the protocol
from its documentation.

Backslash-then-Enter needs nothing from us: the backslash is already in the
line and Claude Code reads the pair itself.

**Copy and paste follow VS Code's rule**, because it is the one people already
have in their fingers. **Ctrl+C copies when something is selected and
interrupts when nothing is** — a person who has just dragged across output
means copy; a person who has not means stop. On macOS the question never
arises, since Cmd+C and Ctrl+C are different keys, so Ctrl+C stays SIGINT
there whatever is selected. Ctrl+Shift+C/V elsewhere, deliberately distinct
from Ctrl+C so interrupting keeps its key.
Keys are matched by the physical key (`e.code`): on a Russian layout Cmd+C
reports a Cyrillic letter, so matching the letter never copied and the
unhandled key made macOS beep (owner, 2026-10-11). A copy takes the event
(`preventDefault`) so the browser's own copy, which finds nothing selected in
xterm's textarea, does not beep, and says so: *Copied · N chars* in the
terminal's corner for a second (`flashCopied`).

**A paste is the browser's own event, handled by xterm, and never a clipboard
read of ours.** Cmd+V and Ctrl+Shift+V return `false` from the key handler so
xterm does not send `^V`, and the browser's paste reaches xterm's textarea,
which applies bracketed paste when the process asked for it — a multi-line
paste has to arrive as one paste, not as N submitted lines. Until 2026-09-29 a
paste key *also* read the clipboard and called `term.paste()`; returning
`false` does not cancel the browser's paste, so every paste went out twice
whenever the clipboard read was allowed (FB-034). The native path needs no
clipboard permission either, which Safari asks for on every read.

**A terminal attached late still knows the modes the session set.** The daemon
replays the last 256 KB of output to a terminal that connects, and Claude Code
turns bracketed paste on once, at startup — measured: 182 KB of redraws with no
second `ESC[?2004h`. Past that point a reattached terminal (a tab switch, a
reload) had bracketed paste off, so a long paste went in raw and was not
collapsed. The output buffer remembers DEC private modes 1 (cursor keys), 25
(cursor), 1000/1002/1003 (mouse tracking, one at a time as in xterm), 1004
(focus), 1006 (SGR mouse reports), 1049 (alternate screen) and 2004 (bracketed
paste), and sends them ahead of the snapshot (`internal/termbuf/modes.go`):
the modes in force where the held bytes start, resets before sets, so each
switch the bytes make replays where the output made it.

**The wheel in an alternate-screen app goes to the app.** Codex (codex-cli
0.161) runs on the alternate screen, owns its transcript there and asks for
SGR mouse reports; xterm sends it each wheel turn as a report and Codex
scrolls. Until 2026-10-18 the screen and mouse modes were not in the list
above: once Codex's startup had scrolled out of the 256 KB ring — it redraws
constantly, as Claude Code does — a terminal attached after that (a reload, a
reconnect, a hidden tab that fell behind the ring) sat on the normal screen
with the mouse off. The wheel moved xterm's own scrollback, which a
full-screen app never fills, and Codex never heard it: "in Codex sessions
scrolling does not work" (owner, translated, 2026-10-18). Claude Code draws on
the normal screen into real scrollback, so it never showed. With no mouse
tracking asked for, xterm still turns the wheel on the alternate screen into
arrow keys, as terminals with alternate-scroll do.

**WebGL where the machine has it.** The canvas renderer repaints the whole
grid; the WebGL one draws from a texture atlas, which is the difference
between a build log scrolling and the tab stuttering. Every failure path falls
back to canvas: no WebGL, a driver that refuses, or a context lost on
sleep/wake. A slower terminal is a cost; a blank one is a broken product —
and that fallback is tested by making the mock throw the way the real xterm
does, after a first attempt whose mock silently never fired because the real
addon class minifies to a one-letter name.

Scrollback is 10,000 lines: a build log passes 5,000 easily, and losing the
start of what you are reading is where a terminal stops being one you can work
in.

**Paste or drop files and Claude Code can read them.** A browser hands over a
file's bytes and never a path — there is no path for something copied out of a
screenshot tool, and a file dragged from Finder arrives as a name and contents
— while Claude Code reads files by path. So each file goes to `POST /v1/paste`
with its name, the daemon writes it to `<data_dir>/paste/<stamp>-<random>/`
under that name, sanitised, and the path it returns is typed into the session,
quoted (a macOS data directory contains "Application Support", and unquoted
that is two arguments) and followed by a space — an image is pasted instead,
below. Claude reads `contract.pdf`,
not a timestamp.

Every dropped or pasted file is sent, one at a time and in order, so several
paths land in the order they were dropped. The daemon accepts images, PDF,
text, Office documents, data and source files by **extension**, because a
browser leaves the type empty for Markdown, CSV, JSON and source files (the
list and the naming rules: [03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)). A refused or
failed file prints a yellow `[caprock: <name>: …]` line naming what is
accepted, and the rest still go. Until 2026-10-04 only the first file was sent
and only by MIME type, so a Markdown file dragged from Finder came back
"415 Unsupported Media Type".

**An image becomes `[Image #N]`, not a path** (owner, 2026-10-11: people who
paste an image do not want to see its path). Claude Code attaches an image
only when its path arrives as a *paste* — that is how a file dragged into
Terminal or iTerm turns into `[Image #1]` there. Measured on 2026-10-11 against
the real `claude` 2.1.296 in a PTY, writing candidate bytes to the prompt and
reading the screen, nothing submitted:

- **Attached (`[Image #1]`)**: a bracketed paste (`ESC[200~ … ESC[201~`) of one
  image path, in every form tried — raw, double-quoted, single-quoted,
  backslash-escaped spaces; a space in the folder and in the name; an inner
  `\"`; `.PNG` in capitals, `.jpg`, `.gif`.
- **Left as text**: the same path written without the brackets, in one write
  (what Caprock did) or one character at a time; a `.txt` path in a paste;
  two double-quoted paths with spaces in *one* paste.
- **Several images**: one paste per path gives `[Image #1] [Image #2]`, even
  when both pastes arrive in one write. Claude Code adds the space after each
  itself.
- **Order**: Claude Code reads the image before placing it, so text written
  straight after an image paste landed first. 150 ms between them kept the
  order.

So `pathInput` (`lib/xtermInput.ts`) sends a png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp path — the
types Claude Code's image reader takes — as its own bracketed paste of the
same quoted path a file gets, with no trailing space, and the queue waits
150 ms after it. That holds for the clipboard paste, the browser drop, the
app's native drop and the phone's photo button, and needs no change to the
paste directory: quoted, its spaces are fine. It applies only while the
process has bracketed paste on (`term.modes.bracketedPasteMode`): a process
that never asked would get `ESC[200~` typed into its line. A shell that did
ask (zsh, bash's readline) inserts a paste as text, so a shell tab still gets
the quoted path. Other files are typed as before. The chat, which has no
terminal, sends the saved paths to `POST /v1/agents/{id}/input {paths}`, and
the daemon writes the same bytes from the mode it tracked
(`internal/api/pathinput.go`).

An ordinary text paste is left alone: xterm already handles it and already
applies bracketed paste, so intercepting it would turn a multi-line paste into
several submitted lines.

**What is checked, and how.** `testdata/ptyecho` prints the bytes it receives,
so `-tags ptyspike` can assert that an arrow key arrives as `1b 5b 41` and a
resize reaches the process — verified against `stty size`, which is the
kernel's view rather than our own struct. Asserting on our own bookkeeping
would have passed while `Resize` did nothing at all, which is exactly what it
did.

## Tasks (kanban)

- **Redesigned 2026-10-11 after owner review** (translated): "completely unclear what this is, how it works and what to do with it; nothing ready-made to start from; the interface is done badly." Each complaint got one answer, below: a sentence and a worked example, templates, and a list in place of six columns.
- **What it is, in one sentence, then one worked example.** The header says *give an agent a job and the commands that prove it is done; it works on its own branch; Caprock runs the commands itself; only green counts as done.* The example (`HowItRuns`) walks one task through four steps — *you write the task* ("Fix the failing tests in billing/", checked by the repo's own test command when one was detected), *a worker does it* (branch `caprock/worker-1`, own git worktree), *Caprock checks it*, *you take it* (read the diff, merge; nothing is merged for you). It is the off state's body and sits under the gallery on an empty board.
- **Templates are data in the UI** — `ui/src/lib/taskTemplates.ts`, a typed list: *Fix failing tests*, *Bump a dependency*, *Add tests for a file*, *Fix lint and type errors*, *Write docs for a module*, *Port a script*, plus *Blank task*. Each carries a name, a one-line blurb, a title and a prompt with `<placeholders>`, and `wants` — the *kinds* of check it needs in order of preference (`test`; `all` then test+lint+typecheck+build; `lint`+`typecheck`; …). A template names no command and no budget.
- **Checks are detected, never guessed.** `GET /v1/hive/starter` ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)) returns the commands the repository declares, from its files alone (`internal/projcheck`). `checksFor` takes, for the first preference group the repo can satisfy, one command per kind — the project's own entry point before the toolchain's stock command, so `make test` and not also `go test ./...`. A repo with no detected checks prefills none, and the gallery and the form say so. Each gallery card shows the checks it would use in *this* repo.
- **Picking a template opens the existing form prefilled.** The form names where each value came from: the checks field lists every detected command as a chip to add with a click (`from Makefile` on hover); the budget is **empty** with the daemon's own ceiling (`default_budget_usd`, `board.DefaultBudgetUSD`) as the placeholder and the hint "Empty means Caprock's ceiling of $5.00" — the form invents no figure, and the blank form no longer prefills `go test`/`go vet` and $3. The first `<placeholder>` in the title is selected on open, and the form will not create a task while any template placeholder is still in the title or body.
- **Picking a template in the off state** opens the turn-on confirmation, and the template's form opens the moment the runner is on: the pick is not lost to the dialog.
- **The board is one list grouped by what the task needs from you** — Needs you, Working (assigned, in progress, verifying), Waiting to start, Finished (done, failed) — not six columns, five of them usually empty and each too narrow to read at 1280px. A row reads left to right: state pill (Waiting, Starting, Working, Checking, Needs you, Done, Failed); title and one line of **what it is doing now** (`nowLine`: "Checks failed in round 1; worker-1 is fixing it", "Over its budget…", "Checks passed. The work is on branch caprock/worker-3"); the branch; **checks** (`checksLine`: not run yet, running round n, failed n×, passed); **cost** against budget with a bar, red when over; and **one action** — Approve/Reject on Needs you (approve sends it back to the worker, reject marks it failed), *Review & merge* on Done, *Open* otherwise. The action column is a fixed width so every row's columns line up. Once there is a task, the gallery folds to a one-line *Start from:* strip.
- **A queued task with nothing moving says why**: "1 task is waiting. The orchestrator hands tasks to workers — start it if it is not running", with the button. Starting it twice is safe (it re-reads the queue). The header's *Start orchestrator* is hidden over an empty board, where it has nothing to assign.
- Columns (the states): inbox → assigned → in-progress → verifying → done / needs-you.
- Tasks are files on disk (`tasks/<id>.md` with YAML frontmatter); the board renders them. Format: [05-orchestration.md § Task file](05-orchestration.md#task-file).
- "needs-you" = approvals queue (spend thresholds, destructive ops, scope changes).
- Shipped in v0.3.0.
- **A card opens to the work.** Any task with an assignee is clickable and opens a drawer answering, in this order: what changed (the file list and patches from `GET /v1/sessions/{id}/diff`, reached through the newest session in the detail's `work.sessions`), what proved it (the latest verification round — command and exit code), where the work is (branch, worktree, repo), and how to take it (`git merge --no-ff caprock/<worker>`, copyable). The card itself carries the branch name so the board answers "where did that go?" without opening anything. Before this, a card was a title, an id and a dollar figure, and the diff endpoint had no reference anywhere in the UI — which is the whole reason the feature read as having no output.
- **The merge command is text, not a button.** It writes to the user's repository; a dashboard that runs git on your branch without you having read the command first is not something a local-first tool should ship. `Copyable` (lifted out of `UpdateBanner` into `components/ui.tsx`) is the shared primitive.
- **The off state is a sentence, a worked example, a button and the templates** (since 2026-10-11; before, three abstract steps). It carries the concept, not the flag: the same three facts that decide whether anyone runs this — the work happens in a separate git worktree, **Caprock** runs the checks rather than the agent, and the queue directory is created for them — plus the limit that it suits independent tasks only. They arrive as a numbered strip (*you write a task* → *Caprock runs it* → *Caprock checks it*), because nobody reads a wall of text to decide whether to try a button. "Phase 2" and "hive" are our vocabulary and appear in no user-facing string, here or in the CLI.
- **Turning it on is a click, not a command to paste elsewhere.** The screen previously handed over `caprock up --hive ~/caprock-tasks`, which is the opposite of a start button: the one control the feature needed lived in a terminal. `POST /v1/hive` ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)) turns the runner on over the running daemon, and the board replaces the off state in place — no restart. The paths it will use are the daemon's own suggestions (`suggested_hive`, `suggested_repo`), shown filled in and editable.
- **It confirms before it arms.** Enabling this is what lets Caprock spawn Claude sessions with permission prompts skipped, so a dialog names the queue directory and the repository, says in three lines what will happen — the directory is created, sessions may run with prompts skipped one worktree each, and *nothing starts yet* — and only then offers the button. A silent one-click that begins spawning agents is not something a local-first tool should ship; neither is a dialog nobody can read, so it is three short lines and two named paths.
- **The empty board shows what to hand off.** Six empty columns and two buttons said nothing about which comes first; a one-line *start here* hint followed, and is now replaced by the template gallery and the worked example, with the orchestrator button hidden until there is a task.

## Queue (Tasks → Queue)

- **A tab of Tasks, not a new nav item.** The Tasks screen opens on a
  *Board | Queue* switch; the Queue is `#/queue` and in the palette as
  *Queue*. Both answer "what is being worked on": the Board for tasks handed
  to workers, the Queue for the messages waiting for the agents Caprock
  started. The owner asked (translated): "can we understand the task queue?
  Throw tasks in and see what is being done and when it will be ready."
- **Now:** every session Caprock started, working ones first — name (a link
  to the session), state (*working*, *waiting for your approval*, *asked you
  a question*, *at its prompt*), project and branch; the in-progress item of
  its own task list and "3 of 7 done"; how long this turn has run and about
  how long is left.
- **Waiting:** each session's queued messages in order, with where each
  stands ("next, after the current turn", "2nd after the current turn") and
  a range such as "starts in 2–9 min · done in 4–14 min (rough)".
- **Done in the last 3 hours:** each finished turn's prompt, its session,
  how long it took and when, *from the queue* when it was a queued message,
  *stopped on an error* for a StopFailure.
- **Every estimate is a range with its basis**, under the row: "rough
  estimate: middle half of the last 200 turns in caprock (16 s–4 min,
  typical 48 s)", the figures measured on the owner's history. Under five turns, "No estimate: 3 turns finished in this
  project so far; an estimate needs 5." Other agents say why they have none.
  Measured, not assumed: [12-risks.md RISK-08](12-risks.md#risks).
- **Add to queue** (the machine, or a controller phone): a project, then a
  session of it or *Whichever is free first* — which names the session it
  picked and when it is free — and the text; ⌘/Ctrl+Enter or *Add*. It goes
  into that session's own queue, so it is typed in at once at its prompt,
  otherwise when the turn ends. A viewer sees the queue without the form.

## Work

Every piece of work on the machine on its way to a release, one row each
([ADR-064](08-decisions.md); owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "exactly this I
want to see on the project-manager page", of a status report that listed
*RELEASE*, *IN WORK* and *NEXT* with a bar per item). `screens/Work.tsx`,
`lib/work.ts` (pure: rows, lanes, estimates, words), `lib/useWork.ts`
(reads), `components/WorkRow.tsx`, `WorkCard.tsx`, `WorkLane.tsx`,
`NextRelease.tsx`, `WorkHomeCard.tsx`. It is the route `#/work`, second in
the dashboard's nav (Home, **Work**, Cost …), kept mounted once opened like
the other screens (`KEPT_SCREENS`), and in the app it renders inside the
Dashboard tab. ⇧⌘W (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+W off macOS), the palette's *Open Work*
or Home's *Open Work →* opens it; from it ⇧⌘W goes back to the tab or
screen it was pressed on, as ⌘P does for the Pipeline (`openWork`,
`workBack` in AppShell). In the app a session row brings the session's tab
forward, opening one when it has none (`onOpenSession`, handed through
`PipelineHostContext`); in a browser it opens the session's page.

- **A row is a repository and branch at its furthest stage**, with the live
  sessions on it attached. A session joins its newest recorded pull request
  (a session cut across several pull requests goes to the newest), else the
  open pull request whose head is its branch — never by its branch on the
  default branch; with neither it is a row of its own. Two pull requests
  from one branch are one row: the open one, else the furthest along, else
  the newer. A pull request the Pipeline answer does not hold (its
  repository not read, or past its list) is drawn from the session's
  record, at *PR open*. A task joins its session's row (and names it) or
  its branch's; a queued message is its own *Planned* row, after its
  session's turn. Closed unmerged pull requests and done tasks are not
  listed.
- **The bar is a measure, not a guess.** Four steps — agent → PR → CI →
  merged — filled to the stage the row is in: 1 an agent with no pull
  request, 2 a draft or an open pull request whose checks were not read, 3
  CI (running in the warn colour, red when a check failed, it conflicts or
  changes were asked for, the accent when green and ready), 4 merged,
  released or not — the bar full, in the merged colour (owner, 2026-10-20,
  translated: the final status should be merged; a grey *released* block
  read as unfinished, or closed). Planned rows are empty. Beside it the
  stage in a word (*CI running*, *conflict*, *ready*, *merged*, and once
  released, muted, *in v0.117.4*); a merge waiting for a release, where
  the repository cuts them, adds a muted *· not released*
  (`WorkItem.note`, `data-work-release-note`).
- **Each row** says its title (a pull request's opens the Pipeline at its
  repository, `#/pipeline?repo=…&pr=…`; a session's opens the session — in
  the app its tab comes forward through `onOpenSession`; a task's opens
  Tasks), the repository when more than one is on the board, the branch,
  who works on it — the agent and its model, *started by Caprock* or *your
  terminal* — how long since the work started, and its agent cost at API
  list price, *not a bill* on hover (`AGENT_COST_NOTE`): the pull request's
  card when Caprock recorded sessions on it, else its sessions' sum.
- **Estimates only where a measured basis exists**, each with its basis on
  hover: *turn 1–4 min left (rough)* from `turn_left` while working (the
  Queue's range and basis; nothing once the turn runs past it); *CI running
  6m · usually 11m* from the repository's CI median (two or more runs);
  *usually released in ~2h* on a merged row from the merge → release median,
  with three merges or more behind it; the agent's own task list as *3 of 7
  steps* with a small bar, only when it keeps one. Never a task's completion
  time, a pull request's merge countdown or a clock time for the next
  release (rule 6, [RISK-08](12-risks.md#risks)).
- **The list (default)** groups the rows *Next release* (merged and waiting,
  and pull requests ready to merge), *In review* (draft, CI running or red,
  a conflict), *In work* (agents with no pull request, tasks in progress),
  *Planned* (queued messages, then tasks not started) and *Released* (the
  newest ten, folded). Inside a group, rows that need you come first (a
  permission prompt, a question, a task needing you — marked with a warn
  edge and the reason), then working ones, then the furthest along. Sessions
  at their prompt for over 30 minutes with no pull request fold into one
  *N idle* line under *In work*. On a narrow window a row stacks its title
  and notes over the bar, elapsed and cost; nothing scrolls sideways at
  390px and no text is clipped (measured in a browser, list and Kanban, Dark
  Pastel, Book and Sunlight).
- **Kanban** (the *List | Kanban | Release* switch, remembered per browser
  as `caprock.work.view`): columns *Planned*, *In work*, *In review* (ready
  ones included), *Merged · waiting*, *Released*, the same cards; one column
  under another below 900px of width.
- **Release** (the switch's third view; owner, 2026-10-15): a panel per
  repository with something on its way, in the board's repository order,
  each its release plan ([§ Release plan](#release-plan)) with the
  repository named beside the version, built from the board's own rows.
  The *Next release* strip of the other two views is not drawn here: the
  plans say it. With nothing on its way, *Nothing on its way to a release*.
- **The head** says *N agents live · N building · N on open PRs · N needs
  you* (building: live sessions on a row with no pull request; on open PRs:
  on a draft, open or ready one), today's spend on live work and on the
  whole machine, at list price.
- **Next release**, per repository with a tag, merged work or unreleased
  notes: *2h 05m since v0.117.4 · usual gap 2h 40m (median of 9)* — the
  usual gap only with three gaps or more, from the repository's tags, else
  the Pipeline's release gap — commits on the default branch since the tag,
  the merged pull requests waiting, and the CHANGELOG's [Unreleased] items
  (plus any `changes/unreleased/` fragments) on the default branch *as of your last fetch* (git is read, never
  fetched). A repository that is not on GitHub, or GitHub not connected,
  says so in one plain line; its agents are still listed.
- **Reads.** `GET /v1/work` (03-contracts.md § Work) is never polled: read
  when the page is shown and again on a live frame that can move it (a
  session, a turn, a prompt, a queue, a task, GitHub), at most once a
  second; nothing while the page is behind another screen (`ScreenShown`)
  or the window is hidden, one read on coming back. The pull requests are
  the Pipeline's own answer (`usePipelineStats`, kept per repository) for
  at most four repositories, those with agents running first, so the two
  pages share one kept answer and never both poll; a line says when more
  repositories are on the board than were read.
- **Home's strip** (`WorkHomeCard`, on Home above the Live pulse): the
  head line and the first three rows that need you or are working, with
  *Open Work →*; a session row brings its tab forward in the app. It
  reads `/v1/work` alone, so its rows show the pull requests recorded on
  the sessions. Nothing with no agent live.
- **Empty**: *Nothing in work* — start an agent in a git repository and it
  shows here; sessions started in your own terminal show too. With the
  hive off, a line says planned work is only the queued messages.

## Release plan

One repository's next release as a heading and rows of work with block
progress bars (owner, 2026-10-15, translated, of a text status report —
the next version, *#656 brain: agents → shared brain ▓▓▓▓▓▓▓▓░░ merged*,
*#659 sidebar footer edge ▓▓▓▓▓▓░░░░ CI*, *Second Brain live build
▓▓░░░░░░░░ agent*, then what comes later — "exactly this, as a view").
One component, `components/ReleasePlan.tsx`, over pure `lib/releaseplan.ts`,
drawn in four places: the Pipeline's *Next release* panel ([§ The
Pipeline](#the-pipeline)), the cockpit's *Release* section (§ Inspector),
the Work page's *Release* view ([§ Work](#work)), and the phone home's
*Releases* ([§ The phone home](#the-phone-home), `phone`).

- **The heading is the next version, by a stated rule** (rule 6): the last
  tag (`WorkRepo.tags.last`, else the newest published GitHub release)
  bumped by the changes waiting for a release — any `Added` entry makes the
  next minor, entries all of other kinds the next patch (owner, 2026-10-15:
  "a feature is a minor release, fixes are patches"). The kinds are
  `WorkRepo.unreleased.kinds` (03-contracts.md § Work): a changelog
  fragment's `kind:`, or the `### Added` / `### Fixed` heading an entry sits
  under in the CHANGELOG's [Unreleased]. Hovering it says the rule and what
  it counted (*1 Added entry since v0.119.0, read from origin/master's
  changelog entries as of your last fetch. When it ships is your call.*).
  With no tag, a tag that is not x.y.z, or an entry of no kind (a feature
  cannot be ruled out), it says *Next release* with no number and the
  reason on hover; with no pending entries at all, no number either. Never
  a date.
- **Under it**, one line of where the release stands (owner, 2026-10-18,
  translated: the old *7 changes waiting for the next release · last
  release v0.134.0, 18h ago* "is not readable, I cannot see the PRs in
  progress"): *Next release · 7 merged · PR #755 in CI (2/3 ✓) · 1 agent
  working · last v0.134.0 18h ago* (the *Next release* word is dropped when
  the version heading already says it). Each part but the last is a button
  that scrolls to its section below (`lib/releaseplan` `releaseLine`):
  *N merged*, the open pull request (the failing one, red, *PR #N CI
  failed*; else the furthest on, *in CI* with the OSes whose checks passed
  of those that ran; *+K more* for the rest), *N agents working* and *N
  branches ready*, each only when there are some. Nothing under way:
  *7 merged, nothing in progress*. Nothing merged and nothing under way:
  *Nothing new since v0.129.0, released 9m ago*. Parts wrap, two lines at
  most on a phone, no sideways scroll. The usual gap is on hover only —
  *Releases here usually come 2h 40m apart: the median gap between the last
  10* — with three gaps or more, from the repository's tags, else the
  Pipeline's release gap. No tag, no line.
- **The rows are the Work board's** (`buildWork`), one repository's, the
  furthest along first: pull requests merged since the last tag (one merged
  before it is in that tag, though no GitHub release says so), open ones,
  and agents working with no pull request yet (stage word *no PR yet*).
  Each is its number and title (a pull request's opens its detail on the
  Pipeline; on the Pipeline page itself, in place), a bar, the stage word,
  and as width allows what it waits for, its agent cost, its CI per OS, its
  CI time and its age.
- **A row opens its session** (owner, 2026-10-17, translated, of an idle
  agent's row: "from here I want to go into the session"). An agent's row
  — in the next release or in *Not in a release yet* — is itself a button,
  its whole title (*Open the session: …* on hover, Enter or Space from the
  keyboard); a pull request's row keeps its title's link and, when an agent
  session is known on it, adds a small terminal button after the title
  (*Open session: <name>*). Of several sessions on a row, the newest that
  is not idle, else the newest (`pickSession`). It goes exactly as a
  *Waiting for you* row does (`lib/sessionJump` `openSession`): in the
  desktop app the session's tab, brought forward or opened (the cockpit's
  *Release* rows too); in a browser its terminal when Caprock holds it,
  else its timeline. On the phone an agent's row is a 44px tap target into
  the session's chat, as the phone opens a session.
- **The bar** is a segment per step of agent → PR → CI → merged, each
  named on hover with the steps in order under it (*CI: under way — CI
  running* / *Agent → PR → CI → Merged*; on an agent's row *Pull request:
  none yet — the agent has not opened one*; on a merge waiting for a
  release *Merged: done — not released yet, it ships with the next release
  (v0.118.0)*), filled to the step the row is in: 1 *agent*, 2 a draft or
  *PR open*, 3 CI (*CI running*, *ready*), 4 *merged* — the bar full, in the
  merged colour. It ends at merged (owner, 2026-10-20, translated: the
  final status should be merged — a grey last block read as unfinished, or
  closed); a release is never a block, and a merge's wait for one is the
  *a release* it waits for, beside the word.
  The step it is on is red on a red check, a conflict or changes asked
  for, and breathes softly (opacity, still under reduced motion) while it
  is under way — CI running, an agent at work. A landing-queue label on
  the pull request (`landing: queued`, `landing: updating`, `landing: needs
  a hand merge`, `landing: CI red`) is the stage word when present, red for
  *CI red*, amber for *needs a hand merge*, the board's own word on hover.
  On a page the bar grows with the row (owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "a
  lot of empty space wasted — make it bigger, or add some indicator"; then
  "the progress bar can be wider but fluid … costs are missing, and a bit
  of CI/CD data"): the title and the bar share what the other columns
  leave, 1.4 : 1 (the cockpit 2.2 : 1), so the bar is never past ~40% of
  the row and never
  under 88px (60px compact); segments 9px tall (7px compact).
- **Beside the word**, from what the board already read, never a guess:
  *what it waits for now* — *CI on Windows · 6m* (the OSes whose checks
  still run, since the first of them started; *CI · 6m* when no check
  names an OS; the usual CI time on hover), *fix: go (windows-latest) +1*
  (the failed checks the card names), *needs a rebase* (a conflict),
  *requested changes*, *a review* (GitHub says one is required), *a merge*,
  *a release* (merged; the usual merge → release lag on hover), *marked
  ready* (a draft), *its turn to land*, *your merge* (the landing queue's
  hand merge), *your permission* / *your answer* / *your next prompt* / *the
  agent · 3 of 7 steps* (an agent row); nothing when none of it says.
  Then its *age* since opened (an agent row: since its first session) at
  one unit — *45m*, *19h*, *3d* — and its *CI per OS*: a dot each for
  macOS, Linux and Windows its checks ran on (`BoardCard.os`, the checks'
  names read as a matrix names them), green passed, red failed, the
  accent breathing while running, each named on hover; none on a merged
  row or where no check names an OS. Its *CI time*: finished, its head
  commit's checks from the first start to the last finish
  (`BoardCard.ci_ms`) against the repository's usual CI (the flow's
  `ci_median_ms`) — *14m +27%*, amber past +20%; running, the time so far
  over the usual — *6m / ~11m*; nothing on a merged row or without the
  stamps. Its *agent cost*: the spend Caprock recorded on it at API list
  price (`BoardCard.spend`, `store.SpendByPR`, the Work board's cost;
  an agent row, its sessions' sum), the sessions counted and how it was
  attributed on hover; *—* where no session was recorded, never an
  estimate. The columns come in as the plan's container widens — what it
  waits for at 520px, the cost at 600px, CI per OS at 680px, the CI time at
  760px, the age at 840px (a container query, so the cockpit and the
  Pipeline differ by their own width, not the window's) — so a narrow
  panel drops the age first, the cost last but one and what it waits for
  last, and never scrolls sideways; a column no row fills is zero wide.
- **Layout.** Bars and words sit in one aligned column (a CSS subgrid),
  whatever the title's length; the cockpit draws no bars at all (*The right
  panel*, below). At phone size (`phone`) a row is two lines in a 44px tap
  target — the number and title, cut to one line, then the bar (fixed
  width), the word and what it waits for, cut — so every bar starts at the
  row's left edge, and a pull request's row opens it on GitHub in a new
  tab.
- **The right panel** (`compact`, the cockpit's *Release* section, ~320px;
  owner, 2026-10-19, translated: the titles were unreadable there, cut to a
  few letters beside a bar, and only a pull request's title, a branch and
  when it was pushed matter). No bars and no columns (`CompactRows`):
  - **The heading** is one line: the version, *next*, *· last v0.137.0 1h
    ago* (`ReleasePlanData.last`). The release line above is not drawn
    here: the sections below already say each of its parts.
  - **Sections**, in small capitals with their counts: *Shipping (1 merged,
    not released)*, *Open PRs (4)*, *Branches without a PR (3)*, *Agents
    working (2)* (`planSections` `short`; the hint on hover).
  - **A row** is its number and title over the full width, wrapping to two
    lines at most (line-clamp), never cut to a few letters, then one small
    muted line: *state · extra · age*. The state is plain words
    (`stateOf`, `PlanRow.state`) in place of the bar — the owner read
    *merged* beside one empty block as broken: *CI 1/3* (the OSes whose
    checks passed of those that ran), *CI failed*, *needs a rebase*,
    *changes requested*, *needs your merge*, a landing label, *ready*,
    *needs a review*, *draft*, *merged · not released* (*merged* where work
    ships on merge), *released*; an agent's *no PR yet*, *working 12m*,
    *asking*. Its hover says what the row waits for and the checks per OS.
    The age is since the merge on a merged row, else since it was opened.
  - **Open pull requests sort by urgency** (`byUrgency`): blocked first (a
    red check, a conflict, changes asked for, a landing label that waits on
    the person), marked *!* in red (amber for the person's merge), then
    under way, then ready; the board's order within each.
  - **A branch an open pull request already carries** (`in_pr`, a batch)
    is not listed; that pull request says *+N branches* instead
    (`carriedBranches`), a button that opens the branch names under it.
  - **Branches without a PR** fold to their heading by default; the
    heading's ▸ opens them, remembered per viewer (the panel folds' key
    `release-branches-shown`). Each is one line: the branch name (cut) and
    its age at the right; the subject and commits ahead on hover; no chip,
    no subject line, no ↗ — the row opens the comparison on GitHub. Five,
    then *Show all N* and *+N older*, as below.
- **Later**: *Later · drafts, not yet ready for review*, then *Not in a
  release yet · agents waiting for you, idle 30+ min* (at their prompt for
  over 30 minutes, no pull request; stage word *idle*), each a small label
  in sentence case, with the same bars.
- **What else heads into it, in sections** (owner, 2026-10-18,
  translated, of the right panel: "it shows vaguely what's going on on the
  way to the release" — two merged rows while six subagents worked and
  twelve pushed branches waited for the batch pull request). In the
  cockpit's *Release* section, where the session is known, the plan also
  lists its repository's **pushed branches with no pull request**
  (`branchRows`, from the deck's Branches answer, `GET
  /v1/sessions/{id}/branches`, read while the section is unfolded, once a
  minute, kept by the daemon): on origin, ahead of the default branch,
  committed in the last 14 days, with no pull request in the branch's own
  read nor in the Pipeline's list by head, not carried by another branch's
  open pull request (`in_pr`, a batch), and not already on the default
  branch by content (`landed`, 03-contracts.md § Session branches — a
  squash merge or a batch), the newest 15 (`BRANCHES_LISTED`). A branch row
  is two lines, no bar and no chip (stage `branch`; the section's heading
  says they are branches): its last commit's subject, up to two lines and
  never cut to a few letters, then, muted, the branch name (cut first,
  whole on hover), *3 commits · 2h ago* (*1 commit* when one; the base on
  hover), its agent cost when one is known, and ↗ at that line's end; the
  whole row opens its comparison on GitHub (owner, 2026-10-19, translated,
  of the old one-line row at 360px: "the information isn't perceivable like
  this; I don't even know what to do with it"). The section shows five
  (`BRANCHES_SHOWN`, `shownRows`), then a quiet *Show all N*; a branch with
  no commit in 7 days (`BRANCH_FRESH_MS`, `older`) waits behind *+N older*
  and is not in the section's count nor the release line's *N branches
  ready*. In the right panel a pull request's or an agent's title also
  takes up to two lines, where it was cut to one. And the session's **working subagents**
  (`subagentRows`, from the cockpit's own subagent read, handed down by
  `SessionSubagents`: nothing is asked again), the newest 10: what each was
  asked as *Agent: <task>*, one segment, *working 12m* (*asking* in amber on a permission
  prompt), what it does now beside the word. The rows stand in sections,
  each a faint label with its count and a one-line muted hint under it
  (`planSections`, `data-release-section`, `data-release-hint`):
  *In this release · 2 merged*, *Pull requests · 1 open*, *Branches pushed,
  no PR yet · 3* (hint: *Will go into the next batch PR*), *Agents working
  now · 6*; an empty section is left out. The Pipeline page, the Work
  page and the phone draw the same sections over the rows they have; the
  right panel names them shorter and draws its rows as *The right panel*
  above says.
- **What released means.** The released segment's hover names it —
  *Released (shipped in a published release): …* — and on a merged row it
  says what the merge waits for, *waits for the next release (v0.133.0)*
  (the number when the rule above predicts one); what the row waits for
  says the same, *the next release (v0.133.0)*. Where nothing read says
  whether the repository cuts releases (`board.ships_by` absent) the
  released segment is dotted, *not known* (`data-step-state` `unknown`),
  never a wait.
- **A repository that cuts no releases has no next release** (owner,
  2026-10-18, translated, of caprock-web: eighteen merged pull requests
  under *Next release*, each at four of five blocks, "waiting" for a
  release that never comes). Where the board says it ships on merge
  (`board.ships_by` `merge`: no release published and no version tag made
  in 180 days; 03-contracts.md § Board), the heading, the version, the
  release status and the last release give way to one line, *No releases
  here: work ships when it merges* (`data-release-ships` `merge`; the
  Pipeline panel is titled *In flight*), and the merges are done: the three newest (`MERGED_RECENT`) under
  *Merged recently · shipped when merged*, their bars full, waiting for
  nothing. Open pull requests and agents stay as rows. A plan with nothing
  but those merges counts as empty, so the cockpit's *Release* section and
  the Work page's view draw nothing for it.
- **The release status** (owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "I don't have a
  release indicator — it exists but is organised differently, it's not
  clear how releases go and where … the PR statuses there now are cool and
  correct"). On the Pipeline page only, the plan carries the release's own
  status (`lib/releasestatus.ts`, `components/ReleaseStatus.tsx`), in the
  pull request rows' pills (`JourneyPills`, the same shapes: done ticked,
  the current one filled in its colour, ahead dashed); the rows under it
  are unchanged.
  - **Which release.** A release pull request (`chore(release): x.y.z`,
    `release: vx.y.z`), open or merged, for a version past every known tag
    names it; else the newest tag while its release run is running, red,
    or has no published release yet is *releasing*, drawn in its own box
    above the next release's heading (the work merged since is the release
    after it); else, with work merged or entries waiting, the predicted
    next version; else no stages, only the last release.
