  - **Why the number.** Beside the version, the bump as a pill — *minor*,
    *patch* (*major* when a release pull request says so) — and the reason
    from the entries' kinds: *a feature since v0.126.0 (2 features, 3
    other)*, *fixes only since …*; a tag under way, against the release
    before it. With no rule to say, no pill.
  - **The stages**, merged → release PR → CI → tag → builds → published →
    site, each from what is read, its source on hover: *n merged* (the
    plan's merged rows, else the entries); the release pull request (*#730*
    once open, a draft current); its CI from its board card (running, red
    with the failed checks named, green); the tag (any known tag — git's,
    a published release's, the release run's — equal to the version); the
    builds from the newest release run (`…/ci/release`, running / failed /
    passed) with *builds 6/14*, the files on the release against the
    release before it (`assets`), else the run's jobs finished of its
    total; *published* when GitHub has the release. The current stage is
    the first not done; a later one already done stays ticked (the release
    is published before the last build attaches its files). A stage
    nothing read can place — the site's deploy always, a release pull
    request or its checks not among those listed — is drawn neutral, a
    dotted outline with a *?*, *Not known: …* on hover; never guessed.
  - **The last release**, under the rows: *Last release v0.126.0 · 3h ago
    · 14 files · GitHub ↗*, the newest published release that is not the
    one under way.
- **Reads nothing itself.** The placements hand it the Pipeline answer and
  `GET /v1/work` they already read (`planForRepo`, `releasePlan`); no new
  polling. Without the Work answer it draws the pull requests alone, with no
  version and no agents. The Pipeline page also reads the newest release
  run (`GET /v1/sessions/{id}/ci/release`, the deck's own answer, kept as
  `release:<repo>`) while it is in front, every 12 s while the run runs;
  the daemon keeps it, so an idle read costs GitHub nothing new.

## Graph (live orchestration graph)

- The "wow" view (Phase 3 delight), chosen over a radar / activity-river by an ICP persona panel because the picture **is** the differentiator: the orchestrator pinned dead-center, workers on a stable ring, and tasks flowing along fixed edges through a **verify gate** that turns green only after `done_criteria` pass — the verified-team story the competitor's office metaphor structurally can't show.
- **Fixed radial layout, never force-directed** (the panel's #1 constraint): a node's angular slot is its index in the ever-seen sorted registry, which only grows — so a node appearing or leaving never moves any other. Task dots glide along their spoke with critically-damped rAF motion (overshoot-free; a mid-flight verify bounce re-aims smoothly). Pure theme-aware SVG (light/dark for free via the CSS vars).
- **Empty-state**: when no orchestrator/hive is running, the same canvas degrades to your hand-started live sessions on the ring around a neutral `caprock` hub (never blank). Reads the existing live `task`/`session` frames + `/v1/tasks`,`/v1/sessions` — no backend change.
- **Not in the top nav** (decided 2026-08-20 on owner review). Without a running orchestrator the empty state can only draw session ids around a hub — topology rather than work — so a permanent nav slot bought a screen that says nothing to a solo user, and the hub-in-the-centre framing puts Caprock at the centre of the picture instead of the user's work. The route stays at `#/graph` and the Tasks board links to it while any task is assigned and unfinished. Reconsider the framing (time, not space) before promoting it back.
- Fast-follows (deferred): a real mailbox-pulse frame, a cumulative-spend arc, click-through to a session. Implementation: `ui/src/screens/orchestration/`.

The header carries the **running version** at all times — it was previously only on the status page, and "which build am I on?" is asked far more often than that placement implied. With release checks on and a newer version available it reads `v0.9.0 → v0.9.4` in the accent colour and links to the notice. A build from source shows "dev build" instead of a `git describe` string, and is never offered an upgrade: it is not a published release, so the comparison is meaningless.

The chip is a button. It used to be a label that said a newer release existed and stopped there — which answers the half of the question nobody needs help with. It opens a dialog with the running version, what is published, a "check now", and **the exact command for how this copy was installed** (`brew update && brew upgrade caprock`, `go install …@latest`, or the release page for a downloaded binary — the daemon derives it from its own path, `internal/update`), with a copy button.

**Caprock does not update itself, and the dialog says so.** A daemon that overwrites its own running binary — as root on some install paths — is a worse thing to own than a stale version. Stating it once beats leaving a person hunting for a button that should not exist.

## Update notice

When a newer release exists, Now leads with a line naming it and **the exact command for how this copy was installed** (`brew update && brew upgrade caprock`, `scoop update caprock`, `go install …@latest`), one click to copy. When no package manager owns the binary it links to the release page instead. The Homebrew command refreshes the tap first, and that is load-bearing: a tap is not served by the Homebrew API but read from a local git clone, which `brew upgrade` refreshes only through auto-update — at most once every 24 hours. A user who ran any brew command earlier the same day is told `already installed` for a release that has been public for hours, and the command Caprock gave them looks broken. Reported by a user on the day v0.31.1 shipped.

There is deliberately **no "update now" button**: upgrading replaces the running binary, so the daemon would be killing the process running the command, and executing a package manager on the user's behalf from a web page is a surface a local-first tool should not open. You run one line in your own terminal, where you can see what it does.

When the upgrade would end sessions Caprock started, the line says how many
will close. Since [ADR-033](08-decisions.md#adr-033--an-owned-session-outlives-the-daemon-its-terminal-lives-in-a-pty-host)
a session whose terminal is in a pty-host is picked back up after the restart
and is not counted (`survives_restart`); what is counted is a session in the
daemon's own PTY, the fallback when a holder could not start.

**An open terminal reconnects across the restart, and loses nothing.** The
daemon closes the socket with code 1000 when the session's process exits, and
only that prints `[session ended]`. Any other close is the connection —
Caprock restarting, a dropped network, a phone waking — and the terminal
reconnects forever (full jitter from 0.5 s to a 15 s cap; at once when the
page becomes visible, comes online, gets focus, is restored from the
back/forward cache or changes network — [03-contracts.md § Client reconnect policy](03-contracts.md#client-reconnect-policy)) over [terminal protocol v2](03-contracts.md#terminal-socket-protocol-v2):
it asks for the output after the last byte it has, so the screen and the
scrollback stay as they were and nobody reading back is moved, and it resends
what was typed and not yet acknowledged, which the session types once. A pill
over the terminal shows the connection state (**Reconnecting (n) · next try
in s**, **Catching up…**, **Control revoked — reason**) and, while
reconnecting, **Typed keys will be sent**; after 3 failed tries or 10 s
down it adds **Reconnect now**, which tries at once on the same session.
The app's terminal tabs show the same in their corner badge — *Reconnecting
(n) · next try in s*, then **Reconnect now** — drawn above the terminal's
canvas so the button takes the click. The retries cannot stop by themselves:
a client between sockets is checked every second, so after a Wi-Fi drop a
thrown `WebSocket` constructor or a lost timer no longer leaves every tab at
*Reconnecting…* until it is closed (Vova, 2026-10-15). The status strip's and
the header's connection state offer the same button for `/v1/live`.
It used to write "reconnecting" into the terminal itself, retry every second
for a minute, then ask for a reload. Up to 4 KB typed while offline is kept;
past that the pill says the extra keys were not. A daemon from before v2 is
still spoken to in version 1, which clears and repaints on reconnect.

**In the desktop app** (F12) the notice is quiet: *vX.Y.Z is out* in the
status strip, which opens a small panel with the command for each part this
machine installed through Homebrew — the app's cask (`brew update && brew
upgrade --cask caprock-app`, `app_command`) first, then the daemon's
formula (`command`) — or a pointer to the release page when neither is
known, *what's new*, and **Not now**, which hides that version (the same
`caprock.update.dismissed` key as the banner, so dismissing in one place
dismisses in both). It reads `GET /v1/update` every 10 minutes, which never
touches the network; with checks off it shows nothing. The daemon checks at
most hourly, conditionally (ETag), and within five minutes of waking.

**Since F20** (ADR-042) an app that can replace itself also announces a
newer version once in a bottom-right card (`AppUpdateToast`: **Update and
restart**, *What's new*, **Later**, which hides the card for that version
only, under its own key `caprock.update.toastSeen`; the card shows progress
while it downloads). The strip's line turns into one click: **Update to vX.Y.Z — Restart** (newer than the *app's* version),
then *Downloading vX.Y.Z* with a bar and a percentage, *Installing … —
restarting…*, and the restart; or **Update failed**, which opens to the
reason, **Try again**, the release page and **Not now**. A ▾ beside the
button opens *what's new* and **Not now**. A check by hand (the app menu, the
tray menu, the palette) shows *Checking for updates…* and then the offer or
*Caprock is up to date* for 6 seconds, whatever the release-check setting. A
`.deb`/`.rpm` install or a development build keeps the command line above,
with the reason it cannot update itself. On its first launch the app asks
once in a small card above the strip — *Check for updates automatically?*,
**Yes** focused — instead of the dashboard banner's offer, which the app does
not show ([21-app.md § Updates](21-app.md#updates)).

**Update automatically** (2026-10-15, ADR-042 as amended), chosen in
Settings → Updates or with the card's secondary *Always update
automatically*: the card no longer asks (`AppAutoUpdate` takes over). A
newer version is downloaded and verified in the background (the strip shows
*Downloading* and then the one-click offer). It installs only when no
session is working or waiting on a permission prompt (`GET
/v1/update/busy`), the page has been left alone for two minutes, and no
text field holds an unsent draft; it looks again every 30 seconds. Then a
card in the same corner — *Updating to X in 10 s*, *Sessions and tabs stay
as they are.*, **Not now** (that version waits an hour), **Restart now** —
which a key press or a click elsewhere calls off. After the restart: one
line, *Updated to X · What's new*, closed with ×.

**The answer to a check, beside the version** (owner, 2026-10-16,
translated: "I see a banner at the top centre saying everything is up to
date — it's not noticeable, because I expect the update result at the
bottom right, near the version"). In the desktop app every check answers in
the status strip, just left of the version (`AppUpdateNotice`): *Checking
for updates…* while one is out; *Caprock is up to date* for 6 s after a
check that was asked for — Check now (Settings → Updates, the version
panel), *Check for updates* in the palette, **Check for Updates…** in the
app or tray menu; a failed check as *Couldn't check for updates* (amber
warning, the error in its tooltip) with **Retry** and ×, which stays; and a
newer version as **Update to vX — Restart**, an amber pill, until it is
installed or dismissed. A failed check is never shown as a failed install.
The background check (every 15 minutes, and on focus) says nothing unless
there is a new version. The top-centre banner (`UpdateCheckBanner`,
`lib/checkbanner`, 2026-10-15) no longer appears in the app; it remains the
answer in the browser dashboard, which has no status strip: *You're up to
date — Caprock X is the latest · checked just now* with **Close** (gone
after 8 s unless the pointer is on it), or *Could not check for updates*
with **Retry**.

Because the check is the only outbound call Caprock makes, the same strip is where it is switched on — offered once, in plain words, with "no thanks" remembered. `not now` dismisses a specific version, so the next release speaks up again.

## Settings

`#/settings`, reached from **settings** at the right of the header (it was
labelled "status" and titled Status). Rebuilt on 2026-10-04 after the owner's
verdict, translated: "honestly, NOTHING here is understandable, and it should
be easy and simple." The rule it now follows: **short sections with plain
titles, and every option says in one line what happens when it is on** — not
how it works or why it exists; that lives in code comments and here. Anything
few people need is one click down under a **Details** or **Experiments**
disclosure, not on screen.

In order (problems first, then the phone, because that is what the owner opened
it for):

- **Problems**, only when there is one — nothing new being recorded, Claude
  Code not found, hooks missing — each with the one command that fixes it.
- **Open Caprock on your phone** — see below.
- **Telegram** (`PhoneAlerts.tsx`; [ADR-036](08-decisions.md),
  [ADR-047](08-decisions.md)) — *When a session is waiting for approval* and
  *When a session has finished*, both off until ticked, each saving alone, and
  *Include the last reply's first line*, on by default (up to 120 characters
  of the final reply). The bot is set up in the section (`TelegramBotFields`,
  shared with the weekly report), because alerts are free and the report's own
  panel is locked, first in the section, as **one card with three numbered
  steps**, each number turning into a green tick when done (owner,
  2026-10-18, translated: "everything worked, but the instruction is
  complicated"; three side-by-side step cards over a separate row of
  controls until then): **1** *In Telegram open @BotFather → /mybots (or
  /newbot) → your bot → API Token → Copy* with **Open @BotFather**
  (`t.me/BotFather`; it also puts the cursor in the paste field); **2** *Paste
  it here* — a large password field, **Paste** (reads the clipboard where the
  webview allows it; a token of the right shape, `looksLikeToken`, connects
  at once, else the field takes the cursor and a line says to paste by hand)
  and **Connect** (Telegram's `getMe`; its refusal in its own words); **3**
  *Send this code to @bot:* the six-digit code large, **Open @bot** (the
  `t.me` link that sends `/start <code>` in one tap), *or type `/start
  <code>`*, the time left and *Cancel* — before the token is in, a faint *The
  code shows here once the token is in*; with a bot and no chat, **Get a
  code**. The screen asks every two seconds and the card becomes one line the
  moment the code arrives: *✓ Connected to @bot · alerts to <chat>* with
  small bordered **Change bot**, **Pair again** and **Disconnect** buttons
  and the (i); the bridge's last error under it. Where the token is kept and
  what it is sent to is only behind the (i) (`SecretInfoDialog`), not a
  paragraph on the page. *Change bot* brings the card back titled *Change the
  bot*, with *Cancel*. A chat id typed in before pairing existed reads
  *alerts to a chat id typed in by hand*. The switches stay hidden until the
  bot has a chat, then a note asks for one alert if both are off; **Send a
  test alert** and the last outcome follow, with a line about *Mute this
  session* and `/mute all`. One line says what a message carries — whose it
  is (agent, project, title), the folder and branch, the command or file a
  dialog asks about, a finished run's time, cost, tool calls and changed
  files — and that Telegram can read it. When Telegram refuses the token
  (*Unauthorized* or *Not Found*, from the test or the last alert;
  `tokenRejected`), the same card comes back in red (`role="alert"`) headed
  *Telegram no longer accepts this bot's token*, with why (regenerated in
  BotFather or the bot deleted) and steps 1 and 2 open; *Last alert failed*
  is not repeated under it. A token saved there ends the refusal at once,
  before the next alert clears it on the daemon's side.
- **Talk to your agents from Telegram** (`TelegramChat.tsx`, Premium,
  ADR-047) — under the alerts, inside `<Locked feature="telegram">`: behind
  glass without a licence, live with one. What a reply does, the three
  commands with an example each, *Let my paired chat talk to sessions* (off
  until ticked; its hint is rule 7 — only sessions Caprock started) and *Send
  code blocks in replies* (off: each block is one line saying how long it
  was). A line asks for a paired bot first when there is none.
- **GitHub** (WP-19, `GitHubSettings.tsx`) — not connected: **Use my
  GitHub CLI login** (when `gh` is installed; "Caprock asks `gh auth token`
  when it needs the token and never writes it down"), *Or paste a token*
  with the permissions it needs and where it is kept, and **Sign in with
  GitHub** only when a client id is configured (the code, *Copy*, *Open
  github.com/login/device*, until when it works, *Cancel*). Connected:
  *Connected as @login (name) · through …*, the scopes (a warning without
  `repo`), a health line (*last worked 2 min ago · 4,812 of 5,000 requests
  left*, a rate-limit pause, the last error in full), the keychain fallback
  note when there is one, *Tell me when CI fails or a review arrives*, and
  **Disconnect** with what it removes. A paired device sees the state and
  is told to change it on the computer.
- **Desktop notifications**, in the desktop app only (WP-09) — *When a
  session is waiting for approval* (ticked by default) and *When a session has
  finished* (not ticked), each saving alone, apart from the Telegram switches.
  One line says what a notification carries and that clicking one opens the
  session with its Yes and No buttons.
- **Keep awake** ([ADR-049](08-decisions.md), `KeepAwake.tsx`), right
  after *New sessions* — *Stay awake*: **Off**, **While agents work** (the
  default) or **Always**, saved by the daemon (`keep_awake`). One line says
  what the chosen mode does and that the screen can still turn off and a
  closed lid still sleeps the machine; a second says what is happening now
  (*Keeping this computer awake now · 2 agents working*, *No agent is
  working, so your computer may sleep*), or why it cannot (no
  `systemd-inhibit`) or why the last hold failed. *awake now* with a cup
  sits beside the title while it holds.
- **Plan & licence.** The plan is set right here with the header picker's own
  choices (`PlanOptions`), not "change it in the header"; the licence key field
  sits under it, label above the field so a phone has room for the key.
- **Appearance.** Nine palettes as large swatches in two groups
  (`ThemePicker`; owner 2026-10-11: plain names, and more of them). See
  § Themes below for the palettes, the storage and the contrast numbers.
  Hovering a swatch shows it on the whole page and leaving puts the
  kept one back; a click keeps it. The arrows walk the swatches as laid out
  (`nextSwatch`), each one focused showing on the page as hovering does, and
  Enter or Space keeps it (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "I can't move
  across the themes with the keyboard"). The header's and sidebar's sun/moon open
  the same picker as a popover (*Theme · hover or arrows to try*, grouped Dark then
  Light) instead of stepping through the palettes, which was a guess per
  click; the palette's *Switch theme* still walks all nine, dark ones
  first. The dashboard terminal stays dark in every palette.
  **The popover opens whole, above everything** (owner, 2026-10-15,
  translated: "look how it appears!!"): it is portalled to `<body>` and
  placed in window coordinates (`placeThemeMenu` in
  `components/ThemePicker.tsx`). Inside the sidebar it was cut at the
  sidebar's right edge — the sidebar's backdrop filter makes it the
  containing block of anything positioned inside it — and the terminal drew
  over the rest. It opens on the preferred side (up from the sidebar footer,
  down from the browser header) when it fits, else on the side with more
  room, and is held 8px inside the window on every edge, at any sidebar
  width and folded; taller than the window, it is capped and scrolls.
  Opening it puts the focus on the palette in use; Tab and ⇧Tab walk the
  palettes, and past either end, a pick or Esc close it and give the focus
  back to the button. A click outside closes it.
  **Book is caprock.dev's light palette in full** (2026-10-10): the site's
  surfaces, its warm dark-brown ink (`#2b251c`, `#4f473b`, `#665d4f`) rather
  than grey, the deep amber it uses for text (`#8a4f0c`), and a filled accent
  control drawn as the site's button (`#eb9a3e`, dark ink). So the app and
  the screenshots of it on the site read as one thing. Light keeps the
  neutral palette.
- **Terminal**, in the app workspace (F21) — *Colours* (*Match app*, the
  default — Caprock in the dark theme, Slate in Slate, Paper on the light
  panel in the light one — then Caprock, Slate, Paper,

  default — each app palette's own terminal on its panel — then Dark
  Warm, Dark Cold, Book, Light Cold, Light Side, Sunlight, Dark Side,
  Catppuccin Mocha, Tokyo Night, Solarized Dark; MIT-licensed palettes,
  credited in `lib/termthemes.ts`), *Font* (JetBrains Mono, which ships with
  the UI, plus whichever of SF Mono, Menlo, Fira Code, Cascadia Code, Hack,
  Monaco, Consolas, DejaVu Sans Mono and Ubuntu Mono a canvas measures as
  installed), *Size* (10–20 px, default 13), *Line height* (1.00–1.60,
  default 1.2) and *Cursor* (bar, block, underline), over a preview in the chosen palette and face.
  Every open pane follows each change at once; kept per browser
  (`caprock.app.terminal`), with *Reset to the defaults*.
- **Editor**, on this machine when one is installed (F18) — which editor
  *Open in editor* uses; stored by the daemon (`editor`).
- **Subscriptions** ([ADR-048](08-decisions.md), `SubscriptionsSetting.tsx`,
  self-contained: its own fetch and state), on this machine, after *Default
  folder*. Per agent (Claude Code, Codex; an agent shown when installed or
  when it has a subscription), the own sign-in first (*Own sign-in* until
  renamed), then each added one: its name, a *default* badge on the one new
  sessions start on when none is picked (with more than one), the account it
  is signed in to or *not signed in*, its own plan when it has one, its
  current 5-hour and weekly windows (red at 90% or more, with the reset
  time; *no plan figures yet* otherwise), *7 days: N sessions, $X*, its
  folder (*· your own sign-in* on the first), and *Sign in* / *Sign in
  again*, *Make default* (on the others), *Rename* (inline, the own one too),
  *Remove* (not on the own one; inline confirm naming what is deleted;
  disabled while a session Caprock started runs on it). *Make default*
  forgets the picks this browser remembered for that agent, so it reaches
  every project until one picks another; running sessions stay where they
  are. A tester, 2026-10-12 (translated): "I can't change the default
  account … maybe I want to change it while working." The add form is Agent + Name + **Add subscription**;
  on success the sign-in command is typed into a shell tab
  (`SetupShellContext`, provided by the app shell) — in a browser, with no
  tabs, it is shown with *Copy*. At three for an agent without a licence the
  form says it is the free number and the button opens the Premium dialog
  (`subscriptions`) instead of asking the daemon, which refuses with 402
  anyway. A footer line says three per agent are free, that settings,
  plugins and servers are not copied, and that the user chooses which
  subscription each session uses and Caprock never switches one; a second
  says Claude Code keeps a Console sign-in without an API key outside the
  folders, so two of those are not kept apart (its authentication docs).
  When the login shell sets a credential variable the agent would use over a
  folder's sign-in (`ignored_env`), a note under that agent's list names it
  and says a session on an added subscription starts without it while the
  own sign-in's keep it. Remove's confirm on a signed-in added one also
  says, on macOS for Claude Code, that the sign-in is in the Keychain as
  *Claude Code-credentials-…* (`keychain_item`), or for Codex that a keyring
  sign-in is outside the folder, with **Sign out first**, which types the
  agent's own sign-out (`logout_command`) into a shell tab like *Sign in*.
  Under Claude Code's list, a **DeepSeek** block (`DeepSeekKey`): *off* or
  *key sk-…1a2b* (the daemon's mask), *N running* and *7 days* when there are
  any, a line saying a session on it sends its prompts, files and tool
  output to api.deepseek.com and that Caprock itself sends nothing — kept
  with explanations off (`data-keep`) — then, hideable, that it is billed to
  the user's DeepSeek key and does not use the Claude plan or its limits,
  with a link to DeepSeek's key page; a password field, **Save key** /
  **Replace key**, and **Remove key**. The DeepSeek entry is never a row of
  the list, never counted toward the free number and has no *Make default*,
  *Rename* or *Remove* of its own; Settings → Plan & licence does not list
  it either (it is billed per token by DeepSeek).
- **The Subscription picker** (`SubscriptionPicker.tsx`) sits in New agent
  under Agent (beside it a third column cut the selects' text off), in
  Quick chat under the agent row and in the New session dialog (the phone's
  start form, not remembered there), only when the agent has more than one.
  Options read `Work Max (default) · 18% 5-hour` or `Personal · not
  signed in`; the select's tooltip names the chosen one's e-mail. It opens on the remembered pick, else on the
  default one, and the request always names the choice (`default` for the
  own sign-in). A pick is remembered per viewer, agent and project
  (`caprock.subscription.<agent>.<project>`; Quick chat uses `-`); picking
  the default one remembers nothing. At 90% or
  more of a current window on the chosen one, a note says so with the reset
  time and offers the subscription with the most room as a link — a click
  picks it for this session only, and the note says running sessions stay
  where they are. Copy never speaks of avoiding a limit, and DeepSeek is
  never the one offered. With a DeepSeek key set, Claude Code's picker has
  a last option, `DeepSeek · your API key`, which is also what makes the
  picker appear for someone with a single sign-in; chosen, the note under it
  says the session runs on DeepSeek Flash whatever Model says, what it sends
  to api.deepseek.com, and that Caprock sends nothing. A session that ran on
  it is named *DeepSeek* even after the key is removed. The cockpit, the
  inspector and the session page name the subscription it ran on
  (`SubscriptionTag`), or *removed subscription*; once an agent has more
  than one sign-in, a session on the own one names it too. The inspector
  offers **Continue on another account… ▾** under the folder line when the
  agent has another: the relay dialog (ADR-032) starts a new session on the
  picked account with a brief of this one — a running session never changes
  account. The session page's *Continue in… ▾* lists each account of an
  agent that has several (`Claude Code · Work`).
- **A plan per account** (`AccountPlan.tsx`). Settings → *Plan & licence*,
  with more than one Claude Code sign-in, shows a row per account — name,
  e-mail, the plan that applies as a chip (*Max 20× · $200.00/mo*, *not
  set*) and under it *Its own plan.*, *No plan of its own: the main plan
  stands for it.* or, on the own sign-in, *The main plan: also used for
  sessions with no plan of their own account.* The chip opens the same plan
  list as the header, with *The main plan* first on an added
  account. With one sign-in the section is as it was. A session on an
  account is set against that account's plan: the cockpit's *Plan* chip
  shows and sets it, the cockpit's windows are that account's (not the own
  sign-in's), and the session page's cost basis reads it. The header chip
  and the screens that add up every session keep the main plan. A tester,
  2026-10-12 (translated): "I have different plans on the accounts and it's
  unclear what to choose here."
- **Global shortcut**, in the desktop app only (WP-10) — the key that shows
  or hides the window, changed by pressing a new one or turned off; one the
  system refuses is shown with its reason, and a Wayland session says global
  keys do not work there.
- **Updates** (2026-10-15). "Tell me when a new version is out" — the
  release check, every 15 minutes, on waking and on focus — then when it last asked (*Up to date
  · checked 5 min ago*, or *vX is out*) with **Check now**; in the desktop
  app where it can replace itself, *New version*: **Ask me** / **Update
  automatically**, with one line on what each does (Update automatically
  also switches the check on). An app that cannot update itself says why
  instead (`components/UpdatesSetting`).
- **Privacy.** One line: whether the release check is on (Updates, above),
  and that apart from it, the Telegram messages the owner sets up and
  GitHub once connected, Caprock sends nothing over the internet.
- **Memory between sessions.** "Claude knows what you did here last time", with
  what it is handed (the last thing a session in that folder wrote, a few
  paragraphs, past two weeks). **Measure whether it helps** is under
  **Experiments**.
- **Use Caprock from your agents (MCP)** ([ADR-078](08-decisions.md),
  `McpSetting.tsx`), after Second Brain, on the machine only. One paragraph
  kept on screen whatever the explanations setting says: what the agents
  can read, that it is read-only, that a tool's answer goes into the agent's
  conversation and so to its model, that key-shaped text is redacted, and
  that it opens no port. Then **Claude Code** with `claude mcp add caprock
  -- caprock mcp` as a copy button (a line on `--scope user`), **Codex**
  with `codex mcp add caprock -- caprock mcp` and the three
  `~/.codex/config.toml` lines in a block with its own **copy**, and a line
  for any other client (stdio, command, `mcp`). **Use the full path to
  caprock** (shown when `/v1/status` gives `executable`; on by default in
  the desktop app, which puts no `caprock` on the PATH) puts that path in
  every command, quoted for a shell when it holds a space and as a TOML
  literal string. **What your agents can read** folds the eleven tools, one
  line each. Nothing here switches anything on; the settings search finds
  it by *mcp*, *tools*, *agents*, *codex*, *claude*, *server*.
- **Storage** — see below.
- **About this install.** Version, uptime and event count on one line; the
  daemon's full table (pricing, loop rule, hooks, ingest…) under **Details**,
  scrolling inside itself on a phone.

A paired device sees Appearance (and Terminal, in the app workspace), Storage and About only; the rest change on the
machine Caprock runs on (ADR-029).

### Use on your phone

**Plain, one question first (2026-10-18).** The owner (translated): "I don't
understand at all what's going on here — not one clear piece of info." The
section then had a status pill, a four-step checklist, a QR with three
paragraphs beside it, and device rows each carrying a role, a route, two
dates, an id, a sentence about m.caprock.dev and three buttons. Every piece
was true; together they did not answer what a person opening it wants to
know — is my phone connected? Rebuilt (`Pairing.tsx`, id `settings-phone`),
top to bottom:

- **One sentence** (`phone-status`, `phoneSummary`): *iPhone · Safari is
  connected and can control sessions · seen 2 min ago* (seen in the last 5
  minutes; before that *is set up and … · last seen 3 days ago*), *· 1 more
  device* when there are others, *No phone connected yet.* when no device
  other than a computer (ADR-067) was seen in 30 days, and *Waiting for your
  phone to scan the code…* while one is out. A phone that pairs while the
  panel is open is the one it names. What is in the way is said in the same
  sentence with the one fix beside it: access from anywhere off (*is set up,
  but access from anywhere is off, so it can't reach this computer.*
  **Turn it on**), the machine offline (*can't get online right now (error).
  Retrying.*), or a phone that can only view (**Allow control**). The
  section's header carries no status pill any more: the sentence is it.
- **One button**, **Connect a phone** (*Connect another phone* once one is
  connected). Under it the tick **It can control sessions: type, start
  agents, approve, stop** (*View only: it can read, not type* unticked;
  `allow-type`, remembered as `caprock.pair.allowType` on this computer):
  the press sends it as the code's role (`POST /v1/pair/code {role}`,
  ADR-034 amended 2026-10-14), so the phone is a controller the moment it
  pairs, decided here, never by the phone. With access from anywhere off, a
  line under it (`anywhere-consent`) says the press turns it on for this
  computer and keeps it on — the decision on the machine ADR-058 asks for.
  The press turns it on (`POST /v1/pair/remote {on: true}`), issues the code
  and draws its QR (`remote.link`, up to 240 px) with one line, *Scan with
  your phone's camera. Works once, 4:43 left.*, and a quiet **New code ·
  Cancel**; **Cancel** withdraws it on the daemon (`DELETE /v1/pair/code`).
  While it is out the state is polled every 1.5 s, so the sentence turns to
  the phone within a couple of seconds. A daemon without the relay (no
  `remote` in the state) falls back to the network listener: the button
  turns it on and the code shows the address, the six digits and *Only on
  this Wi-Fi* (`pair-reach`).
- **Your devices**: one row each (`device-row`): the name, one capability
  word (`device-can`: *Can control*, *View only*; another computer reads
  *Computer · waiting for Allow* or *Computer · controls this one*), *seen 3
  days ago*, and two small buttons: **Allow control** or **Take control
  away** (ADR-034; taking it away holds from the phone's next request, and
  an open terminal on it stops taking keys) and **Remove** — while the guest
  list is not live too, from `devices.json`. A computer has only Remove.
  When it was connected, how it reaches the machine (`via`) and whether it
  runs as the installed app are in the name's tooltip; the first four
  characters of the id show only beside two devices with one name. A phone
  still on a Wi-Fi link (`needsRepair`: last came over the network listener,
  no twin of its name through the relay since) has one line, *Works on your
  home Wi-Fi only.* **Reconnect**, which is Connect a phone.
- **Old devices.** Devices not seen for 30 days (`isOldDevice`; one never
  seen counts from pairing; a computer waiting for Allow never folds) fold
  into *N old devices · not seen for 30+ days* (`old-devices`), unfolded by
  a click. **Remove all** unfolds them so the reader sees what goes and asks
  once — *Remove all 2? They stop working at once.* **Remove 2** · **Keep**.
- **How it works**, a closed disclosure at the bottom (`phone-how`): the
  three steps (press, scan, tap the link; optionally Add to Home Screen /
  Install app); that it works on Wi-Fi or mobile data, encrypted end to end
  through a relay that cannot read it, and only devices connected here get
  in; while a code is out, **Copy link** for an iPhone home-screen app
  (which keeps its own storage, and the camera opens Safari); *Closed the
  page on a phone you already connected? Just open m.caprock.dev on it
  again.* with **Show the address as a code** (`OpenAgain`, Vova,
  2026-10-15: "I don't remember the link — and that's it": a QR of the
  page's address alone, `phonePage`, which pairs nothing; the Add to Home
  Screen tip; that a VPN blocking the page or the relay shows as *Can't
  reach the relay*; with a Telegram bot set up, **Send the link to my
  phone** — *One message to your Telegram chat through your own bot …: the
  address …, no code and no key*, `POST /v1/pair/send-link`); *Access from
  anywhere is on.* with **Turn off** (`relay-state`); and, while the network
  listener is on (`caprock up --lan`, or switched on before), *Also open on
  this Wi-Fi at <address> — that link works only on the same network* with
  **Turn off** (`wifi-listener`). Its lines are not hidden by Settings'
  explanations switch: the reader opened it on purpose.

The four-step checklist (*Access from anywhere*, *Phone paired*, *Can type
from the phone*, *App installed on the phone*) and the numbered steps above
the button were removed: the sentence says the same in one line, and its
**Allow control** is the checklist's one fix. The phone's own pages name the
buttons by these words (*Settings → Use on your phone → Connect a phone*,
*… → Allow control*).

**Phone and Telegram in Settings (2026-10-13).** The owner (translated):
"To the right of Settings it says 'phone, Telegram', I go in there and don't
understand where phone and Telegram are." Right after any problems, Settings
opens with *Use on your phone*, then Telegram (`PhoneAlerts.tsx`, id
`telegram`), then *Talk to your agents from Telegram*, in both the app and
the browser. `#/settings/phone` and `#/settings/telegram` (`router.ts`,
`settings.section`) land on their section: unfolded (`REVEAL_EVENT` in
`SettingsParts.tsx`), scrolled to the top and lit for about 1.4 s
(`revealSection`). In the app sidebar the Settings row's *phone* and
*Telegram* are separate buttons calling `openSettingsSection`, and the row
itself goes to the phone. Only a click — the sidebar's *phone* or the
palette's *Open Caprock on your phone* — also shows a code
(`takeCodeOnArrival`), and only when access from anywhere is already on; a
link alone only scrolls, and an arrival never turns the relay on — the
**Connect a phone** button, with its line saying so, is that decision.

**One way in: from anywhere (2026-10-14, ADR-058 amended).** The owner
(translated): "I'd remove every option except the normal online one — who
cares about working only inside your own Wi-Fi; everyone wants to work
online." The same morning his Android phone had paired on the Wi-Fi address
(`http://10.100.102.7:4173`) a minute before he turned on *From anywhere*,
kept that link, and reached nothing on mobile data. So the panel offers the
relay alone: no Wi-Fi / Tailscale switch, no *Keep on after Caprock
restarts* box. The network listener stays in the daemon for a machine
without internet, reached from a terminal (`caprock up --lan`, `caprock
pair`); see docs/app.md.

**The QR code is drawn in the browser** by `ui/src/lib/qr.ts` (byte mode,
level M, versions 1–10, no dependency), black on white whatever the theme.
The link carries a code that lets a device in; it is not sent anywhere to be
rendered.

**On the phone, a Wi-Fi link says so** (`LanOnlyNotice.tsx`, above every
screen of the browser dashboard on a paired device): when `GET /v1/pair/me`
answers `via: "lan"`, a quiet strip reads *This link works only on your home
Wi-Fi. To use Caprock anywhere: in the Caprock app on your computer, Settings
→ Use on your phone → Connect a phone, and scan it.*, with × to hide it until the
page loads again. With access from anywhere on (`anywhere`, and `app`, the
phone's page) it calls itself the old link instead: *This is the old Wi-Fi
link: it works only on your home Wi-Fi. Use the Caprock app from
m.caprock.dev instead: … Then remove this one from your home screen.* — a
home-screen icon of the Wi-Fi link beside the app is two icons named
Caprock, one dead off the Wi-Fi. Never over the relay, over Tailscale, or on
the machine.

### Storage

A **Storage** section answers "how much does all this take, and what is it?"
— on Settings rather than a screen of its own, because it is a figure checked
once in a while and not a view anyone works in. It reads `GET /v1/storage` once
a minute ([03-contracts.md § Storage](03-contracts.md#storage)) and is shown to a
paired device too, since it changes nothing. The headline stays on screen; the
bars and the levers are under **Details**.

- **The headline** is the data directory's total, the growth rate, and how many
  events since when. The growth rate is labelled "estimate, last 30 days" and
  says how it was made on hover; it is the panel's only derived figure.
- **On disk** splits the directory into the events table, its indexes, the
  other tables, free pages (when there are any), the write-ahead log, logs
  (every `*.log` as one row) and whatever else is there, each with its size
  and share of the total.
- **Recorded, by agent / by kind** split the bytes the events recorded — the
  raw payload, not the file — because indexes and page overhead belong to
  nobody in particular. The shares are of that payload.
- **The levers** are stated as sentences under the bars. Retention: whether
  events are kept forever, how much is older than 90 and 30 days, how to set
  `retention_days`, and what happens to the space deleted rows leave: given
  back to the disk while Caprock is idle once `returns_free_space` is true,
  reused by new events until then. Compaction: how much of the file is free
  space, and that Caprock gives it back on its own (or, before the one
  idle-time VACUUM, that it will run one when the disk has room for a second
  copy). There is **no Compact button** and no command for the user to run:
  the daemon does it ([03-contracts.md § Payload compaction and
  auto-vacuum](03-contracts.md#payload-compaction-and-auto-vacuum-no-migration)).
- **The header says when the figures were measured**, and `· measuring…` while
  a new measurement runs: opening the panel on figures a day old starts one in
  the background, and the next poll shows its result.

## Plan value

A **plan chip in the header** states how you pay for Claude Code — Pro, Max, a Team seat, a custom monthly price, or metered (API key / Bedrock / Vertex / Enterprise at API rates). Caprock cannot detect the plan and never guesses, so the chip reads `set plan` until you say; it is one click from being changed when your plan changes, and the value is stored locally in `config.json`.

The Cost screen then leads with what the measured usage is worth, scaled to the selected range:

- **Flat plan** — a single multiple ("35.7×") over two lines: what you pay for the window, and what the same usage would have cost at API list price. Never phrased as money saved: without the plan you would not have run that much, so "saved $X" would be an invented number.
- **Metered** — no multiple at all. The API-list figure is approximately the real bill, so it is labelled as spend and explicitly marked "not a saving".
- **Not stated** — nothing is claimed; the panel invites you to set the plan.

### Incomplete and background usage

When a public model id has no price, the Cost, Now, and History screens show a
compact **Partial cost** note with the measured token count and model id. The
primary row states only that the total is incomplete; `copy model` copies the
id without usage or project data, `report` opens a prefilled privacy-safe issue,
and `why?` reveals that the total is a lower bound and that no manual price is
expected. It never calls the usage free. The issue link contains model ids only,
not prompts, paths, project names, token counts, or costs.

Known internal machinery is a different state. `codex-auto-review` is shown as
muted **Background usage** with measured tokens and no warning or action;
it produces no warning, no user session, no turn/cost total, and no report
action. The raw events remain available for audit, while user totals stay
reconcilable.

Rules are unit-tested in `ui/src/components/Unpriced.test.tsx`.

## Mail

Caprock Mail at `#/mail` ([ADR-057](08-decisions.md), API in
[03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md)). Code: `ui/src/screens/mail`, loaded
only when the screen opens; styles scoped under `.cm` and drawn from the
theme's tokens, so Mail follows every palette.

- **In the desktop app, a window of its own** (owner, 2026-10-15,
  translated: "Mail still launches in a crappy way; it should be native —
  either call the installed app, or show a big banner to go there"). The
  sidebar footer's *Mail ⇧⌘M* row, ⇧⌘M (Ctrl+Shift+M off macOS; plain ⌘M
  stays the window's Minimize) and the palette's *Open Mail* ask the shell,
  in order (`lib/mailwindow.ts` `openMailOutside`): `open_mail_app`, which on
  macOS launches or brings forward the installed **Caprock Mail** preview
  (`app/mail-mac`, in /Applications or ~/Applications); else
  `open_mail_window`, a second native window of the app, *Caprock Mail*, on
  the daemon's `/?mail=1` (Mail alone, `MailApp.tsx`), focused when already
  open. Only a shell that has neither command (older than this change) gets
  the Mail tab below. A `#/mail` reached any other way — the dashboard's
  *Mail*, the Mail strip's button — opens Mail the same way and goes back
  to the hash it came from; a Mail tab kept in the workspace from before is
  dropped.
- **In a browser, a tab under a banner.** The same entries go to `#/mail`,
  the **Mail tab**: pinned after the Dashboard and Pipeline tabs, its
  envelope and its key (`⇧⌘M`, `Ctrl+Shift+M` off macOS while Ctrl is held), in the strip from the
  first time Mail is opened until its × (`Workspace.mail`, kept across
  restarts). On top of it a full-width banner, *Mail works best in its own
  window* (`MailWindowBanner`, `data-banner="mail-window"`, the envelope
  scene), whose button *Open in its own window ⇧⌘M* (the key through
  `keys()`) opens `/?mail=1` in a browser window named `caprock-mail`, so a
  second click brings the same window up. ⇧⌘M with the Mail tab in front
  does the same; from anywhere else it brings the tab to the front. The
  screen fills the rest of the tab (`MailScreen fill`), with no dashboard
  header; ⌘0 goes to the Dashboard tab and leaves the Mail tab behind it;
  closing the Mail tab goes back to the terminals. The plain dashboard's
  `#/mail` (no app layout) shows the banner too, without a key, as it binds
  none.
- **Reached from** the dashboard's screen row, last item *Mail* with an
  accent *new* mark, from the palette (*Mail*, *Open Mail*), and once from the **Mail strip**: an
  edge banner, *New: Caprock Mail*, shown after two days with sessions and
  only while no mail account exists (`mailEligible`). *Open Mail* answers it
  for good (`mail-done`), × is "not now" for 30 days (`mail-later`); opening
  the screen answers it too.
- **Opens on the sample inbox.** With no account and sample mode off, the
  screen turns sample mode on by itself and shows *Opening a sample inbox…*
  meanwhile (owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "I want to click the button and
  see how cool it is … button → something opens at once"). Measured on a
  preview daemon, 2026-10-14: the Mail tab in 54 ms, the sample inbox with its
  rows 0.85 s after the click. While the inbox is still filling (the first
  sync) the screen polls every 400 ms instead of 4 s. A line on top says *This
  is a sample inbox — add your account to see your own mail*, with *Exit
  sample* (back to the first run; the sample is not started again in this
  window until a reload) and **Add account**, a menu: Google, Microsoft,
  iCloud (each straight to that provider's sign-in or form), Other (every
  provider's tile: Yahoo and Fastmail keep their app-password steps) and
  Import from this computer (Apple Mail, Thunderbird). Each leaves the sample
  first. The daemon leaves it too when a real account is added from anywhere
  ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md) § Mail), so the account never lands in
  the sample.
- **First run** (no account, sample left) is a card with three
  choices (`Onboarding.tsx`), owner request 2026-10-14 (translated: "why
  can't it import right at the start?"):
  - **Try it with sample mail** turns sample mode on again (`POST
    /v1/mail/sample`): two made-up accounts on in-process fake servers.
    Settings' *Add account* in sample mode leaves it and opens the tiles.
  - **Import everything** (`ImportAll.tsx`, the walk in `importFlow.ts`;
    owner, 2026-10-14, translated: one screen where Caprock finds every mail
    account on the computer, the user ticks them, presses Import and is
    walked through each one's remaining sign-in) is shown only when `GET
    /v1/mail/import` answers (a 404/405/501 hides it) and says how many accounts it found,
    naming only the apps it could read. Only what can be read is shown
    (owner, 2026-10-14, translated: "don't even show me this; the Apple
    integration is unclear, not concrete about what to do"): a source with no
    readable account — Apple Mail without Full Disk Access, a profile with an
    error — is simply absent, with no instructions, error line or *Retry*.
    The daemon's merged list is grouped by the app each account imports from,
    under one line that says where it came from — *Thunderbird · profile
    last used 12 Mar 2025*, *Apple Mail · account list last changed …* (the
    profile's last write) — and each account row is its address, a checkbox
    and a chip for its one remaining step: *Password saved*, *Sign in with
    Google / Microsoft*, *App password* / *App-specific password*, or
    *Password*; an address Mail already has is unticked, *Already in Mail*.
    Accounts whose profile was not used in 90 days start unticked. *Import N
    accounts* starts the walk, one account at a time: those with a saved
    password connect by themselves first, then each other account is the
    step on screen — the browser sign-in (with the address as the provider's
    login hint; *Waiting for you to approve…*, *Cancel*, *Use an app password
    instead*, followed through `GET /v1/mail/oauth/flow`), iCloud's
    app-specific password with a link to appleid.apple.com and one line on
    how, another provider's app password with its link and steps, or the
    server's password. A provider this build cannot sign in with says
    *Sign-in with Google isn't set up in this build — use an app password*
    (or Microsoft) and takes the app password, never a dead button. A
    refusal stays on the step with the server's words, once (the progress
    row does not repeat it while the step is on screen), and *Try again*;
    *Skip for now* moves on. Below, the progress list: connected ✓, Now,
    Waiting, Connecting…, Failed with the reason and *Retry*, Skipped with
    *Come back*. A saved password that is refused turns its row into a
    password step and the walk goes on. While the walk is on screen Mail
    keeps it there (`onBusy`) though the first account landing would show
    the inbox; *Open Mail* ends it. The list is read again whenever the
    window regains focus. Settings' *Add account* tiles end with a link to
    the same screen.
  - **Add an account** shows provider tiles: Google, iCloud, Outlook /
    Microsoft 365, Yahoo, Fastmail, Other. Google and Outlook offer *Sign in
    with …* when `GET /v1/mail/oauth/providers` says so: the provider's page
    opens in the browser and the card waits (*Waiting for you to approve in
    the browser…*, *Cancel*) until a new account appears in the state. With
    no sign-in, or a missing route, they fall back to the address-and-password
    form. iCloud always takes that form, with one line on why (Apple has no
    sign-in for other mail apps). The form carries the provider's
    app-password steps and link; *Other* is the plain form with servers
    filled in from the address (or its MX record). A refused login shows the
    server's words and opens the server fields.
  - Settings' *Add account* uses the same tiles. Opening the screen before
    any account creates nothing: state, settings and the list answer from
    nothing.
- **Three columns**: views (Inbox, Unread, Starred, Sent, Drafts, Archive,
  Spam, Trash across all accounts) then each account's inbox and own folders;
  the list, one row per conversation with unread dot, count, star, clip and
  the account's colour bar, archive/delete/read on hover; the reader, the
  thread oldest first with unread and newest messages open, attachments as
  chips, a reply box at the foot.
- **Keys** (ignored while typing): `j k` move, `e` archive, `#` delete, `!`
  spam, `s` star, `u` read/unread, `r` reply, `a` reply all, `f` forward,
  `c` compose, `/` search, `g i` / `g s` / `g t` / `g a` / `g d` go to
  Inbox, Starred, Sent, Archive, Drafts, Escape closes. The composer sends on
  ⌘↩ (Ctrl+Enter off macOS) and closes on Escape.
- **Junk stays out of the Inbox** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "there's a
  lot of spam in my inbox — every mail client filters this"; his provider
  leaves junk in INBOX and Apple Mail hid it locally). The local filter
  (`internal/mail/spam`, [03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md) § Mail) runs on
  by default, with no AI and no network. **Spam in the inbox is one notion**
  for the whole screen (`isSpam` in `junk.ts`, the daemon's `SpamSQL`): the
  filter's flag or the AI triage's spam label (below). Inbox, Unread and
  their counts leave it out. **Spam** (`g !`) carries its count as the
  badge — the same number as the inbox's Spam filter `5` — and lists that
  inbox spam first, each conversation once, the filter's catch before the
  AI's, under *Caprock thinks these are junk · hidden from the Inbox,
  nothing moved on the server*, then the server's Junk folder under *In
  your Spam folder*. Every spam row has *Not spam* on hover, and `n`
  (`MAIL_KEYS.notSpam`, on the `?` sheet next to Spam) acts on the selected
  one through the one `notjunk` action: hidden mail shows in the Inbox
  again, mail in the Junk folder moves back, the filter learns it, the AI's
  spam label is taken back, and that sender is never spam again; one toast,
  *Not spam — back in the Inbox*. A row shows one *Spam* label whichever
  source said so (its tooltip names which). The reader of a spam message
  says so in a note under the subject — the filter's reasons in words (up
  to three), that the AI sorted it as spam (its one-liner shows below), or
  both — and its toolbar swaps Spam for *Not spam `n`*.
- **HTML mail** renders in a sandboxed iframe under a CSP with no script and
  no network; remote images wait behind *Load images* unless Mail's settings
  say always.
- **Keyboard first, keys on every action** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated:
  "I want to use mail with the keyboard, that's the main thing; shortcuts
  visible everywhere"). One list names every key, `screens/mail/keymap.tsx`
  (`MAIL_KEYS`), and the buttons, the folder list and the `?` sheet all read
  it: `j`/`k` move, `c` compose, `r`/`a`/`f` reply, reply all, forward, `e`
  archive, `#` delete, `!` spam, `n` not spam, `v` move (a numbered folder list, `j`/`k` or
  a digit, Enter), `s` star, `u` unread, `i` summarise, `/` search, `,`
  settings, `t` theme, `g r` check mail, `g` + `i u s t d a ! #` the folders,
  `1`–`5` the inbox's AI filters, `n` not spam, `?` every shortcut, Esc back; in the composer ⌘↩ send and ⌘J draft with AI
  (through `keys()` off macOS). Each action shows its key as a small chip; the
  folder list's keys are dimmed until hovered or selected. **`t`** opens the
  theme list (Caprock's palettes): `j`/`k` show each at once, Enter keeps,
  Esc puts the old one back, a digit picks — Sunlight outdoors, Dark Pastel
  at night (owner, 2026-10-15). The button under the folders shows the
  palette's swatch and `t`.
- **AI assistant** (Mail's settings, off by default): *Summarise* above the
  thread — shown even while AI is off: the first press names the provider and
  where the conversation would go, and *Turn on and summarise ↩* switches it
  on — and *Draft reply* in the composer, both through the provider picked
  there. The picker lists *Automatic* (named after the provider it found:
  the first installed of Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, else the local
  server), then each provider, marked *(not installed)* or *(needs a key)*
  when it cannot answer. Below it, by kind: a model field (placeholder: the
  CLI's or API's hint), a write-only API key field with *Save* and *Remove*
  (its placeholder says *Saved in your keychain* once one is), or the local
  server's address and model list. One line under it always says which
  provider answers (with its model) and where the mail text goes, or why none can
  ([ADR-057](08-decisions.md) amendment). Its paragraph says that while AI
  is on, inbox sorting sends the sender, subject and start of each
  conversation there. **Language** (`ai_language`): *Same as the email*,
  English, Russian, Spanish, German — what summaries and triage one-liners
  are written in; the hint under it says a drafted reply stays in the
  language of the mail it answers unless its instruction says otherwise
  (owner, 2026-10-15).
- **Who does the AI work is named wherever AI acts** (owner, 2026-10-15,
  translated: the provider and model pickers existed but he did not find
  them): the triage line under the inbox filters (*Sorted by Claude Code ·
  haiku · change*), the foot of every summary (*by Claude Code · haiku ·
  change*) and the composer's AI bar. Each *change* opens Settings
  scrolled to the AI section (`SettingsPanel section="ai"`); `,` still opens
  it at the top.
- **AI triage** (owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "there is no proper spam
  filtering, my inbox is a mess … I want the AI to sort my mail: show
  labels, work out what's what, and give summaries"). While AI is on the
  daemon sorts inbox conversations in the background
  ([03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md) § Mail, *AI triage*). In the inbox
  (not in a search or another folder):
  - **Filters** along the top of the list: *All* `1` (everything that is
    not spam, the junk filter's catch left out too), *Important* `2`,
    *Updates* `3`, *Newsletters* `4`, *Spam* `5` — the union, the junk
    filter's catch first, then the AI's, with one count; each shows its
    key, and a key pressed in another folder goes to the inbox with that
    filter. The filter resets to All when the view changes. Shown while AI
    is on, in sample mode, or once the AI has labelled a conversation (the
    junk filter's catch alone does not bring them up).
  - **Rows** carry a small coloured label — *Important* (accent), *Update*
    (ok), *Newsletter* (warn), *Spam* (danger, also for the junk filter's
    catch), or a filled *Reply* when the last message waits on the user —
    before the subject, and the AI's one-line summary in place of the
    snippet.
  - **A status line** under the filters: *Sorting 37 conversations… ·
    Claude Code · haiku* while a run is on, else *Sorted by … · change*;
    in sample mode *Sample labels: made up with the sample inbox, no AI is
    asked*.
  - **Spam is only a label**: nothing moves on the server by itself. `!`
    confirms it (moves to the server's Junk with the existing action, and
    the junk filter learns it); `n` *Not spam* is the `notjunk` action
    described under *Junk stays out of the Inbox* above, which takes the
    AI's label back for good (`important`) as well as the filter's flag.
  - **AI off**: one quiet line above the list — *Let AI sort your inbox into
    Important, Updates, Newsletters and Spam. Sorting sends the sender,
    subject and start of each conversation to {where}.* with **Turn on**
    (`PUT /v1/mail/settings {ai_enabled: "1"}`, the reader's turn-on), or
    *Set up* opening Settings on AI when no provider is available; × puts it
    away in this browser (`caprock.mail.triage-offer`).
  - **Reader**: a stored one-liner shows at once above the thread, with
    *by … · change · Full summary i*; `i` still writes the full summary.
  - **Sample mode** labels the sample inbox from a canned table (no model
    call): it gains a promotion and a phishing message so every filter has
    rows. The sample's junk (four messages with failing checks or a
    provider spam header) is caught by the junk filter and never labelled,
    so its Spam shows both sources: five conversations, one count.
- **Freshness**: the screen polls `version` every 4 s while open and visible.

## Private modules

A screen for each private module an admin key opens ([ADR-076](08-decisions.md),
API in [03-contracts.md § Private modules](03-contracts.md#private-modules-v1admin-xid-v1xiddocs)).
Without a valid key none of this is drawn: no row, no pill, no locked tab, no
words in Settings.

- **Where:** in the app, one row per module above the sidebar footer, its
  initial on an accent square, its name and its key (`components/ModuleRows.tsx`);
  in a browser, a nav pill after Mail. Both open `#/x/<id>` on the dashboard.
- **Keys:** ⇧⌘1–⇧⌘9 (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+1–9 off macOS) open the modules in the
  rows' order (owner, 2026-10-17; ⌥⌘1–9 on a Mac until 2026-10-20, when
  the owner asked for Ops, the first, on ⇧⌘1). Matched by the key's code
  (`Digit1`…), since Shift makes the key a symbol and layouts differ; off
  macOS Ctrl+Shift+digit stays the tab jump, so the modules keep Alt there
  (`OFF_MAC_ALT`). ⇧⌘digit is no item of Tauri's default menu nor a WKWebView
  default, and ⌘ never reaches a shell. **macOS takes ⇧⌘3, ⇧⌘4 and ⇧⌘5**
  for its screenshots before any app sees them, while those shortcuts are
  on (the default): modules three to five keep their key — it works on a
  Mac where they are turned off — but their row draws no chip there and its
  tooltip says the key is macOS's (`macTakenModuleKey`); the row and the
  palette still open them. Bound only to as many modules as there are: with
  none the chord is nobody's, so an AltGr layout still types with it. Not in
  the shortcuts sheet, which everyone reads; the row shows it.
- **The screen** (`screens/ModuleScreen.tsx`) is a frame on `/x/<id>/` that
  fills the screen. The app's theme goes in on load and on every palette
  change (`lib/modules.ts`): `data-theme`, `color-scheme` and every
  `--color-*` and `--font-*` token on the frame's `<html>`, and a
  `{type: "caprock:theme", theme, vars}` message; `?theme=` on the URL for the
  first paint. A module written against the same token names is drawn in the
  app's palette with nothing to do.
- **The key:** pasted into Settings → License, the Premium key's field. A
  `CRA1.` key goes to `PUT /v1/admin` instead of settings; once stored the
  field says *Admin key is on — <modules>* with *remove*, and a stored key
  that no longer verifies says why.

## Other computers

The sessions on the user's other computers — a Mac mini, a Linux box, a
Windows PC — seen and typed into from this one ([ADR-067](08-decisions.md),
Phase 1 PR3; API in [03-contracts.md § Other computers](03-contracts.md)).
The dashboard only ever talks to its own daemon; that daemon carries every
request to the other computer through `/v1/m/{id}/…`. Code:
`lib/machines.ts`, `lib/machinepage.ts`, `components/MachineRows.tsx`,
`components/Computers.tsx`, `screens/Machine.tsx`, `screens/MachinePage.tsx`.

- **The sidebar (app).** Under the projects, inside the list's scroll:
  - *None linked:* one quiet row, **+ Add a machine**, in the faint colour of
    *More…*, opening Settings → Computers (`#/settings/computers`). No badge,
    no count, no modal — the owner's install hook (2026-10-15, translated:
    "encourage installing Caprock on their other computers").
  - *Linked:* a **Computers** heading, one row per computer (a dot, its name
    here, and its state when it is not simply reachable: *offline*, *waiting
    for Allow*, *removed there*, *refused*; else how many run there), and
    under it up to six running sessions, each tagged with the computer's
    name in a small outlined chip so it never reads as this machine's. A
    session row opens `#/m/<id>/session/<sid>`; the computer's row opens
    `#/m/<id>`. Their sessions are read every 15 s while the window is in
    view (`useMachineSessions`), so a computer's channel is up while the app
    shows it and closes 90 s after it does not (ADR-067, connected on demand).
- **A computer's screen** (`#/m/<id>`): its name, state and version, then
  *Running* and *Recent* (ended, 20 at most), read through the proxy every
  15 s. Offline says why in words (*the relay could not be reached*, *it is
  not at its relay: asleep, off the internet, Caprock not running there, or
  its access from anywhere is off*), the raw error in the tooltip, with *Try
  again*. Starting a session there from here is Phase 2 (the Machine picker
  in New agent); the screen says to start it there for now.
- **One of its sessions** (`#/m/<id>/session/<sid>`): a back link to the
  computer, and a frame of this same dashboard at
  `/?machine=<id>#/session/<sid>?tab=terminal` — *the machine page*. Its
  `main.tsx` rewrites every `fetch` and `WebSocket` for `/v1/…` and `/healthz`
  to `/v1/m/<id>/…` before anything asks (`installMachineProxy`, as the
  phone's page sends them through its relay), so the Session view, its
  Terminal tab (terminal protocol v2) and `/v1/live` are the ordinary ones,
  reading and typing into the other computer as this machine's paired device
  there. The machine page never watches the UI version (the other daemon's
  differs on purpose), is never the app workspace, draws no *Open in my
  terminal* (that opens a window on the other computer's screen, which a
  computer may not do there), and hands any other route a link inside it opens
  back to the window as `#/m/<id>`. When the channel drops — the relay
  restarted, the network moved — the terminal reopens on a new channel and
  resumes from its byte offset (verified 2026-10-18, below).
- **Settings → Computers** (`#settings-computers`, after Telegram; the
  machine only). Rebuilt 2026-10-20 on the mockup the owner approved, after
  he said of the old one (2026-10-19, translated): "here it's unclear what to
  do". Top to bottom:
  - **Connected computers:** this one first, *This Mac · this one · main* (or
    *· connects to another*, after the card below); then each computer this
    one reaches (`GET /v1/machines`): name, state, *reached from here ·
    Caprock <version> · answered <when> · through <relay host>*, what to do
    in a state that needs something (pending: allow it there, the card or
    `caprock machines allow`; revoked: show a new link there and add it
    again, or disconnect it; refused: another key, add it again; an old
    target: update Caprock there for full control), and **Open**, **Rename**
    (here only), **Disconnect** (a confirm in place; the old *Forget*:
    deletes it and its token here and asks it to drop this one); then each
    computer that reaches this one (the `kind: "machine"` devices of
    `GET /v1/pair/state`, re-read on the `devices` live frame): *reaches this
    one* or *waiting for Allow*, *connected <when>*, **Allow** while it
    waits, **Disconnect** (a confirm; `DELETE /v1/pair/devices/{id}`).
    **Check** asks each computer this one reaches how it stands
    (`?check=1`); the section asks on open, and re-reads the local list
    every 30 s;
  - **What is this computer?** Two cards, a radio group: **The main one**
    (*Other computers and the phone connect to it*) is the side that pastes a
    link and reaches the others, ADR-067's controller; **Connects to
    another** (*Its sessions show on the main one*) is the side that shows a
    link and is reached, the target. The daemon has no such mode — pairing is
    per link and one way, and a computer can be both — so the cards pick
    only which steps are shown. The pick is kept in this browser
    (`caprock.computers.role`); before one is made, a computer that others
    reach and that reaches none opens on *Connects to another*, any other on
    *The main one*;
  - **Add a computer (i)**, three numbered steps whose number turns into a
    tick when it is done. On *the main one*: 1 install Caprock on the other
    computer and choose *Connects to another* there (folded under it: *How
    to install, or do it from its terminal* — `brew install
    dspv/tap/caprock`, `scoop install caprock` or Releases, then `caprock
    up`; `caprock remote on`; `caprock pair --computer`, the link, five
    minutes, once; Allow there by name; the Linux linger note); 2 *Paste the
    link it shows* and **Add** (a refused link, a computer not taking one,
    an offline one and a timeout each say what to do); 3 Allow this computer
    there. Steps 1 and 2 tick once a computer is added, 3 once one no longer
    waits for Allow and was not removed or refused there. On *connects to
    another*: 1 *Turn on access from anywhere* with **Turn on**
    (`POST /v1/pair/remote`), then *— on* with its state and **Turn off**;
    2 on the main computer choose *The main one*; 3 **Show the link**
    (`POST /v1/pair/code {kind: "machine"}`, which turns access from
    anywhere on — the press is that decision, ADR-058 amended, and the line
    beside it says so) shows *Enter this link there: valid N more min, once
    · Copy* and the link; the arriving computer is allowed on the
    `ComputerAsks` card or in its row. Step 1 ticks while access from
    anywhere is on, 2 once a computer has redeemed the link, 3 once one is
    allowed;
  - **the (i)** beside *Add a computer* (a disclosure, closed by default)
    holds what the relay is and what it sends (ADR-047): each link is one
    way; the computer that connects to another waits at the relay with
    access from anywhere on — switched on in step 1, by *Show the link*, by
    *Connect a phone*, or `caprock remote on`, off the same way or `caprock
    remote off` — and this computer's relay host; and the line that used to
    sit under the field: *Adding dials the relay the link names, the one the
    other computer chose. What goes through it is end-to-end encrypted: the
    relay sees that two machines talk, when and how much, never what.*
  - Verified 2026-10-20 with two preview daemons and a loopback relay: link
    shown on one, pasted on the other, *waiting for Allow*, Allowed in the
    row, *online*, every step ticked, at 1280 and 390 px with no sideways
    scroll.
- **New agent's "This Mac is busy — run it on <machine>?"** is not drawn.
  `/v1/resources` carries the machine-wide figures it needs (`machine`, from
  2026-10-18), but its thresholds are set from OQ-19's measurement, which
  does not exist yet ([12-risks.md](12-risks.md)); until then nothing says a
  machine is busy or free.
- **Verified 2026-10-18** in sandboxes: two real daemons (temp HOME and data
  directory, spare ports, a clean environment, the file secret store, the
  fake `claude`), `caprock remote relay` on loopback, this branch's UI under
  `vite preview` in front of the controller, Playwright's headless shell:
  *+ Add a machine* → Settings → Computers; paste, *waiting for Allow*;
  `caprock machines allow` on the target; *online*; the session in the
  sidebar tagged by its computer; typed into its terminal through the frame
  and the echo back; the relay killed and restarted under the open terminal,
  which reopened (`since=<offset>`) and took keys again; the relay gone, the
  computer's screen *offline* with the reason; `caprock machines revoke` on
  the target, *removed there* in Settings and the sidebar; **Forget**.

## Second Brain

What the sessions decided, found and left open, kept as memories linked to
their projects and topics ([ADR-063](08-decisions.md), API in
[03-contracts.md](03-contracts.md) § Second Brain). Code:
`ui/src/screens/brain` (loaded only when opened), `lib/brain.ts`,
`components/BrainCapture.tsx`, `components/BrainDrop.tsx`,
`components/BrainAutoSetting.tsx`. All of it is free — no allowance, no
lock, no Premium dialog anywhere in it (owner decision, 2026-10-14).

- **Its name and mark.** Shown as *Second Brain* wherever it is named (it
  was *Brain* until the owner asked for the full name, 2026-10-14); routes,
  ids and the API keep `brain`. Its mark, `BrainGlyph` (a brain in outline,
  the same drawing as its Settings icon; it was a drop falling into a bowl,
  which did not read as a brain), sits beside the name everywhere: the sidebar heading, the
  screen row, the screen's title, ⌘K and the drop button. One faint line
  says what it is under the sidebar heading and the screen's title:
  *Remembers what your agents learn — so the next one starts smarter.*
  (`BRAIN_TAGLINE`, `lib/brain.ts`). It said *your personal Jarvis* until
  the owner found that might not be quickly understood (2026-10-15).
- **Reached from** the dashboard's screen row, *Second Brain* with its mark
  beside *Memory* and an accent *new* mark, and from the palette (*Second
  Brain*; *Add to Second Brain…*).
- **Dropping an answer in.** *Drop into Second Brain* sits in the header of every
  answer on Memory and in a session's Answers tab, and under an assistant
  reply in the chat view (shown on hover or focus, so a chat reads as a
  chat). It sends the event id only; it turns into *In Second Brain*, or
  *Could not drop* with the reason on hover. A paired device sees no button.
- **The quick capture** (⇧⌘H; ⌘K *Add to Second Brain…*; the screen's *Add
  to Second Brain*) is a sheet: *What to keep* (the first line is the title), the
  kind (Idea, Decision, Finding, Question), topics comma-separated, *Drop it
  in* on ⌘↩. Its foot says *Kept on this machine*. Mounted once per page
  (`BrainCaptureHost`, in `App` and `AppShell`) and opened by the
  `caprock:brain-capture` window event, so anything can open it.
- **The screen** opens on its title (the mark, *Second Brain*) and the
  faint line under it, then one of three states (`data-brain-phase`; the
  owner, 2026-10-15, translated: "totally unclear what to do. It should be
  just one click and off we go … it must feel ACTIVE … minimal settings"):
  - **Not built** (off, nothing kept; `screens/brain/BrainStart.tsx`): one
    picture and one button. `BrainHero` large, the *Free* pill, *Give your
    agents a memory*, the line naming the agents drawn, and *Start
    building*, the screen's only filled button. Under it one line says what
    happens — *Your own Claude reads your last 30 days of sessions on this
    computer · up to 300k tokens a day · nothing goes to Caprock* (the cap
    as the status says it) — and *Details*, folded, holds the rest in three
    short lines in the text colour: your own `claude` (the model) reads each
    session's prompts and answers; they go to Anthropic under your login,
    as the session did; the daily limit and that Settings turns it off.
    That line and Details are the disclosure (rule 4), both on screen
    before anything runs. The click is the consent, the turn-on and the
    past month at once: `PUT /v1/brain/settings {auto: true}`, then
    `POST /v1/brain/backfill {days: 30}` (`startBuilding`, `lib/brain.ts`),
    and the screen moves to *Building*. No search, filters or view switch
    yet, and no instructions for an empty list; *Add a note* is a quiet
    link under it (⇧⌘H in its tooltip in the app). Without `claude` there is
    no button: *Claude Code was not found on this computer. Install it,
    then:* *Check again*. A paired device reads *Start it on the computer
    Caprock runs on.*
  - **Building** and **With memories** share one layout, value first (the
    owner, 2026-10-16, translated: "Second Brain inside looks like crap —
    much better already but it's unclear what to do with it. I'd put the
    ball just as an element, not the centre — it's just a visualisation.
    Want to open it — it opens in a modal, rotate and explore there"). The
    three step blocks, the six figure tiles, the *Graph* | *List* switch
    and the living brain beside the strip are gone from the page.
  - **The status line** (`screens/brain/BrainStrip.tsx`), on top: one line,
    every figure a count from `GET /v1/brain/status`, none estimated. Up to
    date it says what it holds — *Up to date · 278 memories · 137 new today ·
    91 topics · $0.15 in 24 h* (when it last built in the word's tooltip; the
    cost's tooltip says it is what Claude Code reported at API list price,
    which on a subscription comes out of the plan's limits). Otherwise it
    says what it is doing (`brainState`, `lib/brain.ts`, from `auto.stage`)
    then how many it holds: *Building · reading session 13 of 41 · 29% · 8
    memories* over a 6px bar (sessions read of all in reach), *Building ·
    linking what session 13 of 41 gave*, *Building · 12 of 41 read · next in
    3s*, *Paused · you paused it*, *Off · not started*, *Stopped · Claude Code
    was not found*, *Waiting · today's 300k tokens are used*. A breathing
    dot while it builds. The line wraps between figures, never after the
    word. On its right a small *Details* (folded) and *Pause* / *Resume*
    (turns it off; *Resume* turns it on again and carries on, the
    backfill's reach kept), the latter only on the machine.
    - **Details**, unfolded under the line: *Reading now* — the session's
      title, agent, project, passages sent and seconds so far, while a read
      runs; the three steps of a read in one row (*Find finished sessions*,
      *Claude extracts memories*, *Link into the graph*), the current one
      bold, the done ones ticked; *Sessions* (*12 of 41 read · last 30
      days*), *Links*, *Tokens, 24 h* (of the daily limit), *Cost, 24 h*
      (*API list price*) and *Last built*; while it builds, **Where it comes
      from** (each project, *9 of 29 read · 6 memories*, a hairline bar) and
      **Just read** (the last three reads, title, project, memories kept or
      *failed, will retry* / *too short*, age).
    - **A problem** is said under the line, plainly, with the one action
      that fixes it: no `claude` → *Check again*; today's limit used →
      *Raise to 1M a day* (below 1M); a failed read while it builds → that
      it is tried again on its own.
    The status is read again on every `brain` frame (a read beginning or
    ending sends one, op `progress`) and every 4 seconds while it builds,
    only while the page is shown.
  - **The middle** (`data-brain-body`, a column beside a 300px side column
    at 1024px and wider; one column under it, the side column under the
    list). Before the first memory it is a dashed box, *Memories land here
    as they are found.* After it:
    - **What your next session gets** (`screens/brain/BrainNext.tsx`): *A
      new session in* a repository picker (the projects with memories; it
      opens on the project of the newest memory, else the one with the
      most), then the memories a new session there is handed with its
      handoff, numbered in the order they go — read from `GET
      /v1/brain/handoff`, the pick the SessionStart handoff itself makes
      (pinned first, then newest, at most five in 700 characters;
      03-contracts.md § Second Brain), each with its kind, title, two lines
      of summary and *Pinned* or its age; a click opens it. One line says
      whether a session opening there now would get them, from the same
      read: with the passage from *1h ago*; *No session in X left a passage
      to hand over in the last 14 days … so nothing goes until one does*;
